Between the two charts the frame is whole — Birth in her Day and again in her Year, Peak in his Day, the vault in his Year — and its element is Water: his Auspicious God by the first book, her husband star by relation, her Useful God by the handbook, and the mediating god of her chart by the old note on Fire. The two rooms share a direction, and it is the one direction both charts wanted.

Put the frame to the tests of the last section, for each palace in turn.

His Rat is struck by his Horse. The old note names four things that gather in such a motion: a combining god with strength, a gathering god, a vault god, a Noble. The frame brings two of them. Her Monkeys are gathering gods of the Rat, and his own Dragon, which stood too far from his seat to help it alone, is at once the third gathering god and the vault of the Rat's element; the frame his Rat makes with her Monkeys draws the Dragon in. Ren Tieqiao's test asks what the combination takes hold of and what it leans toward. It takes hold of the Rat itself, the favorable Branch; and it leans toward Water, which is the Rat's own element. By the refrain of the old note that is the combination that helps a favorable god, and by Ren's own instance of the Pig and the Tiger it is the second of the two right ways to quiet a clash on a favorable Branch: joining it to partners that turn toward what it already is. His war is on earth only — Yang Wood and Yang Water stand at peace above the Horse and the Rat — so Ren's limit does not touch it. And the strength of the gathering god? Her Monkey is Imprisoned in her season and clashed by her Tiger, and it is not a strong Branch alone. There are two of it, and one is her Day Branch, the room itself.

Her Monkey is struck by her Tiger. The same four things would quiet it: the Snake, which combines with it; the Rat and the Dragon, which gather with it; the vault of its frame's element, which is the Dragon again; a Noble. He brings the Rat and the Dragon, one on his seat and one at the head of his chart. Ren's test must be run twice here, because her chart has two reckonings of what it needs. By the strength method the favorable Branch in her clash is the Tiger, her root, and the Monkey is the attacker that cuts it with Metal; a combination that takes hold of the attacker and turns it away from the element it strikes with is the first of the two right ways, and the frame does exactly that, turning the Monkey from Metal toward Water, which does not cut Wood but feeds it. By the handbook the favorable god in her clash is the Water inside the Monkey, and the frame turns the Monkey toward Water, which is the second right way. On either reckoning the frame is fitting. Her war is on earth only — Yang Fire over the Monkey, Yang Metal over the Tiger, and 丙 and 庚 do not clash — so Ren's limit does not touch her either.

So the same three Branches are the medicine the old note prescribes for his room and for hers, and the frame that holds them is built of both of them: her palace at its source, his palace at its height, his origins as its store. This book knows of no classical author who wrote that sentence about two people, and it has been careful to show every step by which the license of Chapter Eight arrives at it. It is the central finding of the reading, and it is, to this book's knowledge, a rare one.

Three cautions keep it from being a triumph.

The first is the Day-Blade chapter. His seat is one of its three pillars, and the chapter wants no 三会六合 upon it, naming the three-harmony beside the six; and the old manual's worked example of a Blade undone, the general's chart, is a frame raised on the Blade and then a clash. One frame and one clash together is what his Rat would have, between her Monkeys and his own Horse. This book reads that chapter as written of the Blade of a strong self, on the compendium's own principle that each ruling has its application, and his is the Blade of a weak one, which the first book and Shen Xiaozhan read as the root that saves him. But the reading is this book's, the compendium's one qualifier on the matter says that a thin chart finds the warning true and a robust one escapes it, and a reader who prefers the chapter that names the pillar to the note that does not is on good ground.

The second is the first book's own precedent, which both helps and limits. The first book watched a decade complete this frame for him — the decade of 丙申, Yang Fire on the Monkey, from his twelfth year to his twenty-second — and said that the season was, for once, overruled. That decade is her Day Pillar to the character, and Chapter Ten will set the first book's sentences about it beside her. A person is not a decade. A Luck Pillar stands inside a chart's own time and a partner stands beside it, and this book will not claim for her Monkeys what ten years of the Monkey did. The license allows the smaller statement: that the frame each of them holds in pieces is whole between them for as long as they are together, and that it is made of the one element both charts want.

The third is the one old rule, and it will be given its due below.

Then the Stems that cross, and there are more of them in this pair than in most.

At the Months, 甲 and 己 combine: his Eating God with her Hurting Officer, the two gods of Output, what he makes and what she makes reaching for each other. The first book has read a 己 combining this 甲 once already, in the decade he is now living through, and found two consequences: the Provider is partly combined away, which reduces the Output drain a weak Day Master cannot afford; and the public creative identity is dimmed in the same act. Her Month Stem is the same character as his present Luck Stem, and does the same two things. From her side the bond ties up the god Chapter Seven found most dangerous to her husband star: the Hurting Officer that would wound a Killings is held by the man's own Output. The pair leans toward Earth, which is his Authority and her excess, and in neither chart does it turn: his Wood opposes Earth, her season kills it, and the Yang Wood is under a clash. It bonds. And it is a bond under attack, because the Stem beside her Month Stem at the Year is Yang Metal, her Hour repeats it, and 甲 and 庚 are a clashing pair.

That is the second crossing. Her Year Stem and her Hour Stem both strike his Month Stem: her Windfall against his Eating God, Metal felling Wood, twice. The first book named this blow before this book was thought of. Metal is his Resource, and the Indirect Resource in particular has the classical name for what it does to the Eating God — the Owl seizing the Food; when Metal arrives, the first book said, it will suppress the very creative flow that is this person's public identity, and the Metal years will replenish the river by narrowing the channel through which it pours itself out. Her two Yang Metals are that Metal, declared, feeding his Day Master directly — Resource on the surface for the first time in his life — and cutting his Output as they do it. It is the price of the medicine, stated in advance, and the reader should notice that the pair's two gods of Output are at once bonded by her 己 and struck by her 庚. His Eating God is reached for and cut by the same woman's Stems.

At the Years and the Day, the same character. His Year Stem is 丙, and so is her Day Master. The sun at his horizon, the Windfall the first book read as Wealth at the widest horizon, has her face; and to his chart she is one more of it. The reference's rule for a Stem held in common is that each reads it as a different god, and here the difference is as wide as it can be: to him it is his Wealth, to her it is herself.

And at his Hour, 癸. To her it is the Direct Officer, the husband star proper, which her chart does not contain and his contains three times — on his Hour Stem and inside his Rat and his Dragon. The Magistrate she was born without stands in his private world and in his seat.

Then the Branches beyond the center. His Rat and her Rabbit: the punishment of the pair, Rat and Rabbit, the care that does not know its limits, between his seat and her Month Command. He has that punishment inside his own chart, between his seat and his Hour, and her Month Branch is the same character as his Hour Branch: her season doubles the relation his private world already has with his room. His Dragon and her Rabbit: the harm of Rabbit and Dragon, between his origins and her season, the two domains corroding each other's alliances. His Horse and her Tiger: a half of the Fire frame, with the Peak among them, between his working life and her private world. The two Branches that strike the two palaces are allies, and what they lean toward is Fire — his excess and her support. And her Tiger, her Rabbit, his Rabbit and his Dragon make the three Branches of the East complete: Wood, which to him is Output and to her is Resource and the mediating element of the old note on Fire and Water.

Now the virtue-combination and its opposite, since the pillars have been named. The Day Pillar that would join hers in the virtue-combination is 辛巳, and his in the virtue-combination is 丁丑; neither is the other. The pillar that stands at war with her Day Pillar, Stem against Stem and Branch against Branch, is 壬寅. The pillar at war with his is 丙午. Each of them is built of the other's Day Master over the Branch that strikes one's own seat: his Day Stem over her Tiger, her Day Stem over his Horse. The calendar brought the first of those pillars in 2022 and brings the second in the year of this writing, and Chapter Ten will say what that is worth.

So much is given. The costs are six, and a reading that left them out would be the marketplace's method turned upside down. The reader will notice that most of them fall on him.

The clash of the Day Masters has been read: a difference of declared will, two people who say opposite things and want the lead in the same moment, bracing or exhausting according to what lies underneath. The verse says it can be borne, and the palaces say on what ground.

His seat is punished by her Month Branch. The reference's phrase — nourishment that becomes control — is now a description of how her season and her working life are liable to meet his home ground, and it is the same relation his own Hour already has with his seat, doubled.

His Year Branch is harmed by her Month Branch: his origins and public horizon against her season. It is the one contact of this pair the marketplace's second instrument would have found, had it looked at a Month.

His Month Stem is struck by her Year Stem and her Hour Stem. This is the price of the medicine, and it is not small for a man whose public identity the first book found in that Stem. What he makes, she cuts, from the side of her family and from the side of her children, while she feeds him.

His Month Branch and her Hour Branch lean together toward Fire. Her private world warms his season, which did not need warming.

And the Wood of the East is complete between them: more of the element that drains him, and the element that feeds her.

Against these, the gifts, and the reader will notice that they are more evenly shared. The frame, which quiets both rooms and is the element both want. Four Metals, two of them in the open, feeding a Day Master that has never been fed from the surface. His two Fires, which are her Companions, and his three Woods, which are her Resource. The combination of the Month Stems, which binds the god most dangerous to her husband star and relieves the drain the first book counted in his. His Hour Stem and his seat, which hold her Direct Officer. And one note from the lore: her Month Branch, the Rabbit, is one of the two Nobles of a Yang Water Day Master, and the handbooks call a partner whose Branch is one's Noble a benefactor.

And the old rule? A man born on a Rat day, it says, is disposed to a wife born in a year of the Rat, Horse, Rabbit or Rooster, and the wife it warns him against is one born in a year of Monkey, Rat or Dragon: hers is a Monkey year. Read from her chart it is no kinder. A woman born on a Monkey day is disposed, by its classes, to a husband born in a year of Tiger, Monkey, Snake or Pig, and his is a Dragon year; and the Branches it forbids her are those in three-harmony with her Day Branch, and his Rat and his Dragon are exactly those. The one classical statute that compares a husband's pillar with a wife's condemns this pair from both sides, and it condemns it for the very frame this section has called the central finding. The marketplace's zodiac, meanwhile, blessed the same frame at the door. The oldest rule the tradition has and the commonest superstition it has disagree about these two, and this book follows neither as a rule. It has given its reasons for following 滴天髓 instead: the old rule states no ground, was kept by no later author, and condemns in the lump what the later doctrine teaches a reader to weigh. But the rule exists, and the reader is entitled to hold it against the reading.

### A counter-example

Now the third chart, which the opening note promised once. A woman born in Tampere on 15 May 1978, a little after noon by the sun:

| | Year | Month | Day | Hour |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Stem | 戊 Yang Earth | 丁 Yin Fire | 丁 Yin Fire | 丙 Yang Fire |
| Branch | 午 Horse | 巳 Snake | 丑 Ox | 午 Horse |

Compare her with the same man by contact, as a handbook would. Her Day Master is Yin Fire; his is Yang Water. 丁 and 壬 combine: the two Day Masters are one of the five marriages in person, and she is his Direct Wealth as he is her Direct Officer — the star in its proper form, where the woman of this book is his Windfall and clashes him. Her Day Branch is the Ox, and Rat and Ox are the first of the six harmonies. Stem to Stem and Branch to Branch: 壬子 and 丁丑 are a virtue-combination, heaven and earth joined, the highest mark the comparison of Day Pillars can give. On contact she is the better match, and it is not close.

Now do what the last chapter taught. Her census: Fire six, Earth five, Metal two and Water one, all three of those hidden, Wood none. A Yin Fire in command of its season with a chart that is nearly all Fire and the Earth Fire makes. Translate it into his gods. Fire is his Wealth: six. Earth is his Authority: five. Eleven of her fourteen are the two things the first book found him overloaded with, and the thing he needs is two buried Stems. By Yuan Shushan's scale she is the last grade for him — the excess itself, with what the excess feeds — and the woman of this book, who is his last grade in her Day Master, is his first in four Metals and two Waters. Both women are Fire. The difference is what each has made of it.

Read it the other way and it is a different story. To a blazing Fire with no Water but one hidden drop, he is four Waters: her Authority, her husband star, and by the reading of strength this canon uses, the Useful God of her chart. He is very nearly everything she lacks.

So the exchange runs one way. He is medicine to her; she is more of his disease. And the contacts, put to the same tests the first pair was put to, say the same.

The tether first. There are two Yin Fires on her Stems, at the Month and at the Day, and both combine with his single Yang Water: a contested combination, with him as the one contested. His Day Master, tied to Fire by its own partner twice over, is the tethered god of the verse — 日主有合，不顾用神. He would be held, and happy to be held, by the element his chart can least afford, with skirts and hairpins for his Useful God. From her side the same combination passes the test. What her Day Master is tied to is Water, and Water is what her chart is dying for. The woman of this book, whose Day Master clashes his instead, tethers nothing.

Then the palace. Her Ox combines with his Rat, and by the old note's own instance a Rat struck by a Horse is stilled by an Ox. But Ren's test asks what the Ox takes hold of and where it leans, and the answers are the Rat itself and Earth, which dams him: the quiet of a tether, not of a frame. And her chart brings two more Horses, at her Year and at her Hour, and each of them strikes his seat as his own Horse does. One combining god with strength can put two clashes to rest, the old note said. It did not say three. And one of the three comes with Yang Fire above it: her Hour Pillar, 丙午, against his Day Pillar, 壬子, is heaven and earth at war, the one configuration in which, by Ren Tieqiao's ruling, no combining god and no gathering god puts the motion to rest. It is also, the reader will recall, the pillar the last section found at war with his Day Pillar: here it is a person's Hour. Her own Ox fares no better: it is harmed from both sides by her own Horses, and when his Rat arrives as its ally, those are the Horses that strike the ally.

Then the frame. There is none. No three-harmony is completed between these two charts, and so there is no gathering god either: nothing in her chart joins his Rat to partners turning toward Water. What the two hold in common is the Horse, his one and her two, and the Horse repeated is one of the self-punishments.

Nothing in this makes her a worse person to love, or him a worse one for her. The pull would be stronger than with the woman of this book: a combination between Day Masters is felt, and a clash between them is argued. But one of these pairings is two people whose rooms are quieted by one frame of the element both want, with a woman who carries what the man lacks, and a clash of wills above it. The other is a strong attraction that feeds her and consumes him, with the sweetest contact the handbooks know at its center. The marketplace would have blessed the first for a three-harmony and the handbooks would have preferred the second for a combination, and both would have been looking at the wrong thing. The tradition's best authors had the instrument that tells them apart. It is the one Chen Su'an named: only the ordinary principles of the Four Pillars.

---

## Chapter Ten — The Years

The natal chart is a photograph, the first book said, and a photograph does not walk through decades. Nothing in the last nine chapters has yet moved. The palace is where a marriage lives and the star is what it is; the second chart says what two people are to each other; and none of that says when anything happens. For that there are the Luck Pillars and the years, and this is the part of the reading people most want and the tradition was most careful about.

Its care shows in the sentence that governs the subject. 滴天髓:

> 休咎系乎运，尤系乎岁，冲战视其孰降，和好视其孰切。
>
> Fortune and misfortune hang upon the luck, and still more upon the year. Where they clash or make war, see which gives way; where they accord or agree, see which is the more to the purpose.

The decade is the country one is passing through and the year is the person one meets there; Chapter Eight quoted the old note. The country decides what can grow. The year decides what happens to be planted. The note draws a working rule from its own figure: a country is known by its ground, so in a decade the Branch weighs more, and a person is known by the face, so in a year the Stem weighs more, though neither is ever without the other.

The second half of the verse, as the note explains it, concerns the dealings of the decade and the year with each other. Where the Stem of one controls the Stem of the other, that is war; where their Branches clash, that is clash; and the question in both is which of the two the Day Master needs, and whether the other can be brought to give way. Where they combine, the question is whether what they combine toward is to the purpose. This book puts the same two questions, as every reader does, to an arriving pillar and the chart itself: what it fights must be weighed against it, and what it joins must be compared with whatever that thing was already joined to. That extension is practice. The verse does not make it.

One principle stands over everything that follows, and Chen Su'an put it in a line: 若运途之宜与不宜，即原局之喜与不喜 — what suits or does not suit along the road of the luck is simply what the natal chart welcomes or does not welcome. Time adds nothing of its own. It brings Stems and Branches, and what they do depends on what they find. A year does not make the ground a marriage stands on, and does not unmake it. It can bring, for a season, the thing a chart was born without; Yuan Shushan's sentence in Chapter Eight was that a chart so supplied is to be spoken of, while the supply lasts, as having attained its balance. What time brings it also takes away.

### When a marriage comes

The old books give three signals plainly and are divided about a fourth. The modern handbooks have kept two of the three, turned the third upside down, and settled the fourth by keeping its cheerful half.

The star arrives. For a man the rule was given in Chapter Six: 欲识男儿早娶，定是运合财乡 — early marriage belongs to the man whose luck meets the country of Wealth. A decade or a year that carries the spouse star on its Stem brings the relation itself into the open, and most of all where the natal star was hidden, weak or absent. For a woman the rhapsody states the converse: 要知女命难婚，运入背夫之位 — marriage comes hard while her luck runs through the place that turns its back on the husband; and what is true of that place is true, turned round, of the places that face him. Where the husband is taken by function, as Chapter Seven taught, it is the arrival of that god which counts.

The star's frame is completed. 大运流年，三合财乡，必主红鸾吉兆: where the Luck Pillar and the year together make the three-harmony of the Wealth country, it is surely the glad omen of the Red Phoenix. A frame assembled from the chart, the decade and the year floods the chart with the star's element as no single Stem can.

The palace is clashed. This is the signal the handbooks have turned upside down, and the reader should have the Song text itself, which 五行精纪 quotes from an older manual:

> 凡人妻位冲辰，为娶妻之年，罕有与日三六合而见妻者，更以巧拙言之。
>
> For people in general, the Branch that clashes the wife's seat marks the year of taking a wife; it is rare to get a wife in a year that stands in three-harmony or six-harmony with the day. Beyond that, one speaks according to the aptness or awkwardness of the case.

The reasoning is one this book has met in every chapter. A clash moves what it strikes. A seat that nothing moves stays as it is, and the year that changes a household is the year the seat is struck. The same clash falling on a marriage already made is a test of it, and the next section returns to that. The rule speaks of the wife's seat in a man's chart. This book applies it to a woman's Day Branch as well, by parity; no text does.

The year joins the Day Pillar. Here the old books do not agree with one another, and the handbooks have kept only the cheerful half of one of them. The Song collection has a chapter on what it calls 晦气 (huì qì), gloom. The year is the sovereign of its gods and the pillars of a birth are its subjects, the chapter says, and when the Stem of the birth day or the birth hour combines with the Stem of the year, a subject has presumed: 必主阴灾, hidden trouble is sure, and chiefly for the women of the house. Then it adds a line that has no parallel anywhere else:

> 若在地支六神中相合，为之鸳鸯合，主有好事，若有用，主添进人口。
>
> Where the combining lies among the six gods of the Branches, it is called the mandarin-duck combination, and promises good things; where it is of use, the household gains a member.

The line is obscure. It seems to mean that where the Branches of the two pillars stand in six-harmony — together with the Stems, or in place of them, it does not say — the gloom becomes its opposite; and it goes on, 则有别离之事, that where the two are at odds there will be a parting. The other two authorities know no such exception. The old manual: 日干支合太岁干支曰晦 — when the Stem and Branch of the Day combine with the Stem and Branch of the year, that is the gloom, and its year is one of reversals and of haste that does not arrive. The compendium: the Stem of the Day and the Stem of the Hour ought not to combine with the Stem of the year, and the trouble is heavier where the birth Stem is the yang one of the pair and lighter where it is the yin. So of three old texts, two hold that a year which combines with the Day Master is a clouded year whatever its Branch does, and one obscure line makes of it good news.

Two signals that are the staple of every modern reading therefore stand on thin ground. One is the Day Master combined: the year that brings a Day Master its combining partner draws the self into a bond, whatever god that partner happens to be, and for a yang man and a yin woman the partner is the direct spouse star, so that this signal and the first are one. By all three of the old texts that year is the gloom, unless the Branches agree; and by two of them, even then. The other is the palace combined: a Branch in six-harmony with the Day Branch, or completing a frame that includes it, joins the room to something outside it. The Song rule says one seldom takes a wife in such a year, and the only line that blesses it is the line about the mandarin ducks. This book keeps both as colors, because the relations they rest on are real, and gives the old signals the greater weight.

And there are the stars of the lore — the Red Phoenix and Heavenly Joy, counted from the year of birth, and the Peach Blossom. Part Two gives their tables and the critics' opinion of them. 滴天髓's advice about the Peach Blossom applies to the family: believe the half.

No one of these makes an event. The tradition's practice, which the old note on the country and the person implies, is to look for agreement. The Luck Pillar must make the season possible: a decade that brings the star, or what feeds it, or at least does not bury it. Within that decade a year must speak, and the more of the signals it sounds at once, the louder. And the life must be at an age and in a state where the signal can mean a wedding. The rhapsodies were written for a world that married at eighteen. The same configuration at eight is a year in the family's fortunes, and at eighty it is a year of companionship or of memory.

### When a marriage is tested

The signals of strain are the mirror of these.

The palace is struck. A clash, a punishment or a harm from the decade or the year falls on the room, with the meanings Chapter Three gave each; the Song rule that makes the clash year a marrying year for the unmarried makes it, for the married, the year the household is moved. Two cases are graver than the rest and the first book named both. When the arriving pillar clashes the Day Pillar in Stem and Branch together, it is the turning moan: the self and its ground overturned at once. When it repeats the Day Pillar, or repeats the Day Branch, it is the crouching moan: the palace laid on top of itself, which the tradition reads as heaviness and stalling. The old proverb preserved in the Song collection has them together: 伏吟反吟，悲哭淋淋 — the crouching moan and the turning moan, and the tears in streams. It is a proverb. What it remembers is that these are the years in which the marriage ground is least like itself.

The star is attacked. For a man, years and decades of Companions fall on a thin Wealth, and for a woman, years of the Hurting Officer fall on an Officer — in each case only where the natal chart had the weakness and lacked the bridge. Where the husband is taken by function, the attack comes from whatever controls that god.

The star is buried or drawn off. The decade that carries the spouse star to its Tomb or its Extinction is the rhapsody's parting of the paired birds; Chapter Seven asked that it be read as a season in which the star is out of reach. A Stem that arrives and combines with the spouse star — with the star, not with the Day Master — takes it out of play for as long as it stays.

The star is doubled. A woman whose chart holds an Officer meets a decade of the Killings, or a man steady in one Wealth meets a decade of the other: the mixing that the natal chart was spared arrives by time.

And one case is special to the calendar. The manuals speak of 岁运并临, year and luck arriving together. The old lines mean by it a decade and a year that bring the same thing at once, and they have the Blade chiefly in view: 劫财阳刃，切忌时逢；岁运并临，灾殃立至 — Rob Wealth and the Blade are most to be feared at the Hour, and when year and luck arrive upon them together, calamity is at the door. The compendium knows a narrower sense as well, the year whose pillar is the very pillar of the decade, and judges it in a line: 独羊刃、七煞为凶，财、官、印绶亦吉 — only with the Blade and the Seven Killings is it ill; with Wealth, Officer and Resource it is good as well. In either sense whatever arrives arrives twice over, and the old manual's own judgment is conditional, as it should be: 若损用神皆有祸. If it injures the Useful God, there is harm.

A chart with a Blade has a calendar of its own, and since one of this book's charts is seated on one, it must be given. 阳刃冲合岁君，勃然祸至: when the Blade clashes or combines with the lord of the year, calamity comes of a sudden. The compendium says which years are meant — the year whose Branch clashes the Blade, and the years whose Branches stand in three-harmony or six-harmony with it — and qualifies at once. A chart of shallow resources will find the saying true. A chart that is robust, or that has the stars of virtue to relieve it, comes to no great harm. And where a decade and a year both touch the Blade the trouble is whole, and where only one of them does it is halved. This is the Day-Blade chapter of Chapter Nine laid out along the calendar, and it is to be weighed as that chapter was weighed.

### More than once

The old books took it for granted that a man might marry more than once and held that a woman should not, and Chapter Seven has dealt with the second assumption. People now bring the tradition questions it was not written to answer in their present form: whether a marriage will end, whether there will be another, what the second will be like. The instruments are the same ones. It is as well to say plainly what they can do.

The signs the manuals read as more than one marriage have nearly all been met already. For a man they are Blades and Companions in number — 男多阳刃，必重婚; 比肩成局，几度新郎 — the wife star twice declared with direct and indirect mixed, the palace clashed, the star contested between two that combine with it. For a woman they are Officer and Killings mingled, the Hurting Officer in layers — 女犯伤官，须再嫁 — the husband star combined away, a chart with no husband star in it, the palace clashed. None of these is a count. What they have in common is a marriage ground that does not keep its first arrangement easily. In a world without divorce the manuals had two ways of saying so: that a spouse would die, or that the house would hold more than one. In a world with divorce the same signs are far more often a parting and a second beginning, and the reader has to make that translation every time one of the old words appears. The compendium half knew it. Its escape clause, quoted in Chapter Six, puts a wife who has been married before outside the rule: what the chart measures can be spent on a first marriage and need not be paid a second time.

Three things follow for anyone who reads a chart at a second marriage.

The room is the same. Whoever a person marries, first or third, comes to live in the same Day Branch, with the same tenants inside it and the same neighbours striking it or holding it. Whatever was hard about that room is hard again, and whatever it gave it gives again. This is the most useful thing a chart has to say to someone who is beginning again. The palace was never a description of the first husband or the first wife. It is a description of the place the person offers.

The guest is different, and so is the time. A second partner is a second chart, and everything in the last two chapters is asked again from the beginning; the first answer has no bearing on the second. And a second marriage is made in another decade, under another Luck Pillar, with the star in another condition. A star that was in its tomb at twenty-five may be standing in the open at forty-five. Some of what looks, in a life, like having chosen better the second time is the decade having turned.

And the years are found as before. The years in which a marriage is likeliest to end are the testing years of the last section, falling in a decade that agrees with them, on a chart that had the weakness. The years in which another is likeliest to begin are found exactly as the years of a first.

Two devices of modern practice should be named, because the reader will meet them. One takes the direct star for the first spouse and the indirect star for a later one. The other moves the palace of a second marriage from the Day Branch to some other Branch. Neither is in the texts this canon follows. The old definition made the direct star the principal wife and the indirect the concubine, which is a statement of rank within one household and not of order in time. This book uses neither device.

One limit is absolute. The manuals speak without embarrassment of the man who overcomes his wife and the woman who overcomes her husband, and Chapters Six and Seven read those phrases as descriptions of a marriage ground. They are not forecasts of another person's death, and no reading of a chart entitles anyone to tell a man or a woman that they are a danger to the person they marry. The first book said of a hard year that it is not a prophecy but weather. That holds twice over where the weather in question is someone else's life.

### Two clocks

Everything so far applies to one chart. A couple has two, and time treats them differently in two ways.

The decades are separate. Each person has a sequence of Luck Pillars fixed at birth, running forward or backward from a different month and turning at a different age. At any moment the two of them are in two different countries. One may be in the best decade of a life while the other is in the worst; one may be receiving the spouse star while the other's is entering its tomb. No comparison of natal charts shows this, and it may be the most useful thing the method has to tell a couple who have been together a long time: not that the two were ill-matched, but that for ten years they have been living in different weather.

The years are shared. The annual pillar is the same for everyone alive. But it is a different god to each Day Master, and its Branch does a different thing to each palace. So every year sorts itself, for a given couple, into one of three kinds. There are years that speak in both charts, years that speak in one, and years that speak in neither. The first kind matters most. A wedding is an event in two charts. A year in which his star arrives and her palace is moved is a year in which something can happen between them; a year in which his palace is struck and hers is quiet is a year he has and she watches.

This book's working rule for reading a year for two, then — and here again the method is the tradition's and its application to a pair is an extension — is to ask six things. What is the year's Stem to each Day Master? What does its Branch do to each palace? Does it bring, attack or bury each one's star? What decade is each of them in, and does the year agree with it? Does the year speak in both charts, and if so, does it say the same thing in both? And last: what do the natal contacts between the two charts do with it — for a clash on one palace that the other palace holds is a different event from the same clash falling on a seat that stands alone.

One structural fact is worth carrying into any such reading. The six harmonies come in opposed couples. Rat and Ox harmonize; the Branches that clash them are the Horse and the Goat, and Horse and Goat harmonize. Tiger and Pig harmonize; their opponents are the Monkey and the Snake, which harmonize. Rabbit and Dog, Rooster and Dragon. Two people whose palaces are joined in a harmony therefore have their testing years joined too. The year that strikes his room and the year that strikes hers are a pair, and so are the year that repeats his room while it combines with hers and the year that does the reverse. Their marriage has a rhythm built into the calendar, and it can be written down in advance.

### The charts

Begin with the two sequences, side by side.

| His decade | from | Its Stem is to him | Her decade | from | Its Stem is to her |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 乙未 | early 1979 | Hurting Officer | 戊寅 | May 1986 | Eating God |
| 丙申 | early 1989 | Windfall | 丁丑 | May 1996 | Rob Wealth |
| 丁酉 | early 1999 | Direct Wealth | 丙子 | May 2006 | Companion |
| 戊戌 | early 2009 | Seven Killings | 乙亥 | May 2016 | Direct Resource |
| 己亥 | early 2019 | Direct Officer | 甲戌 | May 2026 | Indirect Resource |
| 庚子 | early 2029 | Indirect Resource | 癸酉 | May 2036 | Direct Officer |
| 辛丑 | early 2039 | Direct Resource | 壬申 | May 2046 | Seven Killings |

The first thing to see is that the two clocks run in opposite directions. He is a man born in a yang year and his pillars run forward from his Month Pillar; she is a woman born in a yang year and hers run backward from hers. His Stems climb the cycle, 乙 丙 丁 戊 己 庚 辛, and hers descend it, 戊 丁 丙 乙 甲 癸 壬, so that the two sequences pass through each other once and then draw apart. So far one Stem has stood over both lives, and only one: Yin Fire, from early 1999 to May 2006, his Direct Wealth and her Rob Wealth. One Branch has stood under both, and only one: the Pig, from early 2019 to May 2026, his Companion and her Seven Killings, the Branch in which Yang Water has its Office. His wife star and her rival; his equal and her husband. The one Stem they have shared is his marriage and her competition, and the one ground is her marriage and his company. A second shared Stem comes in 2049, and it is his own; the walk ends there.

The second is that each sequence visits the other chart, once squarely and twice at an angle. His second decade, 丙申, from twelve to twenty-two, is her Day Pillar to the character. Her second decade, 丁丑, from sixteen to twenty-six, is no pillar of his, but it is the pillar Chapter Nine found joined to his Day Pillar in the virtue-combination, 壬子 and 丁丑, Stem to Stem and Branch to Branch. And her seventh, 壬申, from sixty-six, is built of his Day Master over her own seat, a pillar of neither chart and made of both. The first book read the 丙申 decade before this book was thought of, and its sentences can be read now as a description of her:

> The decade splits. Yang Fire in the Stem repeats the natal Year Stem's Windfall — Wealth pressure, the demand to manage speculative forces the weak Water can barely afford to govern — and colors the first half. But the Monkey is a Metal Branch: Yang Metal as principal, the Indirect Resource, the Owl Spirit — the unconventional mentor, the self-taught mastery — with Yang Water as secondary, a Companion, and Yang Earth as residual. For the first time, Metal enters the chart, through a Branch, in the second half of the decade: more felt than seen, arriving as an interior discovery rather than an institution.

It goes on to the frame: the Monkey is the third member, the Water frame is completed, the chart is flooded with Water, and the Monkey is the Branch of Yang Water's Birth, the ocean's first spring emerging from Metal's mountain. And it ends:

> The Fire in the Stem keeps it from being easy. Beneath the surface something is being fed.

Every clause of that is a sentence of Chapters Eight and Nine: the Fire above that presses and clashes, the Metal beneath that supplies, the frame completed, the Birth of his Water on her Branch, the ease withheld and the feeding that goes on underneath. What time brought him from twelve to twenty-two, by a decade, she brings by presence, and the difference is the one Chapter Eight marked: a decade turns over after ten years and a person does not. There is no marvel in the coincidence. There are sixty pillars, and in two lives of ten decades each some will fall where a chart already stands. What is worth the reader's attention is not that her pillar appears in his sequence but what the first book, reading it blind, said it would do.

The third is the count Chapter Eight promised, and for these two it comes out crossed. His spouse star is Fire and hers is Water, and each is the other's Day Master. So the years that bring him the wife star, the years of Yang Fire and Yin Fire, are the years whose Stem is her own character or its sister; and the years that bring her the husband star by function, the years of Yang Water and Yin Water, are the years whose Stem is his. In every Yang Fire year the calendar does to him exactly what she does to him: the Windfall arrives and clashes his Day Master. In every Yang Water year the calendar does to her what he does to her: the Killings arrive and strike hers. His star years are her Companion years and her star years are his. No year brings the spouse star to both of them in the Stem, where the old note said a year is known; what a year can do is carry his star in the Stem and hers in the Branch, or the reverse, and 1996, 1997, 2002, 2003, 2007, 2013, 2016, 2022, 2036 and 2042 are such years. That is the ordinary case for a pair whose Day Masters clash, and it should be said plainly, because the handbooks' habit of looking for the year that is good for both will find little here. What the calendar gives this couple instead is a year that is one of them, in the Stem, standing in the other's chart.

The Branches have a rhythm of their own, and it is not the paired rhythm of the last section. Their palaces stand in a half of the Water frame, not in a six-harmony, so the years that strike their two rooms are not harmonizing partners but the Horse and the Tiger: his seat is clashed in the Horse years, 2002, 2014, 2026, 2038, and hers in the Tiger years, 1998, 2010, 2022, 2034, 2046. Tiger and Horse are two of the three members of the Fire frame, and its third member, the Dog, is her Tomb. Every Dog year, 2006, 2018, 2030, 2042, completes that frame across the two charts, from her Hour, his Month and the year, and floods both with Fire: his wife star's element and her own. The Water frame is touched in its turn. Every Monkey year, 2004, 2016, 2028, 2040, completes his frame inside his own chart and lays her palace on itself; every Dragon year, 2000, 2012, 2024, 2036, completes the frame across the two charts, from her Monkey, his Rat and the year; every Rat year, 1996, 2008, 2020, 2032, 2044, doubles his seat and makes a half of the frame with hers. Six years in every twelve assemble or strike one of the two frames, and in ten of the twelve something touches one of the two rooms; only the Rooster, their shared Peach Blossom, and the Dog, whose business is with the Fire frame and not with either palace, leave both rooms alone. This is a couple whose marriage ground is on the calendar nearly all the time.

Since one of the two charts is seated on its Blade, and this chapter promised to weigh that saying evenly, here is his Blade calendar entire. The Rat is his Blade. The years that clash it are the Horse years already named. The years in three-harmony with it are the Monkey and Dragon years already named, and the years in six-harmony with it are the Ox years, 1997, 2009, 2021, 2033, 2045. By the compendium's rule the trouble is whole where the decade and the year both clash or combine the Blade, and halved where only one does. Two of his decades do so: 丙申, whose Monkey gathers with it, and 辛丑, whose Ox combines with it. So the whole years are 1990, 1992 and 1997 in the first and 2040, 2045 and 2048 in the second, and every other year named is a half. The 庚子 decade is a different case. Its Rat neither clashes nor combines the Blade; it repeats it, and a decade or a year that brings the Blade again is what the other saying means by year and luck arriving together. So 2033, 2036 and 2038 are halves by the compendium's rule, with only the year striking or holding the Blade, and fall at the same time under the other saying, since the decade beneath them has brought the Blade itself; and the Rat years, 1996, 2032 and 2044, repeat it as years, 2032 inside the decade that repeats it too. Then the discounts, which the compendium gives in the same breath. A chart of shallow resources will find the saying true; a chart with depth of vigor, or the virtue stars to relieve it, 止有浮实，亦无大咎, meets only surface trouble and no great harm. His chart has little depth, but it has one of the two virtue stars. By the table of the months in 五行精纪, the Moon Virtue of a Horse month is Yang Fire, and Yang Fire stands at his Year; it is also, as it happens, the character of her Day Master. The Heavenly Virtue of that month is the Pig, which his present decade brought him. Hers, for the record, are the Monkey and Yang Wood: her palace is her Heavenly Virtue twice over, and her Moon Virtue arrives with her decade of 2026. And the first book called his chart saved by its Blade: 日干无气，时逢阳刃不为凶. And the compendium records one exception that happens to be written for this very pair of Day Masters: 壬丙逢刃见子午冲，多无祸 — where Yang Water or Yang Fire meets its Blade and sees the clash of Rat and Horse, for the most part there is no harm, 以丙见子、壬见午俱为正官 — because for Yang Fire the Rat, and for Yang Water the Horse, carry the Direct Officer, and the clash is reckoned a noble influence instead. His own chart is the case, Rat under Horse. Her chart has no Blade, so the calendar is his alone; what the Horse years bring her is the Branch that would be her Blade, which her birth does not hold, and which the book will treat as a Companion's ground. The reader can carry this paragraph through the walk and apply it where the years fall. The book will not repeat it at each.

One extension must be declared before the walk begins. The verse at the head of this chapter speaks of the dealings of a decade and a year with each other: war where one Stem controls the other, clash where their Branches clash, and the question of which gives way. The tradition puts that question within one chart. This book will also put it between two, asking what his decade does to her chart and hers to his, and what the two decades do to each other, as Chapter Nine asked what his pillars do to hers. It is the same arithmetic. It is not in the texts, and where the book uses it the reader will be told.

Now walk it.

**Before 1999.** From six to sixteen her decade is 戊寅, and the Tiger is her own Hour Branch repeated: for ten years the Branch that clashes and punishes both her Monkeys stands doubled, with the Eating God above it. The clash of her birth at twice its strength, through her childhood. The first book's 丙申 decade is his twelve to twenty-two, and what it said of it has been quoted. Then come the first pair of his star years in the walk, 1996 and 1997, Yang Fire clashing his Day Master over his own seat repeated, and Yin Fire combining with it over the Ox that holds his seat. 丁丑 is the pillar of his virtue-combination, and 1997 is that pillar as a year; it is also the pillar of the decade she entered in May 1996, so that for her the year is the Luck Pillar itself, year and luck arriving as one in the compendium's narrow sense. The country she lived in from sixteen to twenty-six is the pillar that marries his Day Pillar above and below. She was seventeen. At that age, as the first section said, such a year is a year in the family's fortunes and not a wedding, and the book notes it only because it is the first time the calendar lays one of these charts on the other.

**1999 to 2006: one Stem over both.** From twenty-two to thirty-two his decade is 丁酉, which the first book called the marriage signature of his sequence: the Direct Wealth bound to his Day Master for ten years, and beneath it the Rooster, his Useful God in its purest form and his star's Birth. From sixteen to twenty-six hers is 丁丑: the same Yin Fire, her Rob Wealth, above an Ox that holds three hidden Stems, her Direct Officer, her Direct Wealth and her Hurting Officer, the husband of the fable, what feeds him and what strikes him, shut together in a vault. The Ox is her Heavenly Joy, and her Day Master is at its Nurture there. Set the two decades side by side and their Branches make a half of the Metal frame, Rooster and Ox, with the Rooster as its Peak: in his chart Metal is the Useful God, and in hers it is the Wealth, which the handbook's formula for women would make the husband's own element. For seven years the two clocks, read against each other, were combining.

The years inside it fall where the signals of the first section said they would, and they do not fall on the same year for both. 2001, 辛巳, is her virtue pillar as a year: Yin Metal combining with her Day Master, the Snake in six-harmony with both her Monkeys. The one line about the mandarin ducks blesses it; the two books that know no such line call it the gloom; and the Snake, with her Tiger and her Monkeys, completes the three-way punishment of those three Branches, the one the manuals call the punishment without kindness. A year of being drawn in and pulled about, at twenty-one. 2002, 壬午, is the Song rule's year for him: the Horse clashes his seat as his own Horse does, and the Stem is his own Day Master in the open, so that the year is made of his characters. For her the same Stem is the Seven Killings, her husband by function, standing in the open for the first time in her adult life and striking her Day Master as it arrives; the Horse beneath it is her Blade's Branch, which her birth lacks, and it is the Branch of his Month. 2003, 癸未, is her Direct Officer on the Stem, the husband of the fable that her birth withheld, in the open for one year over her Red Phoenix; for him the Goat harms his seat and the Stem repeats his Hour. The same lore that makes the Goat her Red Phoenix makes it her Widow's Lodge, and the family of stars contradicts itself in one Branch; believe the half. Two consecutive years, then, in which his room is struck in the first and her star is declared in both, and in both of which the Stem is his Water. She turned twenty-two and twenty-three in them, he twenty-six and twenty-seven. 2004, 甲申, completes his Water frame and repeats her palace, the first of the Monkey years whose pattern was given above: the year that completes him lays her room on itself. 2005, 乙酉, is the Peach Blossom for both of them, since both Day Branches belong to the Water frame and share its Rooster.

**2006 to 2016: her decade on his seat.** In May 2006 her clock turned to 丙子, and the pillar should be looked at in both charts. In hers the Stem is her own Day Master, a Companion standing over the decade, and the Rat beneath it carries Yin Water alone: the Direct Officer, the husband of the fable, as the principal Stem of her ground for ten years, and the one god her natal chart has none of. The old note to the chapter on Water and Fire, quoted below, has a name for a pillar of Fire above and Water below: 未济, Not Yet Across, which wants the breath of the earth to rise. The Rat makes a half of the Water frame with her two Monkeys, Birth and Peak, so the frame of her husband's element is half assembled in her own chart by time; and the Rat punishes her Rabbit, the Month Command and the Seal. Yang Water is at its Peak on the Rat, and that is the converse of the rhapsody's rule: the decade faces the husband. In his chart, by the extension declared above, the same pillar is his Year Stem standing over his Day Branch, and it does to him what the year 1996 did: the Windfall clashes his Day Master, his seat is doubled, his Horse is clashed and his Rabbit punished. A reader who wants a single figure for what she is to him by time can take this one: for ten years her ground was his seat, and her Stem was the Fire at the head of his chart. His decade in those years was 丁酉 until early 2009 and then 戊戌, which the first book called the crucible.

Now the years, and this is the window. 2006, 丙戌, is the first year of her new decade and the first of his second pair of star years. The Stem is her Day Master, and it strikes his: the year is her, in the Stem, standing in his chart and clashing it. The Dog beneath completes the Fire frame across the two charts, from her Tiger and his Horse, so that the year floods both with Fire, his wife star's element and hers; in her own chart the Dog is her Tomb, it binds her Rabbit toward Fire, and with her Tiger it makes the cradle of the frame; in his it clashes the Dragon at his Year, and a clash, by the classics this canon follows, opens a vault. 2007, 丁亥, is the loudest year of his loudest decade by the handbooks' count: Yin Fire combining with his Day Master inside the decade of the same Stem, which is a contested combination, and the Pig, his Red Phoenix, standing beside his seat. For her the Pig is her Noble by the lore and the Branch that harms her palace by the relations; it carries her husband by function as its principal Stem, and it combines with her Tiger. 2008, 戊子, doubles the Rat of her decade and repeats his seat; 2009, 己丑, is her Heavenly Joy and the Ox that holds his seat. 2010, 庚寅, is her Hour Pillar as a year, the crouching moan on the Hour, and the Tiger strikes both her Monkeys with the year behind it: this is the Song rule's year for her, by the parity this chapter declared, and she turned thirty in it. 2011, 辛卯, is Yin Metal combining with her Day Master over her Month Branch repeated. For a yang woman the Stem that combines with the Day Master is the Direct Wealth, not a husband star, and that is a difference from his case the reader should hold onto: the signal that is one signal for a yang man is two signals for her, and they never fall in the same year. 2012, 壬辰, is the center of the window. The Stem is the Seven Killings, her husband by function, in the open and striking her Day Master; the Dragon completes the Water frame inside her own chart, from her two Monkeys and the Rat of her decade, so that the frame of her husband's element is whole by time in the year whose Stem is that husband. And the Stem is his Day Master, over the Branch of his Year. The year is him, standing in her chart as the completed frame. She turned thirty-two in it and he thirty-six. 2013, 癸巳, is her Direct Officer on the Stem over a Snake that combines with both her Monkeys and completes the three-way punishment again: the year the Song rule says one seldom marries in, and the year the handbooks would mark for her first; for him the Snake is his Heavenly Joy. So the window in which her charts speak loudest runs from 2010 to 2013, and the window in which his did ran from 2002 to 2007, and the two overlap in no year. That is what two clocks in opposite directions do. Whether these two met in those years, or married, or had already, the book does not know and does not say. It says that the calendar did not hand them a shared year, and that the years in which it spoke for one of them were years in which the Stem was the other.

2014, 甲午, is his Month Pillar as a year: the clash of his birth doubled, the Eating God above it, and his Blade clashed, with the discount the compendium wrote for his Day Master. For her the Horse is a Companion's ground and the Yang Wood above it is the Owl, clashing both her Windfalls. 2015, 乙未, harms his seat and is her Red Phoenix.

**2016 to 2026: the Pig.** In May 2016 her clock turned to 乙亥. The Stem is her Direct Resource, the Seal that commands her chart, and the first thing it meets is her two Yang Metals, which combine with it toward Metal. That is the old note's own example in Chapter Nine, word for word: where 乙 is favorable and 庚 combines with it, and tethers it, because the pair turns to Metal. The decade's Resource arrives and is bound at once by her Wealth, twice; and the binding runs both ways. Chapter Seven asked for the decades and years that strike her Metal or tie it up, since her Metal is what converts her Output before it reaches the husband star. This is the first of them: for ten years both her Yang Metals are held in a combination, and the conversion runs through a bound god. The Pig beneath carries Yang Water as its principal Stem, her husband by function in the ground of the decade and at his Office there, with Yang Wood, more Seal, beneath him. It harms both her Monkeys. It combines with her Tiger, the Branch that strikes them, and the pair turns toward Wood; by the rule this book stated as its own, that is a combination that takes hold of the attacker and feeds its weapon, not one that stills it. It makes a half of the Wood frame with her Rabbit. And it is her Noble, one of the four things the old note names to quiet a struck Branch, standing in harm with the Branch it would quiet. Her Day Master is at its Extinction on the Pig. A decade, then, in which the self is at its faintest, the Seal is doubled and tethered, the husband by function stands in the ground at his Office, and the palace is harmed from beneath by the Branch that houses him.

From early 2019 his clock stood on the same Branch. 己亥 is the pillar the first book called held, structured, waiting: the Officer above, combining with his Eating God; the Pig beneath, his Companion, Yang Water in its Office. For seven years, until May 2026, the two were in one country, which the last section said does not happen, and it was the country of the Pig: the Branch of Yang Water's Office, his Day Master's and her husband's. Read the two decades against each other and their Stems are at war, Yin Wood controlling Yin Earth, and the war has a face in each chart: his decade's Stem is, in her chart, the Hurting Officer, the god that strikes the husband star, and hers is, in his, the Hurting Officer that strikes his Officer. Her decade's Stem controls his, and in each case the god that controls is the one that attacks what the other decade brought. The ground is the same beneath both.

The years. 2016, 丙申, is her Day Pillar as a year: the crouching moan in full, the pillar laid on itself, and a third Monkey in her chart for the Tiger to strike. 生方怕动, the places of birth dread being set in motion, and in 2016 three of them were. For him the same year completes his Water frame and is the pillar of his adolescence come back, Windfall above and supply beneath. The year that is her, in Stem and Branch, is the year of her heaviness and of his completion; the pattern of the Monkey years at full strength, because this time the Stem is hers as well. She turned thirty-six, he forty. 2017, 丁酉, is the Peach Blossom for both, Yin Fire binding him, the Rooster her Noble and the second member of her Metal row. 2018, 戊戌, is his Luck Pillar as a year, 岁运并临 in the compendium's narrow sense, and its judgment on that case named the Seven Killings as one of the two gods that make it ill; the Dog completes the Fire frame across the two charts again. 2019, 己亥, is his next Luck Pillar as a year, the Officer, which the same judgment calls good, and his Red Phoenix; in her chart the Pig now stands three deep, her decade, his decade and the year, so that her palace is harmed from three sides by a Branch that is three times her Noble. 2020, 庚子, repeats his seat and half-frames her palace, and its Stem is his Useful God in the open for a year and her Windfall repeated. 2021, 辛丑, combines with her Day Master and turns toward Water, her husband's element, over her Heavenly Joy; in his chart it is the pillar of his sixties come early, the True Patron binding his Year Stem, which is the character of her Day Master, and the Ox holding his seat and harming his Horse. For one year the calendar did to his chart what the first book said the 辛丑 decade would do, and bound, in character, the Stem that is her.

2022, 壬寅, is the heaviest year of her forties and the book will give it room. Against her Day Pillar 丙申 the year 壬寅 is the turning moan: Yang Water against Yang Fire, Tiger against Monkey, the Stem and the Branch of her seat overturned at once, and the old proverb has the tears in streams. The Stem is the Seven Killings, her husband by function, striking her. The Tiger clashes and punishes both her Monkeys, with the year behind it and in her Hour's own character; it is the Song rule's year for her, and for a married woman the rule's meaning is the one the last section gave, the year the household is moved. And the Stem is his Day Master. The year is him, in the Stem, overturning her seat. What stands against the Tiger is the Pig of both decades, which combines with it; but the pair turns toward Wood, the Tiger's own weapon, and by this book's rule that is not a stilling. For him the same year is a Companion on the Stem over a Tiger that carries his star and makes a half of the Fire frame with his Horse: a year he has, by his own chart, almost nothing in. Read for him alone it is quiet. Read for her alone it is the turning moan on the seat of the self. Read for two, it is the year in which the calendar was made of his Day Master and of the Branch that is at once her Hour and her palace's attacker, and in which the man who is her husband by every reckoning of Chapter Eight stood, as a Stem, against the Day Master he is married to. The first book's counsel for his own turning moan was that it is weather, and one dresses for it. For two people the counsel has a second half, and here it is his to hear: the weather of 2022 fell on her in his character, and a man in his place would do well to know it.

2023, 癸卯, is her Direct Officer on the Stem over her Month Branch repeated; for him it is his Hour Pillar as a year, the crouching moan on the Hour, and the punishment of his seat doubled. 2024, 甲辰, completes the Water frame across the two charts, from her Monkey, his Rat and the year's Dragon, which also repeats his Year Branch; the Yang Wood above repeats his Month Stem and clashes both her Windfalls. 2025, 乙巳, doubles the Stem of her decade, tethered again by her two Metals, over a Snake that combines with both her Monkeys and completes the punishment of her three Branches; for him the Snake is his Heavenly Joy.

**2026 to 2036: the present.** This book is written in 2026, and the year is 丙午. The first book read it for him: his Year Stem and his Month Branch arriving together as a year, and against his Day Pillar the turning moan, Fire against Water and Horse against Rat, a year that asks for caution in the body, the marriage and the collision of work and home. Add what this chapter adds. The Horse clashes his Blade, which is the Blade saying's first case; his decade's Pig does not touch the Blade, so the compendium halves the trouble; and the exception it wrote for Yang Water meeting its Blade in the clash of Rat and Horse applies to him by name. For her the Stem is her own Day Master: the year is her, as 2016 was, and this time it stands in his chart striking his. The Horse is the Branch of her Blade, which her birth lacks, and it makes a half of the Fire frame with her Tiger. And in May her clock turned to 甲戌. The Dog is the third member of that frame. From May 2026 her Hour, her decade and the year assemble the Fire frame inside her own chart, with the Peak supplied by the year, whose Branch is also the Branch of his Month: her chart is flooded with her own element by time in the year whose Stem is herself, and the flood runs through his working ground. The Dog is also her Tomb. Her Day Master is carried into storage for ten years at the moment her element is at its fullest, which is not a contradiction; it is the difference between the self and the self's element, and the book will return to it in a moment.

What 2026 is between them should be put in one figure. The two charts hold two frames, the Water frame of his seat and her palace and the Fire frame of his Month, her Hour and now her decade, and in 2026 the Horse, the Peak of the one, strikes the Rat, the Peak of the other. 滴天髓 has a chapter on exactly this, under the names of the two trigrams, 坎离, and its verse is 坎离气不并行，而有相济者在: the breaths of Water and Fire do not run side by side, and yet there are cases in which they complete each other. Ren Tieqiao's note draws the working rule. 水火相战，不能缺木 — where Water and Fire make war, Wood cannot be lacking, 以水与木为调和通关之神也, for Water and Wood are the gods that harmonize and open the pass; and a chart that has none must find it in its luck. Wood is what both charts have, his Month Stem and his Hour, her Month and the Stem of her new decade, with the Eastern row of Tiger, Rabbit and Dragon complete between them. The go-between the old chapter asks for is present on both sides. It is a year to use it. For her the year is also the year of a Companion, her own character standing in the open over the Branch of her Blade, and a woman's chart reads a Companion year as a year of contest for what she holds; the book records the rule and makes nothing more of it.

Her new decade needs its own paragraph, because it is the first the first book could not have known about and the one she will be living in while this is read. 甲戌 is the Owl over the Dog. The Yang Wood above is her Indirect Resource, and it meets her two Yang Metals and is clashed by both: 甲 and 庚 are at war, and the war is the old hazard of her Resource structure, Wealth ruining the Seal, fought in the open for ten years. It is also the second of the seasons Chapter Seven asked for, in which her Metal is struck rather than bound, and with it the years of Yang Wood inside and around it, 2024, 2034 and 2044. The same Yang Wood combines with her Month Stem, the Hurting Officer, and the pair turns to Earth; that is the other of the old note's two examples from Chapter Nine, where 己 is harmful and 甲 combines with it and helps it, because the pair turns to Earth. For the husband star it is a mixed thing: the Seal binds the god that strikes the husband, which the women's chapters welcome, and the binding feeds that god's element. The Dog beneath combines with her Rabbit toward Fire, so that the Month Command is bound toward herself; it makes the cradle of the Fire frame with her Tiger, completed by his Horse and, in 2026, by the year; it stands with her Monkeys as two of the Metal row; and it is her Tomb. By the extension, read against his chart, the Dog does what the Dog of his own 戊戌 decade did: it clashes the Dragon at his Year and opens that vault, it makes a half of the Fire frame with his Horse, and it binds his Rabbit toward Fire; and the Yang Wood above is his own Month Stem, the Eating God doubled. The ground of his crucible returns, this time as her ground.

From early 2029 his clock turns to 庚子, the pillar the first book called the most significant of his sequence and read with both eyes open: the Useful God in the Stem at last, his seat doubled beneath it, and the gift delivered by clashing his Month Command and seizing his Eating God. In the pairing it has a further face. Yang Metal over the Rat is her Year and Hour Stems over the Branch that half-frames her palace: what she is to him in what she carries, arriving as a decade. And set the two decades against each other, his 庚子 and her 甲戌, and their Stems are at war from 2029 to 2036, Yang Metal controlling Yang Wood, the clash that in his chart is the Owl seizing the Food and in hers is Wealth ruining the Seal, the price of the medicine in both. His wife star should be followed through these years too. Yin Fire is his Direct Wealth, and on the Rat it is at its Extinction, as on the Ox after it it is in its Tomb: the two decades of his structural prime, by the first book, carry the wife star through the two stages the rhapsodies name for the parting of the paired birds, 财衰败墓绝. Chapter Six took those lines as a measure of the star's availability and not as a sentence, and the book takes them so here. What they say is that from 2029 to 2049 the star in his own chart is at its least, and that whatever Fire he has in those years he has from elsewhere.

The years. 2027, 丁未, is Yin Fire combining with his Day Master, the Goat harming his seat and holding his Horse in the harmony of noon and afternoon, so that for one year the Branch that has struck his palace all his life is held; for her it is Rob Wealth over her Red Phoenix. 2028, 戊申, completes his Water frame and repeats her palace, now with the Dog of her decade making two of the Metal row; and in her chart the year's Yang Earth, her Eating God, is controlled by her decade's Yang Wood, which is the verse's war between the luck and the year, the Owl seizing the Food by time. 2029, 己酉, is the Peach Blossom for both and her Noble. 2030, 庚戌, completes the Fire frame across the two charts again and doubles the Dog of her decade; its Yang Metal controls her decade's Yang Wood, the year at war with the luck, and the verse asks which gives way: the Wealth or the Seal. 2031, 辛亥, combines with her Day Master toward Water and harms her palace; it is his Red Phoenix. 2032, 壬子, is his Day Pillar as a year, inside a decade whose Branch is the same Rat: the crouching moan, three deep, with the Blade doubled by the year and the decade together, which is the old lines' own case of year and luck arriving as one and their sentence is that calamity is at the door. The discounts of the Blade paragraph apply, and the verdict is tempered by the diagnosis it rests on; it is not cancelled, and three Rats against the Horse of his month is the clash of his birth at a strength it has never had. For her 2032 is the Seven Killings on the Stem striking her Day Master, the Direct Officer in the Branch, and the Rat making a half of the Water frame with both her Monkeys: both husband stars in one pillar, which is his Day Pillar, joining her palace to his seat in the frame of his element. 2016 was the year that was her, and it completed him and laid her on herself; 2032 is the year that is him, and it lays him on himself and joins her room to his. 2033, 癸丑, is her Direct Officer twice, Stem and Branch, over her Heavenly Joy, and the Ox combining with his Blade inside the Blade's own decade. 2034, 甲寅, is the Song rule's Tiger again for her, with her decade's Stem doubled above it and both her Windfalls clashed; she turns fifty-four. 2036, 丙辰, is his Year Pillar as a year and completes the Water frame across the two charts, and its Stem is her Day Master; in May her clock turns.

**2036 to 2049: the Officer and the Patron.** 癸酉, from fifty-six to sixty-six, puts the Direct Officer on her Luck Stem for ten years: the husband of the fable, which her birth did not hold and which came to her before only as a year, standing over her sixties. The Rooster beneath it is her Direct Wealth, her Peach Blossom and her Noble, and it feeds the Officer above: 财旺生官. It also clashes her Rabbit, the Month Command, which the first book called the gravest of clashes, and with her two Monkeys it makes two of the Metal row, so that the Wealth which feeds her Officer is the Wealth that ruins her Seal, and her Day Master is at its Death on the Rooster. The decade gives her the husband by relation and strikes the self that would receive him. By the extension the same pillar in his chart is his Hour Stem repeated over a Rooster that combines with his Dragon toward Metal, as the Rooster of his 丁酉 decade did, and clashes his Hour. His own clock turns in early 2039 to 辛丑, and the first book read it: the True Patron binding his Year Stem, the Ox holding his seat in the harmony of the night and harming his Horse, the Useful God present above and sealed below. In the pairing the Patron binds, in character, her Day Master; and by the extension the same Yin Metal is the one Stem that combines with her Day Master, turning toward Water, so that for ten years his decade would do by binding what the two Day Masters do by war. The two decades 辛丑 and 癸酉 stand in a half of the Metal frame, as 丁酉 and 丁丑 did forty years before. The clocks that combined in his twenties combine again in his sixties.

The years in it are the ones the frames and the Blade have already named. 2037, 丁巳, binds him and is his Heavenly Joy, and the Snake combines with her Monkeys and completes her punishment. 2038, 戊午, clashes his Blade inside the decade that repeats it: a half by the compendium's whole-and-half rule, since the decade's Rat neither clashes nor combines, and the other saying's case besides. Its gravest clause, a natally clashed Blade clashed or combined again by decade and year together, does not apply here, and the discounts stand. 2040, 庚申, completes his frame inside the decade whose Ox combines with his Blade and lays her palace on itself a fourth time. 2041, 辛酉, binds her Day Master and doubles the Rooster of her decade, the Peach Blossom for both. 2042, 壬戌, completes the Fire frame across the charts under his Day Master; 2043, 癸亥, is her Direct Officer over the Pig that harms her palace and carries her husband by function, and his Red Phoenix. 2044, 甲子, doubles his seat inside the decade that combines it. 2046, 丙寅, is the Song rule's Tiger for her at sixty-six under her own Day Master, and in May her clock turns to 壬申: the Seven Killings over her seat repeated, a decade that strikes her Day Master from above and lies on her palace from beneath, and whose Stem is his. From early 2049 his clock is 壬寅, and the two decades then stand under one Stem, Yang Water, his Companion and her Killings, over a Tiger and a Monkey that clash. The clash of her birth, Hour against Day, stands between the two lives as their two grounds. He will be seventy-three and she sixty-nine.

That is the reading of time for these two, and its outline is simple enough to hold in the mind. Two clocks running in opposite directions, so that no year brings the spouse star to both and every year that speaks for one is a year whose Stem is the other. A pillar of his adolescence that is her Day Pillar, read blind by the first book as pressure above and supply beneath. One Stem shared in his twenties, his marriage and her competition; one ground shared in his forties, her marriage and his company. Her loudest window and his, four years apart and not overlapping. A present year in which the Peak of the one frame strikes the Peak of the other, with the go-between present on both sides. A decade coming for him that is what she carries, and one for her that is his crucible's ground. And at the end, two clocks under one Stem, which is his, over the two Branches of her own clash.

---

## Chapter Eleven — The Household

The oldest portrait of a marriage in Chinese is a disagreement about getting up. It is in the Odes. The wife says the cock has crowed; the man says it is not yet light. Get up and look at the night, she tells him — the morning star is shining. So he goes out to shoot wild duck and geese, and she says what she will do with them when he brings them home:

> 弋言加之，与子宜之。宜言饮酒，与子偕老。琴瑟在御，莫不静好。
>
> When your arrows find them, I will dress them for you. Dressed, we will drink over them, and I will grow old with you. The zither and the lute are in their places, and nothing here is not quiet and good.

That is the thing itself. Not the wedding, which the ritual books describe, and not the fate, which the manuals compute, but a morning: who wakes whom, who goes out, who cooks, and the two instruments standing ready in the room. The 易 has a hexagram for it, 家人 (Jiā Rén), the Household, and its commentary asks of the person who would keep one only two things: 言有物，而行有恒 — that one's words have substance, and one's conduct have constancy.

A chart has less to say about mornings than about decades, and a book should admit it. But it has something to say, because everything the last ten chapters established has a daily form. The palace is the room a person makes around themselves. The Ten Gods are the roles a person plays without deciding to. The exchange between two charts is what each of them is doing for the other on an ordinary Tuesday. This chapter reads those things at the scale of a household. Most of it is application: the classical doctrines are classical, and their use as a description of who does the shopping is this book's. Where an old text speaks directly to the point, it is quoted.

### Five offices

A household needs five things done, and they are the five relations.

Someone must shelter. Resource is the element that generates the self, and in a house it is everything that restores: the meal that is simply there, the patience, the knowledge of how things are done, the room one is allowed to be tired in. The person who holds this office for another is that person's rest.

Someone must stand alongside. Companions are the self's own kind, and in a house they are friendship: the partner as equal, as ally, as the one who carries the other end of the table. Where this office is filled a couple can work side by side. It is also the office of the rival, and the question the first book asked about the Rival in a spouse palace — what is mine and what is yours — is asked in such a house about everything from money to credit.

Someone must make and speak. Output is what the self generates, and in a house it is play, talk, cooking, invention, children: all that comes out of a person and brightens a room. The Eating God holds the office gently, as pleasure. The Hurting Officer holds it sharply, as wit and as criticism.

Someone must manage. Wealth is what the self controls, and in a house it is the purse, the practical arrangements, the tending of bodies and things. To be another person's Wealth is to be what they take responsibility for, and to be what gives their effort an object.

And someone must hold the line. Authority is what controls the self, and in a house it is the rule: standards, limits, the public face, the thing one does not do. To be another person's Authority is to be the one before whom they behave.

Chapter Eight's table of translations now tells who holds which office. What his Day Master is to hers is the principal office he holds for her, by simply being who he is. What the bulk of his chart is to her — the element he has most of, seen through her Day Master — is the office his whole world tends to fill in her life: his friends, his work, his way of spending a day. And the same in the other direction. A woman whose Day Master is her husband's Resource and whose chart is, to him, mostly Authority, is his rest in person and his rule in everything she brings with her. Both are true at once, and couples recognize the distinction when it is put to them: what you are to me, and what your life is to mine.

Every office has its excess, and the excess is always the same god in too great a quantity. Shelter becomes management of another adult. Alliance becomes competition. Expression becomes a commentary on the other's failings. Stewardship becomes ownership. The rule becomes fault-finding. The reading does not accuse; it locates. A couple who know that one of them is, by the charts, the other's Authority, know in advance which quarrel is theirs.

### Who leads

The old world had an answer to this before any chart was cast, and the manuals record, with visible discomfort, how often the charts disagreed with it.

> 女命印旺官轻，夫权在手；男命财多身弱，妻语惬心。
>
> A woman's chart with the Seal strong and the Officer light: the husband's authority is in her hand. A man's chart with Wealth abundant and the self weak: his wife's word is what satisfies his heart.

That is 三命通会, and it is not complaining. It is describing. 渊海子平 says of the fortunate arrangement, 财旺生官，夫唱妇随 — where Wealth is strong and feeds the Officer, the husband leads the song and the wife takes it up; and the commentary on the Household hexagram wants 女正位乎内，男正位乎外, the woman's right place within and the man's without.
