But the charts, read without prejudice, distribute the lead by weight and not by sex. Where one of two people has a strong Day Master and a spouse star it can easily carry, and the other has a weak Day Master under a star too heavy for it, the first will lead in the house, because the bond costs that one less. No rule about who ought to lead survives contact with that fact, and the tradition's better observers did not pretend it did.

What the charts can add is whether the arrangement is likely to sit easily. It sits easily where the one who leads is also, for the other, a welcome god — the Resource of a weak chart, the Authority of a strong and scattered one. It grates where the one who leads is the other's excess. And the oldest gloss in the language is still the best standard: 妻者，齐也. Level. Not identical in weight, which no two charts are, but each standing at full height on their own ground.

### The purse

Chapter Four insisted that Wealth is the wife and is also wealth, and the household is where that double meaning is lived. Money in a marriage behaves the way the Wealth stars of the two charts behave.

A man of abundant Wealth and a weak self is the poor man in the rich house: resources pass through his hands that he cannot hold. In such a house the old line about hearing the wife's word first is sound domestic economy. A chart heavy with Rob Wealth is generous to the point of leakage; the first book's phrase was social boldness with a leak in the purse. A Wealth star in its storehouse is the rhapsody's thrifty wife — or, since the palace is the same for either sex, simply the partner who saves. A woman's Wealth is her own competence, the thing Ren Tieqiao called the husband's star of grace, and Chapter Seven showed that in her chart it feeds the husband star and guards it. The plainest inference the classical material allows is this: the purse should be kept by whichever of the two has the Wealth their chart can carry. The tradition never said so, because it had already decided who kept the purse. Its own doctrine says so.

The translation table adds the most practical line in this book. One person's Output is frequently the other's Wealth: it is so whenever his element generates hers, or hers generates his. In such couples, what one of them makes is what the other is fitted to manage. They do not need to negotiate a division of labor. They need to notice the one they already have.

### The other family

The manuals read kin by the same cycle as everything else, and what they found there about marriage is the most universal domestic fact there is.

For a man, the wife is Wealth and the mother is Resource. Wealth controls Resource. Chapter Six met this as 贪财坏印, greed for Wealth ruining the Seal, and translated it: the man between his wife and his mother. It is built into the definitions. Every man's wife star stands in the controlling relation to his mother star, and the only question a chart can answer is whether the two are kept apart, or bridged — the Officer stands between them, fed by Wealth and feeding Resource, which is one reason the old books prized it — or left to fight. A man whose chart has a strong, needed Resource and a strong Wealth beside it with nothing between will live that conflict however fond his wife and his mother are of each other.

For a woman, the parents-in-law are read from the element that generates her husband star, and that is her Wealth. Ren Tieqiao states it in passing as a thing everyone knows: 不孝翁姑，只为财轻劫重 — want of duty toward a husband's parents comes only from Wealth that is light and Rob Wealth that is heavy. On that reckoning, and the gloss is this book's, the house she marries into is in her chart her own Wealth: something she is expected to tend, and something her Companions compete with her for. Chen Su'an preferred to place a woman's parents-in-law with her own parents, under Resource, as the elders who stand over her. The two reckonings can be put to different uses, though neither author proposed it: the one asks what she owes that house, the other what it gives her. In either, a woman's relation to her husband's family is read from her own chart first, like everything else.

Across two charts the families meet at the Year Pillars, as Chapter Nine said, and at whatever touches them. A combination between one person's Day Master and the other's Year Stem is a bond with that family; a harm or a clash between the Year Branches is two backgrounds that do not sit easily. The marketplace read a whole marriage from that one contact. It is real. It is the outermost ring.

### Children

On the child star the tradition changed its mind, and the change matters for couples.

The old reckoning gave a man's children to his Authority — the Killings his sons, the Officer his daughters — and a woman's to her Output. It was not arbitrary. Shen Xiaozhan gives its reason in a sentence: 官煞者，财所生也，财为妻妾，则官煞为子女矣 — Officer and Killings are what Wealth generates; Wealth is the wife; so Officer and Killings are the children. A man's children were counted as what his wife bears. The seasonal handbook has the same shape in functional dress: name the Useful God, and the element that generates it is the wife and the Useful God itself the child. The old note to 滴天髓 still reads a man's children from the Officer, and the verse it explains ties them to the Killings by name: 子女根枝一世传，喜神看与杀相联 — sons and daughters, root and branch, are handed down a lifetime; see how the favorable god stands linked with the Killings.

Chen Su'an and Ren Tieqiao both rejected it, in nearly the same words. Chen: 今定男以印为父母，食神伤官为子，我克之财为妻 — let it now be settled that for a man Resource is the parents, Eating God and Hurting Officer the children, and the Wealth he controls the wife; and for a woman the same, with the Officer and Killings for the husband. Ren: what generates me is father and mother; what I generate is son and daughter. This is the reckoning this canon follows. It keeps the cycle whole, since a child is then what a person generates, whichever the sex; and it has a cost the reformers accepted, which is that it stops reading a man's children through his wife.

Having reformed the star, Ren at once declined to be bound by it. Fix the Output first, he says, and then look to the strength of the Day Master and to what the chart welcomes. His own rule of thumb, which he says he had tested, is 大率身旺财为子，身衰印作儿 — in the main, for a strong self Wealth serves as the child, and for a weak self, Resource. It is the functional doctrine of Chapter Four again: the child, like the spouse, is in the end what the chart needs. For a woman he says so outright, in the sentence quoted in Chapter Seven: her husband star is the Useful God, and her child star is the Auspicious God.

So there are two questions, as there were for the spouse, and it helps to say which governs when. The star by relation, Output, says what children are to a person in kind: what one makes, what comes out of one, and for a woman what stands against the husband star unless Wealth converts it. The god by function says whether, in this particular chart, children are a support or a strain, and where to look for them when the Output is absent, buried or overwhelmed. Read the first for the nature of the tie and the second for its fortune.

The palace is not in dispute. Every school reads children at the Hour, and the oldest instrument for it is the one Chapter Seven met among the eight formats: the life stage at which the child star arrives at the Hour Branch. Shen Xiaozhan tells the student to learn the old counting rhyme by heart, so many sons at Birth and so many at the Peak and none at Death, and then tells him what matters more, which is whether the Hour Stem carries something the structure can use. Where it does, he says, then even with the Hour at Death or Extinction the children do well, only they are not many. A child star that reaches the Hour in vigor was read as children who come readily; one that reaches it at Death, Tomb or Extinction, as children who come late, or few, or hard.

The counting rhyme should be left where it lies. No chart counts children, and none decides whether there will be any. What the manuals noticed is narrower and more interesting: the charts they called childless are, for the most part, charts of a certain climate. Ren Tieqiao's list for a woman's chart reads like a description of ground on which nothing will grow:

> 火炎土燥无子，土金湿滞无子，水泛木浮无子，金寒水冷无子。
>
> Fire blazing and Earth parched: no children. Earth and Metal sodden and stagnant: none. Water in flood and Wood adrift: none. Metal cold and Water freezing: none.

Three more follow that are imbalance of another kind: Resource in layers, Wealth and Officer too strong, a chart that is all Output. Read as medicine these lines are worthless, and no one should be told anything about their body on the strength of them. Read as the tradition's own account of what a child star needs — warmth, moisture in measure, and room — they give a couple one question worth asking. Chapter Eight asked of two charts whether each brings the other's missing season. That question bears on the two Hours before it bears on anything else.

Timing follows the grammar of Chapter Ten. Where children come, they come in decades and years that bring the child star or what feeds it, that complete its frame, or that join or move the Hour Pillar; and the years that strike the Hour are, as with the palace, the years in which that part of the household is moved.

Now set the child star beside the spouse stars, and something appears that the reformers did not remark on. A man's child star generates his wife star: Output feeds Wealth. A woman's child star controls her husband star: Output strikes Authority. For him, by the bare cycle, children run toward the marriage. For her they run against it, unless Wealth stands between and converts the one into the other. The asymmetry belongs to the assignment of the stars and to nothing deeper: it follows from taking a man's spouse in what he controls and a woman's in what controls her. Chapter Seven found this inside the woman's chart as the contest of the eight formats. For a household the cycle suggests, and suggests no more than, this: that the coming of children draws on the bond differently for the two of them, and that what keeps her husband star fed through those years is Wealth, which in a house is the shared, practical economy of it. Where the husband is taken by function the pattern follows the function, and a woman whose husband star is her Output has husband and children on one side of the cycle from the start.

Between two charts, the children's ground is the pair of Hours: the Stems and Branches there, their relation to each other, and what each Hour is to the other person's Day Master. A harmony between the two Hour Branches is a private world easily shared. A clash or a harm there is two people who, late at night and late in life, want different things from the same room.

### Work and home

Inside one chart the Month is the arena of work and the Day is the self at home, and the relation between those two Branches is the first book's reading of how career and intimacy sit together in one life. A couple has four such relations: his Month to his Day and hers to hers, which each brought to the marriage; and his Month to her Day and her Month to his, which the marriage made.

The crossed ones are worth more attention than they get. His Month Branch is his season and his working world at full strength — it is the commander of his chart — and what it does to her Day Branch is what his work does to her home ground. A harmony there is a working life that feeds the other's peace. A clash or a harm is a working life that costs it. The same from her side. Many couples who cannot understand why one of them finds the other's career so much harder to live with than the reverse are looking at an asymmetry in these two contacts.

### How they speak and how they live

滴天髓's distinction between war in heaven and war on earth, quoted in Chapter Nine, is a distinction every household knows. The Stems are what is declared; the Branches are what is lived. Relations between the Stems of two charts describe how the two of them talk. Relations between the Branches describe how they inhabit a house.

So there are couples whose Stems combine and whose Branches clash: delighted with each other in conversation, and unable to share a kitchen. And there are couples whose Stems clash over Branches in harmony: sharp with each other in every exchange, and settled in everything they do without speaking. The verse says which matters more. Heaven's war can still be borne; earth's war is urgent as fire. A reader weighing two charts for daily life should weigh the Branches first and should not be put off by noise among the Stems where the ground is quiet.

The relation between the Day Masters gives the conversation its standing roles. The one who is the other's Resource explains, supplies and corrects. The one who is the other's Output performs, provokes and reports. Authority rules on things; Wealth reports to and is answered for; Companions argue as equals. Opposite polarity makes each role gentler in form — the direct gods — and same polarity makes it blunter. None of this is destiny. It is the part each will fall into when tired.

### Warmth

The manuals are prudish and the cosmology is not. The 易 says without embarrassment that the essences of male and female join and the ten thousand things are born, and its hexagram of attraction, 咸, puts the young man below the young woman — 男下女 — and says that is why it prospers. The image attached to the same hexagram is one of the best sentences ever written about being loved: 君子以虚受人, the noble one receives the other by being empty.

What the charts show of desire is modest and can be said briefly. A combination between Day Masters is felt as attraction, as Chapter Nine said. So is the meeting of a Day Master with its combining partner anywhere in the other's chart. The Bathing stage, where a Day Master sits on it or a decade brings it, is the stage of exposure and of being seen. The lore adds the Peach Blossom, and adds that where one person's Peach Blossom Branch is the other's Day Branch the pull is strong; Part Two gives the table and the caution.

More useful than any of that is the plain question of where the warmth in the pair is. Fire is warmth, display and the wish to be seen; Water is depth and withholding; Metal is reserve; Wood is growth and tenderness; Earth is steadiness. Two charts with no Fire between them tend to coolness, which is not want of feeling. Two charts full of it tend to blaze and to scorch. And where one has it all, that one carries the warmth of the house and should know that it is being carried for two.

### What is left to the two of them

It would be a poor chapter on households that ended on elements. The classical authors who knew most about charts were the firmest that a chart is not a marriage. Ren Tieqiao, in the passage quoted in Chapter Seven, would not put a wife's conduct down to the chart alone, and named beside it the husband's worthlessness and the failings of the house. Chen Su'an left the rest to Heaven. And the Household hexagram, which had every opportunity to speak of fortune, speaks only of conduct: words with substance, constancy in what one does, and in its third line the observation that a house run with some severity has regrets but comes to good, while a house where everyone is always laughing ends in shame.

The charts say what each will tend to give and tend to take, where the friction is, and when the weather turns. Everything in a morning that matters — whether the one who wakes first is kind about it — is not in them.

### The charts

Assemble the household of this book's pair from what the chapters have found.

The offices. To him she is the Windfall: his Wealth, in person, and the indirect form of it. By the table of this chapter that makes her the thing he takes responsibility for and the thing that gives his effort an object, and it makes the office he holds for her, by simply being who he is, the one the Killings hold: he is her rule, the one before whom she behaves, in the sharp form. In the terms of this chapter he holds the line and she is what the line is held for. It is a real division, and it is the fable's, and its excess is the one the table predicts for each god: on his side the rule becomes fault-finding, and on hers stewardship becomes ownership, since what a Windfall is to the one who holds it is something seized rather than cultivated. Each of them should recognize the other's excess as the same gift at the wrong volume. The first book's figure for the Yang Fire at the head of his chart was the sun that illuminates the ocean while the ocean reflects the sun, a polarity that is also generative. That Stem is her character, and the figure is the figure for her.

Then what each chart brings as a whole, which is the other half of the office. Hers, to him, is Resource and Authority in equal and the largest measure, four of each, then Companions, Output and Wealth, two of each. Her world is his supply and his standard at once: the four Metals that his chart has never had, two of them declared, and the four Earths, three Killings and an Officer, that hold him to a law he did not write. A man who has been the poor man in the rich house all his life has married a house that feeds him and expects something of him, and asks him to manage very little of it, since what she brings him of his own Wealth is two Fires, and one of them is herself. His chart, to her, is Authority first, four Waters, then Resource, three Woods, then two Companions and two Outputs, and of Wealth nothing. He is her rule in person and her rule in bulk, with his Wood beside it as her shelter; he brings her no rival of her own kind beyond the two Fires that are her support; and he brings her nothing whatever to steward. A woman whose own chart is four Metals of Wealth, a steward by birth, has married a world that adds nothing to her load. Put the two halves together and the distinction this chapter drew is as sharp here as it is anywhere: what she is to him, his Wealth, and what her life is to him, his supply and his law, are different gods; what he is to her and what his life is to her are the same god, and it is Authority.

Who leads? Both charts answer separately and they agree. Hers is the compendium's first case to the letter: the Seal strong, in command of the month and in season, and the Officer light, the Direct Officer absent and the Killings twice hidden; 夫权在手, the husband's authority is in her hand. His is its second case to the letter: Wealth abundant and the self weak; 妻语惬心, his wife's word is what satisfies his heart. The old books would have regretted both charts and been doubly sorry to see them marry, and there is nothing to regret. Both Day Masters are weak, and the lead goes by cost: her husband star is her equal in strength, Chapter Five found, and his wife star is more than he can lift, so the bond costs her less and she will lead. Whether it sits easily is the finer question, and the answer is divided in the way Chapter Eight said everything about her is divided. In person she is his excess, one more Fire for a man who has too much of it, and a lead held by the other's excess grates. In what she brings she is the Resource of a weak chart, the god whose lead sits most easily of all. The quarrel that is theirs, by the charts, is the one in which she is to him one more demand of the kind his life is full of; the arrangement that is theirs is the one in which what she brings supplies him. From her side the lead sits easily for a reason her own chart gives: he is her Killings, and the compendium's rule for her structure is that the Seal which meets the Killings flourishes. She will lead, and the one she leads is the god her chart was built to be forged by. 妻者，齐也. Level, in the gloss's sense: not equal in weight, but each standing at full height on their own ground.

The purse. His Wealth is Fire, in command of his season while he is Imprisoned in it, and it has always been too much for him. Hers is Metal, four presences, declared on two Stems and at the head of her palace twice, and it is Imprisoned in her season while she is in Mutual Generation. Neither carries their Wealth lightly, and she carries hers better, because the season is on her side and against her Wealth, and against him and for his. By the rule of this chapter the purse is hers. The practical line about Output and Wealth does not hold for this pair, since neither element generates the other, and what holds instead is more instructive. His Output is Wood and Wood is her Resource: what comes out of him restores her. Her Output is Earth and Earth is his Authority: what comes out of her governs him. Her Month Stem is the Hurting Officer, and to him it is the Direct Officer, the Magistrate; her wit, her criticism and her making are, in his chart, the law. His Month Stem is the Eating God, and to her it is the Owl: his play and his inventions are, in hers, instruction of the unorthodox kind. It is not the division in which one makes and the other manages. It is the one in which one speaks and the other is taught, and one plays and the other is ruled, and the two run in opposite directions.

The other family. His mother star is Resource, which is Metal, which his chart does not contain, and she brings four; his wife star is Fire, and she is one. The conflict that the definitions build into every man's chart, between what he pursues and what restores him, is in this pairing between what his wife is and what she carries, and both are in one person. Inside her own chart the same relation is a healthy one, the Day Master governing its own Wealth and so protecting its Seal; seen from his, it is his Wealth standing over his Resource, 贪财坏印 with both parties under one roof. Chapter Nine found the sharper form of it: her two Yang Metals strike his Month Stem while they feed him. From her side, by Ren Tieqiao's reckoning, the house she marries into is her Wealth, and her Wealth is four Metals with no Rob Wealth to compete for them; his line about want of duty toward a husband's parents names the opposite chart from hers. By Chen Su'an's reckoning the same house stands under her Resource, which is the commander of her chart. On either count his family stands large in her chart, as something she tends or something that stands over her. At the Year Pillars, where the families meet, his Year Stem is her own character, so that his origins are to her a Companion, her own kind; and the two Year Branches, Dragon and Monkey, are the two ends of the Water frame with its Peak missing, and the Peak is his seat. The marketplace, reading the two backgrounds from that single contact, would have found two that fit through him. The one harm in the pair, Chapter Nine found, lies between his Year and her Month: his background and her season.

Children. By relation his children are his Output, Wood, which stands on his Month Stem and inside his Rabbit and his Dragon; hers are her Output, Earth, which stands on her Month Stem and inside both her Monkeys and her Tiger. Her child star controls her husband star, as it does in every woman's chart read by relation, and Chapter Seven found how her chart converts it: her Earth feeds her four Metals and her Metals feed the Water, so that the Hurting Officer that would wound a Killings reaches it as food. By function the answers move. Ren's rule for a woman makes her child star her Auspicious God, which by the first book's method is Wood, her Resource; his rule of thumb for a weak self makes a man's child star his Resource, which for him is Metal. So the element in which he reads his children by relation is the one in which she reads hers by function, and it is the element of the East that stands complete between their charts; and the element in which he would read his by function is the one she carries four of. At the Hours the old instrument gives his child star its Peak, since the Rabbit is Wood's own Branch, and gives hers its Birth, since Yang Earth is born in the Tiger, with the Eating God lying in the Tiger as its residual Stem. Shen Xiaozhan's finer question is what the Hour Stem carries. His carries Yin Water, a Rival to his structure and, to her, the Direct Officer she was born without. Hers carries Yang Metal, the Wealth that is her structure's hazard and, to him, the Owl that feeds him. In climate neither chart is on Ren's list of ground where nothing grows: his is a summer chart with heat to spare and hers a spring chart with every element present, and each has the season the other lacks. The two Hours are the Rabbit and the Tiger, neighbours in the calendar and two of the three Branches of the East, which his Dragon completes; nothing strikes or harms between them. Both were born in the last hours of the night, she in the hour of the Tiger and he in the hour of the Rabbit that follows it. The private worlds of these two are one Branch apart on the wheel, and they lean the same way.

Work and home. In his own chart the Month clashes the Day, the first book's defining interaction. In hers the Month and the Day have nothing to say to each other; her work and her home have always been separate rooms. The crossed contacts are the telling ones, and they run the other way from what the Branches of the center might lead one to expect. Her Month against his Day is Rabbit with Rat: the punishment, the care that does not know its limits, which Chapter Nine found doubled his own Hour's relation to his seat. Her working life presses on his home ground. His Month against her Day is Horse with Monkey, which have no relation at all. His working life leaves her room alone. On the Stems the direction is the same: her Month Stem is his Magistrate and his is her Owl. No two sentences in this book would be of more practical use to such a pair. The traffic between her work and their home should be watched, and the traffic between his work and their home can be trusted, which is the reverse of what a reader who knew only his chart, with its Month at war with its Day, would guess.

Speech and living. Among the Stems there are three clashes, one of them between the Day Masters themselves and two between his Month and her Year and her Hour; one combination, between the Months; and one character held in common, at the head of his chart and the head of hers. Among the Branches there is a frame at the center, completed from his Year; one punishment, between her Month and his Day; one harm, between his Year and her Month; a half of the Fire frame between his Month and her Hour; and the East complete at the edges. Heaven is noisy and earth is settled where it counts. These two will talk as the Killings and the Windfall talk, he ruling on things and she reporting and being answered for, and since both gods are the indirect ones the roles are blunt in form: he says how it is, and she says what happened, and neither softens it. That is the part each will fall into when tired. They will live together more easily than they talk, which the verse said is the bearable order of the two.

Warmth. Both charts have Fire, two presences each, and they have it differently: his is the Fire of his season and of his Wealth, the thing he pursues and cannot own; hers is herself. The warmth of this house is carried on both sides, and more of it by her, and she should know that what warms him is also what he has too much of. Of the manuals' signs of attraction there is not one between these charts. The Day Masters clash rather than combine; neither Day Master finds its combining partner anywhere in the other's eight characters; and the Peach Blossom, which the two of them share because their Day Branches share a frame, is the Rooster, which neither chart holds. One stage speaks. Her Day Master stands at its Bathing on his Hour Branch, the stage of exposure and of being seen, and it stands at its Bathing on her own Month Branch, which is the same character: what she is before him in his private world is what she is in her own season. For the rest the charts say what Chapter Eight said. This is the pull of the controlling cycle without the combination that makes the pull sweet, two that contend; and the hexagram of attraction has its own word for how such a pull is received, 君子以虚受人, by being empty, which is the ocean's nature and not the sun's. The warmth is hers to give and the receiving is his.

And the morning. He is Yang Water over the Rat, whose hour is midnight. She is Yang Fire over the Monkey, whose hour is the late afternoon. By their seats the one is at his deepest when the other's day is ending. By their Hours they were born in the same stretch of the night, an hour apart: in the old song the woman says the cock has crowed and the man says it is not yet light, and these two were born on either side of that quarrel, she in the hour before the light and he in the hour it comes. The charts do not say which of them is right. They say only that the zither and the lute are in their places.

---

## Chapter Twelve — The Reading

What follows is a reading: the thing itself, for two. The first book ended by sitting with one chart and telling it what it contained. This one ends by sitting with two, and the eleven chapters behind it are not walked again. They are gathered.

The charts. A man born in Helsinki on 29 June 1976, about 06:38 by the sun: Yang Fire over the Dragon, Yang Wood over the Horse, Yang Water over the Rat, Yin Water over the Rabbit. A woman born in Nanjing on 24 March 1980 at 04:02 by the clock of Beijing, about 03:51 by the sun, in the hour of the Tiger: Yang Metal over the Monkey, Yin Earth over the Rabbit, Yang Fire over the Monkey, Yang Metal over the Tiger.

### The order of a reading

First, since a reader will want it in one place, the order in which the questions are asked. It is the order of this book.

Cast both charts by the sun. Read each one alone, as the first book taught, until its strength and its need are known: the Useful God, the Auspicious God, and the element it already suffers from. Then, still for each alone, read the room — what the Day Branch holds, whether that is welcome, what its neighbours do to it, what stage of the Day Master's cycle it stands at. Read the star — where it is, how strong, how many, what feeds it and what strikes it, whether it is bound, buried or absent, and if absent, what does its work. Weigh the star against the self, and set star and palace side by side. Name the disease of each marriage ground and say whether its medicine is in the chart.

Only then lay the two together. Translate each chart into the other's gods and weigh it on the scale: does it bring what is needed, or what generates what is needed, or relief, or more of the excess? Do this in both directions and expect the answers to differ. Say what each Day Master is to the other. Then look at where the charts touch, the Day Pillars first, and ask of every combination what it takes hold of and what the pair leans toward, and of every clash who prevails.

Last, time. Set the two sequences of decades beside each other. Read the years for both at once, and mark the ones that speak in two charts.

Then say what is given, what it costs, and when — and stop.

### Each alone

He is the ocean with no spring. The first book read him whole: Yang Water on its own Peak and Blade, born at the crest of the Fire season, weak, giving in three directions, without a trace of Metal. His need is Metal and then Water. Marriage in his chart is abundant and heavy. The wife star commands the season and is fed from above; the self that must carry it is Imprisoned; he is the poor man in the rich house. The palace beneath him holds the one thing that keeps him standing, and it is clashed at full strength by the Branch in which the wife star lives. A fine wife, in Shen Xiaozhan's phrase, and the growing old together hard. The disease of his marriage ground is that it cannot be still. Nothing in his eight characters stills it with any strength; the Dragon that belongs to his seat's frame stands too far off, and the Rabbit at his Hour, which is one of his Nobles, punishes the seat it would protect.

She is the sun before it has risen. Yang Fire born in the hour of the Tiger, in the second month of spring, with the season on her side and not under her command: a Seal structure, Yin Wood in the Month, rooted at her Hour and her Month and nowhere on the surface, where every Stem beside her is a channel leading out, Metal at the Year and the Hour, Earth at the Month. Her need, by the first book's method, is her own element and then Wood; by the handbook's prescription for a Yang Fire of her month it is Yang Water, the god that turns her four Metals into food for her two Woods; and what she suffers from is the crossing of those Metals and Woods, Wealth ruining the Seal. Marriage in her chart is, by the first definition, the Seven Killings and only that, hidden twice, in the Monkey beneath her and the Monkey of her Year, at its own Birth in both, fed from above by her Wealth and governed from below by her Eating God, and never shown. The Hurting Officer stands in the open on her Month Stem, where the old books least wanted it, and her chart converts it: it feeds her Wealth, and her Wealth feeds the husband. Her palace is the Windfall at its head, the husband second and the Provider third, and it is clashed at full strength by her own Hour, the Tiger in its season uprooting the Monkey, in the one relation the old note says both parties lose. The disease of her marriage ground is the one she shares with him: the room cannot be still. Beside it, two more: a star that is real and never seen, and a Wealth that nourishes the star and breaks the Seal. Nothing in her eight characters quiets the room, and nothing declares the star.

Neither passes the tests the classical critics set. He is not balanced. She has no husband star in the open, and the one she has is the irregular one. Each is the chart of the lower sort for which the tradition's one rule of matching was written.

### What each is to the other

He lacks Metal, and she carries four, two of them declared. He is crushed by Fire, and she is Fire: in the Day Master itself. She needs Yang Water, and he is Yang Water, declared and rooted on its Blade; she needs her own element and what feeds it, and his chart holds two Fires and three Woods and nothing at all of the Metal she has too much of.

By the scale he is her first grade, in the Day Master itself and in bulk. She is his first grade in what she carries and his last in what she is. By the Ten Gods she is his Windfall and he is her Merciless: each is the other's spouse star in person, in the indirect form and the same charge, so that the Stems clash where the fable's combine. He is, seen from her chart, the very Stem that lies hidden in her room, walking in at the door, and in his Hour and his seat he carries three times the Direct Officer she was born without. She is, seen from his, the sun at the head of his chart, one more of the Fire he cannot lift, carrying on her two outer Stems the Metal he has never had.

It is the marriage of the fable, in which the one who controls weds the one controlled, but in the charge the fable does not marry. He gives her form and she gives him something to be answerable for. Its strength is that each is doing, with the other, what their own chart asks: he to govern a Wealth he has always had too much of, she to be forged by a pressure her chart was built to convert. Its hazard is the compendium's sentence on yang meeting yang: two that contend.

### Where they touch

At the center, two Stems at war and two Branches in one frame. 丙 against 壬 is war in heaven, which the verse says can be borne, and the old note says why: the Stems' part is to move and the Branches' to be still, and a quarrel above does little harm where the ground is quiet. The ground is the Rat and the Monkey, two Branches of the Water frame with its Peak among them, and the third member, the Dragon, is the Branch of his Year. Between the two charts the frame is whole: Birth in her Day and again in her Year, Peak in his Day, the vault in his Year. Its element is his Auspicious God, her husband star, her Useful God by the handbook, and the mediating god of her chart.

The frame is the medicine, and it is the medicine for both rooms at once. His Rat is struck by his Horse, and the old note's four stillers are a combining god, a gathering god, a vault god and a Noble; her Monkeys are gathering gods of the Rat, and they draw in his own Dragon, which is the third gathering god and the vault of the Rat's element in one. By Ren Tieqiao's test the frame takes hold of the Rat itself and turns toward the Rat's own Water, which is the right way to quiet a clash on a favorable Branch. Her Monkey is struck by her Tiger, and the same four things would quiet it; he brings two of them, the Rat on his seat and the Dragon at his head, and the frame turns her Monkey from the Metal it strikes with toward the Water her chart needs, which is the right way on either of her reckonings. The same three Branches are the prescription for his room and for hers, and they are made of both of them. He needed something that stills, and so did she, and neither had it, and each is it.

Three cautions stand against the finding, and they are the ones of Chapter Nine. The Day-Blade chapter wants no three-harmony on his seat, and her Monkeys make one; this book reads that chapter as written of a strong self's Blade, and his is the Blade that saves a weak one, and a reader who prefers the chapter to the reading stands on good ground. The first book watched a decade complete this frame for him and would not have the same claimed for a person; this book claims the smaller thing, that the frame is whole for as long as they are together. And the one Song statute that compares a husband's pillar with a wife's condemns these two from both sides, for this very frame, while the marketplace's zodiac blesses it; the book follows neither.

Across the charts, one combination: his Eating God with her Hurting Officer, at the two Months. It binds the god most dangerous to her husband star with the man's own making, and it relieves the drain the first book counted in him, and it leans toward Earth, which is his Authority and her excess, and does not turn. One character in common: the Yang Fire at the head of his chart is her Day Master, his Wealth and herself. And one Stem of his that is hers by need: the Yin Water at his Hour, her Direct Officer, which his seat and his Year hold again.

And the costs, which are six, and fall mostly on him. The Day Masters clash: two who say opposite things and want the lead in the same moment. Her Month Branch punishes his seat, the care that does not know its limits, doubling the relation his own Hour has with his room. Her Month Branch harms his Year: her season against his origins. Her Year Stem and her Hour Stem both strike his Month Stem, the Owl seizing the Food: what he makes, she cuts, from the side of her family and the side of her children, while she feeds him; it is the price of the medicine, and the first book named it before this book existed. His Month Branch and her Hour Branch lean together toward Fire, warming a season that did not need it. And the Wood of the East is complete between them, more of what drains him and of what feeds her.

A frame that quiets both rooms and is the element both want; war between the two selves above it; an exchange in which he is her medicine whole and she is his medicine in what she brings and his disease in what she is; and a price paid mostly by his making. That is the natal reading of this pair, and no part of it could have been had from the year of his birth and the year of hers.

### The arc

Two clocks running in opposite directions, his forward from the Horse month and hers backward from the Rabbit month, so that no year brings the spouse star to both in the Stem, and every year that speaks for one of them is a year whose Stem is the other's Day Master or its sister.

One Stem shared, from early 1999 to May 2006: Yin Fire, his marriage signature and her rival. One ground shared, from early 2019 to May 2026: the Pig, in which Yang Water, his Day Master and her husband, comes into its Office; her marriage and his company. A pillar of his adolescence, 丙申, that is her Day Pillar, and that the first book read blind as pressure above and supply beneath, the frame completed and something fed under the surface.

His loudest years for marriage, by the charts, from 2002 to 2007, and hers from 2010 to 2013; no year in common. The year that is her, 2016, which completed his frame and laid her palace on itself. The year that was him, 2022, which overturned her Day Pillar, Stem and Branch, in his own character.

The present year, 2026, his turning moan: the Peak of the Fire frame striking the Peak of the Water frame, while her new decade completes the Fire frame from her Hour, his Month and the Dog that is her Tomb, with the Wood that the old chapter on Water and Fire asks for standing in both charts. The decades to come, 2029 to 2036, at war with each other on the Stems, his Yang Metal against her Yang Wood, which is the price of the medicine on both sides at once; and his, 庚子, the pillar that is what she carries over the Branch she half-frames. His wife star carried to its Extinction and then its Tomb from 2029 to 2049, while her Direct Officer stands on her decade from 2036. The year that is him, 2032, his own pillar laid on his own seat, joining her room to his. And from 2049 the two clocks under one Stem, which is his, over the Tiger and the Monkey of her own clash.

### The marketplace, one last time

Chapter Two asked the reader to keep the marketplace's verdict in mind and called the rest of the book the appeal. Here is the full indictment, since by now the reader can draw it up unaided.

He was born on 壬子, which is a Lone Phoenix day, and the verse about it is as bleak as any in the lore: 黑鼠守空房 — the black Rat keeps an empty room. It is one of the Nine Ugly days besides, of which the compendium says that a man born on one comes to no good end. His seat is his Blade, of which the same book says flatly that such a man obstructs his wife. His palace falls in the Void counted from his Year, and the two Year Pillars of this pair belong to one decad, so that it falls in the Void counted from hers as well. She has no Officer in her four pillars, and the Killings is the irregular husband: 七杀是偏夫. Her Hurting Officer stands in the open: 女犯伤官，须再嫁, she must marry again. Her Wealth is four times in the chart, and the manuals wanted a woman's Wealth thin. Her Day and her Hour clash: 时日对冲，伤妻克子, which for a woman reads husband and children. The compendium's instrument of the Hour finds her husband star at its Sickness there and her child star at its Birth, which is the letter of the format called children prosper, husband hurt. The Branch of her Hour is her Red Allure, the star the compendium says a woman's chart fears most, and it is the Branch that strikes her room. Her palace is one of the four places of birth, which dread being set in motion, and it is in motion; his is struck in the striker's own season. And the contacts that join them are themselves on the list: the Day Masters clash, the old chapter on his seat forbids a Blade any three-harmony and her Monkeys make one, and the one Song statute that compares a husband's pillar with a wife's forbids him a Monkey year and forbids her his Rat and his Dragon.

Count them. There is hardly a mark of ill omen for marriage that this pair does not carry between them, and the method that reads them properly finds two people each made of what the other needs, joined by a frame that answers both rooms, with the two selves at war above it.

The appeal succeeds, then, but notice on what grounds. It is not that the marks were false. Nearly every one of them points at something real. The black Rat does keep an empty room: his palace is a house of one chamber with a rival in it, clashed and restless. Her husband star is indeed the Killings and indeed hidden; her Hour does strike her room; the Day Masters do clash. What the marketplace got wrong was not the observation. It was the inference. It saw a difficulty and wrote down a doom, and it never asked the one question the classical authors asked of every difficulty: what answers this? His restless room is answered by the frame her Monkeys complete upon it. Her hidden husband is answered by a Yang Water Day Master in the open, and her restless room by the Rat he sits on and the Dragon at his head. The clash of the selves is answered by the quiet of the ground, which is the old note's own condition for bearing it. The classical name for a mark of ill omen that has met its answer is not misfortune. It is the first couplet of the verse Zhang Nan quoted: only what has a sickness can be noble.

### Counsel

The two charts of this book are real births, and the tradition would speak to the two people. It would say what follows, and it would say it as the first book said everything: not as command, and not as prophecy, but as the terrain and the weather.

To him it would say this. The first book told him to seek Metal before the decades deliver it, in discipline, in teachers, and in people whose charts carry it. She carries four, and two of them in the open: his Useful God in a person's hands. But she is not Metal. She is Fire, the one element his chart cannot lift, and the sun at the head of his own chart has her character. He has his medicine and his disease in one woman, and the whole art of living with her is in telling the two apart: what she brings him is what restores him, and what she is to him is what he has always had too much of. The first book's counsel for the Metal decades was that the gift is delivered by cutting his Output, and her two Yang Metals do to his Eating God exactly what the first book said the decade of 2029 would do. He should expect what he makes to narrow in her company, as it will narrow in those years, and should know it for the same medicine and not for a wound.

Guard the seat, the first book said. With her he knows what the seat rests on. The frame that quiets his Rat is made of her two Monkeys and his own Dragon, and it is whole for as long as they are together; his medicine for the room is her room, and hers is his. The Horse is his season, his work, the Fire he pursues, and it strikes his seat in the year of this writing with the whole Fire frame behind it, her Hour and her new decade included. The compendium wrote one exception to the Blade's saying, and it wrote it for his Day Master: Yang Water meeting its Blade in the clash of Rat and Horse, for the most part no harm. He may take that comfort. He should not take it for permission.

He is her Killings, and the Killings is pressure, not law. Her chart was built to turn pressure into standing, through her Wealth and her Output, which in a house are her competence and her speech, and the compendium's rule for her structure is that the Seal which meets the Killings flourishes: in measure. Beyond measure the Merciless crushes rather than forges. The excess of his office is fault-finding. He should know that what she says is, in his chart, the Magistrate, and hear it as law and not as the Hurting Officer the old books feared; and he should know that her working life presses on his home ground by the Branches, and that his leaves hers alone, and should not hold against her an asymmetry that the charts made.

And he should know the decades. From 2029 his own decade is the pillar that is what she carries, and it stands at war on the Stems with hers; the thing that will supply him is, in her chart, the enemy of what restores her. In the same twenty years his wife star passes through the two stages the rhapsodies name for the parting of the paired birds. The sources measure the star's availability by that and nothing more, and the book has refused to make a sentence of it; but a man whose star is at its Extinction should not read his wife by his star's weather. He should read her by her own chart, in which those are the years the Direct Officer stands on her decade for the first time in her life.

To her it would say this. Every old book condemned her five times before it had read her, and the method finds a husband star that is real, rooted, fed, governed and never shown, waiting for a Yang Water in the open. He is that Stem. He is also the Killings, and she should expect to be pressed by him rather than ruled, and should know that her chart converts the pressure through what she manages and what she says. Her Hurting Officer is, to him, the law. She should say what she sees. What she does not say to him goes into Earth, and the Earth in her chart is already four deep.

The crossing in her own chart, Wealth against Seal, is bridged by Water, and in this pairing the Water is him. The balance of her eight characters runs, while they are together, through his Day Master. That is circulation and not dependence, and she should know the difference, because her chart will tell her the opposite in the years when Water is struck. In the Fire years, which are her own years, the god that bridges her chart stands in a clash with the Stem that is herself; she will feel strongest in exactly the years the pass is narrowest.

Her room is struck by her own Hour: her children, her private world, her later years, against her marriage, the one domain of her life in which her own Fire is born and her husband star is weakest. His Rat and his Dragon are what gather it, and they gather it by being where they are. In the Tiger years the room is moved, and 2022 was such a year, and the Stem of it was him. She should know that the weather of her forties fell on her in his character and was not his doing.

Her decade from 2026 is the Owl over her Tomb. In it the Wood that restores her and the Metal she carries fight in the open, her Month's Hurting Officer is bound, her Seal is tied toward Fire, and her chart is flooded with her own element through his Horse. It will be a hot decade in a cool self, and the warmth of the house is hers to carry in it. From 2029 his decade's Stem strikes hers, and his supply will read in her chart as pressure on what restores her; she should not take that as a judgment on the marriage, because it is the same clash the natal charts carry, reversed. And from 2036 the Direct Officer stands on her decade, the husband of the fable at last, while the Rooster beneath it strikes her Seal. She should not mistake the Officer that arrives by time for the husband she has by function. The one is a decade and the other is a person.

To both it would say that the purse is hers, and that the division of the house is not the one in which one makes and the other manages but the one in which what he makes restores her and what she says governs him, and that they should notice the one they already have. That the two private worlds lean the same way, and the two families fit through his seat, so that the ends of this household need less courtesy than most and the center needs more. That the calendar will never give them a year that is good for both in the Stem, and will give them instead, over and over, a year that is one of them standing in the other's chart: the Horse years his, the Tiger years hers, the Dog years hot for both, and the Monkey, Rat and Dragon years the years of their frame. A couple who learn to read each year as the other's weather have most of what Chapter Ten can give them.

And it would end with the hexagram that the old note on Water and Fire names for a Water Stem over a Fire Branch, which is what these two are when the cosmology sets the husband over the wife: 既济, Already Across. Its judgment is 初吉终乱, fortunate at the start and disordered at the end, and the commentary says why: 终止则乱，其道穷也, when one stops, disorder, because the way has run out. Its image is the sentence the tradition would leave with them. 水在火上，既济；君子以思患而豫防之. Water above Fire: Already Across; the noble one thinks of trouble and guards against it beforehand. A pair whose charts hold each other's medicine is already across. The hexagram's whole counsel is not to stop rowing.

### What the reading leaves

The reading ends where the first book's ended, with the thing it cannot do. It does not command. It has said what each of these two is to the other, where they join and what the joining costs, and what the weather will be. It has not said whether they should have married, or should stay, or how. It has not said whether the one who wakes first is kind. Chen Su'an, who knew as much about charts as anyone who has written on them, stopped at exactly this line: choose by the ordinary principles, take the one whose chart is balanced — or, the tradition added, the one whose imbalance answers yours — and what comes after the wedding, good or ill, may be left to Heaven.

Left to Heaven is not the same as left to chance. The 中庸 says that the way of the exemplary person has its first beginning among husbands and wives, and that at its furthest reach it is displayed in heaven and earth. The same book gives the word this whole canon has been circling since the first chapter of the first book, and says what follows when it is achieved:

> 致中和，天地位焉，万物育焉。
>
> Let central harmony be reached, and heaven and earth take their places, and the ten thousand things are nourished.

A single chart seeks that harmony across a life and rarely holds it for long. The first book was the story of one man seeking it. The claim of this book is only that there is a second way it can be approached, and that it is the oldest way: not alone. Eight characters lean. Sixteen, if they are the right sixteen, can lean against each other and stand.

The Odes say the rest in eight characters of their own, and they are the last this book will quote. 执子之手，与子偕老. I take your hand in mine. I will grow old with you.

---

# Part Two — The Reference

The *tables* — the same doctrine laid out for looking things up. Part One read two charts from beginning to end. Part Two is for every other pair. It is written in the manner of the companion reference: entry by entry, in plain letters, speaking to the person whose chart it is. Each table answers one question. Who is the spouse in my chart? What kind of ground is my marriage built on? What is this other person to me, and I to them? What do our two rooms do to each other? What does a year do to us both?

Three rules govern everything here, and they are Part One's. A star, a palace or a partner is not good or bad by its name: it is welcome if it is what your chart needs and unwelcome if it is what your chart already has too much of, so every entry must be read with your own strength and your own Useful God in hand. Nothing in a second chart repairs a first chart that cannot carry a marriage. And every relation between two charts runs in both directions, so look up the pair from your side and then from theirs.

One caution belongs to the whole of it. The classical books describe combination, clash, punishment and harm inside a single chart and between a chart and its years. Wherever a table below reads one of those relations between the charts of two people, the application is this book's, made on the grounds Part One gives, and the tables do not repeat the warning at each entry.

---

## The Spouse Star (10×2)

The *relation* — which Stem stands for the spouse in my chart?

For a man the spouse star is the element his Day Master controls: the opposite-polarity Stem is the wife star proper, Earned Fortune (Direct Wealth), and the same-polarity Stem is its secondary, Windfall (Indirect Wealth). For a woman it is the element that controls her Day Master: the opposite-polarity Stem is the husband star proper, the Magistrate (Direct Officer), and the same-polarity Stem is its secondary, the Merciless (Seven Killings). Whichever of the two is present, alone, serves. Both together divide the bond. Neither present, the spouse is read by function: the god that brings the chart to balance.

The combining partner of a yang Day Master is its Earned Fortune; the combining partner of a yin Day Master is its Magistrate. So a yang-Stem man and a yin-Stem woman are drawn by combination toward the spouse star itself, and a yin-Stem man and a yang-Stem woman are not.

---

### **甲 Jia — Yang Wood**

The tree. What you govern is the ground you root in, and what governs you is the edge that shapes you.

**As a man:** Your wife star is 己 Ji (Yin Earth), your Earned Fortune; its secondary is 戊 Wu (Yang Earth), your Windfall. Ji is the moist field the tree stands in — the ground that receives your roots and is held together by them. Marriage for you is rootedness: the thing you grow out of and are answerable for. Wu is the dry mountain, harder to enter and harder to hold. Fire in your chart feeds this star; other Wood competes with you for the same soil.

**As a woman:** Your husband star is 辛 Xin (Yin Metal), your Magistrate; its secondary is 庚 Geng (Yang Metal), the Merciless. Xin is the pruning knife, the fine edge that takes the tree's wild growth and makes of it something of use. Geng is the axe, and it clashes with you. Marriage for you is growth meeting an edge, and the question is always whether the edge prunes or fells. Earth in your chart feeds this star; Fire, your own brilliance, is what wounds it; Water drains it into you.

**The combining partner:** 己 Ji. For a man of this Stem the partner and the wife star are one. For a woman the partner is her own Earned Fortune, and the husband star stands apart from the pull.

---

### **乙 Yi — Yin Wood**

The vine. What you govern is what you climb and cover, and what governs you is the blade.

**As a man:** Your wife star is 戊 Wu (Yang Earth), your Earned Fortune; its secondary is 己 Ji (Yin Earth), your Windfall. Wu is the wall and the mountainside: the large, still thing your flexibility takes hold of and softens. Marriage for you is attachment to something more massive than yourself. Ji is garden soil, closer and easier, and less of a support. Fire feeds this star; other Wood crowds the same wall.

**As a woman:** Your husband star is 庚 Geng (Yang Metal), your Magistrate; its secondary is 辛 Xin (Yin Metal), the Merciless. Geng is the sword and the trellis iron: the hard thing a soft thing binds itself to. The meeting is the most famous of the five combinations — humaneness and duty, the yielding and the unyielding each supplying the other. Xin is the scissors, and it clashes with you outright. Earth feeds this star; Fire wounds it.

**The combining partner:** 庚 Geng. For a woman of this Stem the partner and the husband star are one. For a man the partner is his own Magistrate, and the wife star stands apart from the pull.

---

### **丙 Bing — Yang Fire**

The sun. What you govern is what you illuminate, and what governs you is what can veil you.

**As a man:** Your wife star is 辛 Xin (Yin Metal), your Earned Fortune; its secondary is 庚 Geng (Yang Metal), your Windfall. Xin is the jewel: it has no light of its own and shows every facet in yours. Marriage for you is having something precious to shine on. Geng is raw ore, which asks to be smelted and resists. Earth feeds this star; other Fire competes for the same reflection.

**As a woman:** Your husband star is 癸 Gui (Yin Water), your Magistrate; its secondary is 壬 Ren (Yang Water), the Merciless. Gui is cloud and rain: the one thing that can soften a sun, and the thing that turns its heat into growth. Ren is the ocean, which clashes with you and can swallow the light whole. Marriage for you is sun meeting rain, which is the only way a sun makes anything grow. Metal feeds this star; Earth, your own output, dams it.

**The combining partner:** 辛 Xin. For a man of this Stem the partner and the wife star are one. For a woman the partner is her own Earned Fortune.

---

### **丁 Ding — Yin Fire**

The lamp, the forge. What you govern is what you work upon, and what governs you is the deep water.

**As a man:** Your wife star is 庚 Geng (Yang Metal), your Earned Fortune; its secondary is 辛 Xin (Yin Metal), your Windfall. Geng is ore, and you are the forge fire that makes it a blade: the classical line is that Metal in its vigor must get Fire before it becomes a vessel. Marriage for you is a work in progress that needs your steady heat. Xin is a finished jewel, which a flame can only mar. Earth feeds this star; other Fire shares the forge.

**As a woman:** Your husband star is 壬 Ren (Yang Water), your Magistrate; its secondary is 癸 Gui (Yin Water), the Merciless. Ren is the ocean, and you are the lamp upon it: the vast thing that could put you out and instead takes its direction from you. Gui is the rain that does put a candle out, and it clashes with you. Metal feeds this star; Earth dams it.

**The combining partner:** 壬 Ren. For a woman of this Stem the partner and the husband star are one. For a man the partner is his own Magistrate.

---

### **戊 Wu — Yang Earth**

The mountain. What you govern is the water you gather, and what governs you is what takes root in you.

**As a man:** Your wife star is 癸 Gui (Yin Water), your Earned Fortune; its secondary is 壬 Ren (Yang Water), your Windfall. Gui is mist and spring water: it comes to the mountain of its own accord and the mountain gives it a course. The old books called this pair the combination without feeling, an old husband and a young wife; read without the gossip it is stillness holding what moves. Ren is the great river, which a mountain can dam but not keep. Metal feeds this star; other Earth divides the water.

**As a woman:** Your husband star is 乙 Yi (Yin Wood), your Magistrate; its secondary is 甲 Jia (Yang Wood), the Merciless. Yi is the green that covers the slope and holds its soil; Jia is the tree whose roots split rock. Marriage for you is what turns a mountain from rock into a living slope. Water feeds this star; Metal, your own output, cuts it.

**The combining partner:** 癸 Gui. For a man of this Stem the partner and the wife star are one. For a woman the partner is her own Earned Fortune.

---

### **己 Ji — Yin Earth**

The field. What you govern is the water that crosses you, and what governs you is what grows from you.

**As a man:** Your wife star is 壬 Ren (Yang Water), your Earned Fortune; its secondary is 癸 Gui (Yin Water), your Windfall. Ren is the river that waters a field and, in flood, carries it away: a wife star larger than the self that governs it. Marriage for you is channeling something powerful without being drowned. Gui is rain, gentler and less sure. Metal feeds this star; other Earth lays claim to the same water.

**As a woman:** Your husband star is 甲 Jia (Yang Wood), your Magistrate; its secondary is 乙 Yi (Yin Wood), the Merciless. Jia is the tree that roots in the field and gives it a purpose; the old books called the pair the combination of the centered and upright. Yi is the crop that takes without thanks. Water feeds this star; Metal cuts it.

**The combining partner:** 甲 Jia. For a woman of this Stem the partner and the husband star are one. For a man the partner is his own Magistrate.

---

### **庚 Geng — Yang Metal**

The blade. What you govern is what you cut and shape, and what governs you is the fire that forged you.

**As a man:** Your wife star is 乙 Yi (Yin Wood), your Earned Fortune; its secondary is 甲 Jia (Yang Wood), your Windfall. Yi is the vine that winds around the sword: the one soft thing a hard nature does not cut. Marriage for you is where the edge is laid down. Jia is timber, which you clash with and fell. Water feeds this star; other Metal competes to cut it.

**As a woman:** Your husband star is 丁 Ding (Yin Fire), your Magistrate; its secondary is 丙 Bing (Yang Fire), the Merciless. Ding is the forge fire, the patient heat that makes ore into an instrument: no Stem is more improved by its husband star than this one. Bing is the sun, heat without a smith. Wood feeds this star; Water, your own output, quenches it.

**The combining partner:** 乙 Yi. For a man of this Stem the partner and the wife star are one. For a woman the partner is her own Earned Fortune.

---

### **辛 Xin — Yin Metal**

The jewel. What you govern is what you carve, and what governs you is the light that shows you.

**As a man:** Your wife star is 甲 Jia (Yang Wood), your Earned Fortune; its secondary is 乙 Yi (Yin Wood), your Windfall. Jia is the great tree and the fine blade's proper work: something large made exact. Marriage for you is a thing to be refined, and your standards will be felt. Yi is the flower, which you clash with and bruise. Water feeds this star; other Metal crowds it.

**As a woman:** Your husband star is 丙 Bing (Yang Fire), your Magistrate; its secondary is 丁 Ding (Yin Fire), the Merciless. Bing is the sun, in whose light alone a jewel is seen for what it is; the old books called the pair the combination of awe and command. Ding is the flame held too close, which can damage what it means to show. Marriage for you is being seen. Wood feeds this star; Water, your own output, dims it.

**The combining partner:** 丙 Bing. For a woman of this Stem the partner and the husband star are one. For a man the partner is his own Magistrate.

---

### **壬 Ren — Yang Water**

The ocean. What you govern is the fire you could put out, and what governs you is the bank.

**As a man:** Your wife star is 丁 Ding (Yin Fire), your Earned Fortune; its secondary is 丙 Bing (Yang Fire), your Windfall. Ding is the lamp on the water — in the companion reference's words, the small candle the ocean could easily extinguish and which gives it direction. Marriage for you is the one fixed light in a boundless nature. Bing is the sun over the sea, which you clash with and reflect. Wood feeds this star; other Water threatens to drown it.

**As a woman:** Your husband star is 己 Ji (Yin Earth), your Magistrate; its secondary is 戊 Wu (Yang Earth), the Merciless. Ji is the soft bank and the silt: it gives the water an edge and clouds it a little in doing so. Wu is the dam. Marriage for you is finding a shore: the line at which something boundless can be met. Fire feeds this star; Wood, your own output, breaks it up.

**The combining partner:** 丁 Ding. For a man of this Stem the partner and the wife star are one. For a woman the partner is her own Earned Fortune.

---

### **癸 Gui — Yin Water**

The rain. What you govern is the sun you veil, and what governs you is the mountain.

**As a man:** Your wife star is 丙 Bing (Yang Fire), your Earned Fortune; its secondary is 丁 Ding (Yin Fire), your Windfall. Bing is the sun, and rain with sun is the rainbow: a wife star far brighter than the self that governs it, which a quiet nature tempers without ever outshining. Ding is the candle in the rain, and the two clash. Wood feeds this star; other Water overcasts it.

**As a woman:** Your husband star is 戊 Wu (Yang Earth), your Magistrate; its secondary is 己 Ji (Yin Earth), the Merciless. Wu is the mountain that gathers scattered rain into a stream with a name. The old books thought the pair cold; what it has in place of heat is shelter. Ji is the mud that rain becomes when the ground simply absorbs it. Fire feeds this star; Wood breaks it.

**The combining partner:** 戊 Wu. For a woman of this Stem the partner and the husband star are one. For a man the partner is his own Magistrate.

---

## The Ten Gods in the Spouse Palace (10×2)

The *ground* — what lives in the room where my marriage must live?

The spouse palace is the Day Branch, and its principal hidden Stem stands in one of the ten relations to the Day Master. That god is not a portrait of the spouse. It is what you expect of intimacy, what you bring into the room, and what the room does to whoever enters it. Because a Day Stem and its Branch share a polarity, the same-polarity gods head the palace in eight of the sixty Day Pillars each, and the opposite-polarity gods in only four each — the four pillars seated on Rat, Horse, Snake or Pig.

Each entry gives the ground, then the two readings that Shen Xiaozhan's rule requires: when what the palace holds is what the chart needs, and when it is not.

---

### **The Equal (Companion) — *your own Stem beneath you***

**Day Pillars:** 甲寅 乙卯 戊辰 戊戌 己丑 己未 庚申 辛酉.

You sit on yourself. The intimate ground is made of exactly what you are made of, and you expect a partner to be a second you: same pace, same reflexes, no translation needed. The old warning for these seats is that Stem and Branch alike lessen the wealth and hurt the wife, because your own kind is the natural rival of a man's wife star.

**When it is welcome:** A weak Day Master has here the one ally who lives in the house. The marriage ground is where you are most yourself and most supported, and a partner who meets you as an equal steadies everything else.

**When it is not:** A strong Day Master has too much self at home. There is no room in the room. For a man with thin Wealth this is the star robbed where it should be safest; for a woman it is a household with two heads of it.

---

### **The Rival (Rob Wealth) — *your element, the other polarity***

**Day Pillars:** 丙午 丁巳 壬子 癸亥.

All four are seats of the Day Master's own Peak, and two of the three Blade days are among them. The ground is your element at full strength and differently charged: close enough to understand you completely, different enough to want what you have. You expect a companion and get, along with one, a contest about what is whose.

**When it is welcome:** For a weak Day Master under heavy Wealth or Authority, this is the saving of the chart, and by the classical ruling such a spouse is one who sustains. The marriage ground is the only place you are given anything.

**When it is not:** For a strong Day Master it is the Blade unsheathed at home: generosity that tips into dispossession, intensity that leaves no equal standing. The old books' verdicts on these days are their harshest.

---

### **The Provider (Eating God) — *what you make, your polarity***

**Day Pillars:** 丙辰 丙戌 丁丑 丁未 戊申 己酉 壬寅 癸卯.

You sit on what flows out of you. Home is where you give: you cook, host, make, amuse, and expect to be enjoyed. The ground is pleasant and it costs you energy every day.

**When it is welcome:** A strong Day Master has here its natural outlet, and a man's Eating God feeds his wife star from beneath. The house is generous and easy, and the partner is the one for whom the best of you is made.

**When it is not:** A weak Day Master is giving even to its own foundation. The intimate life drains where it should restore. For a woman whose chart keeps an Officer, this is the child star lodged in the husband's room.

---

### **The Underminer (Hurting Officer) — *what you make, the other polarity***

**Day Pillars:** 甲午 乙巳 庚子 辛亥.

You sit on your sharpest faculty. At home you are brilliant, restless and unsparing; the partner hears what no one else hears, including what would have been better unsaid. Two of these seats are the Day Master's Bathing and two its Death: exposure, or exhaustion.

**When it is welcome:** In a chart built on Wealth it feeds the Wealth; in a chart pressed by the Killings it restrains them; and a strong self with nowhere to go has here its release. Then the partner is the one person before whom the whole intelligence can be used.

**When it is not:** In a woman's chart with a weak Officer it is the wounding god in the husband's own palace. In anyone weak it is a tongue with no strength behind it. The household lives under a commentary.

---

### **Windfall (Indirect Wealth) — *what you govern, your polarity***

**Day Pillars:** 甲辰 甲戌 乙丑 乙未 丙申 丁酉 庚寅 辛卯.

You sit on what you control, in its bolder form. Home is something you run, provide for and take charge of, and you are drawn to a partner with wider horizons than your own.

**When it is welcome:** A strong Day Master is fitted to this seat: authority exercised over one's own ground, to the benefit of everyone on it.

**When it is not:** A weak Day Master is seated on a responsibility it cannot lift; a chart that depends on its Resource has the breaker of Resource at home.

**For a man:** The wife star keeps the wife's palace, in its unconventional form. The bond is close and the marriage has more voltage than rails.

---

### **Earned Fortune (Direct Wealth) — *what you govern, the other polarity***

**Day Pillars:** 戊子 己亥 壬午 癸巳.

Only these four pillars seat a Day Master on its Direct Wealth, and in all four the self is at its Embryo: just conceived, sitting on a field already ripe. Home is orderly, practical and well kept, and it asks to be earned.

**When it is welcome:** For a strong Day Master, steady resources and a steady house; the classical line promises a household and a fortune made through the spouse.

**When it is not:** For a weak one, the daily demand of maintaining what one sits on. The poor man in the rich house lives at this address.

**For a man:** The wife star proper is at home in its own palace, and in 戊子 and 壬午 it is also the Stem your Day Master combines with. The classical gloss on the wife star keeping the wife's palace is that, unclashed, it promises a spouse to rival the paragon of the old stories. No seat holds its marriage more tightly, and sometimes it is held too tightly.

---

### **The Merciless (Seven Killings) — *what governs you, your polarity***

**Day Pillars:** 甲申 乙酉 戊寅 己卯 壬辰 壬戌 癸丑 癸未.

You sit on pressure. The place that should be most restful is where you are held to account, and you choose partners who do not let you off. Four of these seats are vaults and two are the Day Master's Extinction.

**When it is welcome:** A strong Day Master with nothing to push against is made by this seat: discipline at home, a partner who is a worthy opponent, energy given form.

**When it is not:** A weak Day Master is pressed in its own foundation. The intimate life is a campaign.

**For a woman:** The husband star keeps the husband's palace, in its fiercer form. Restrained by an Eating God or converted by Resource, it is one of the noble patterns; unrestrained, it is the bond as an ordeal.

---

### **The Magistrate (Direct Officer) — *what governs you, the other polarity***

**Day Pillars:** 丙子 丁亥 庚午 辛巳.

Only these four. Home is where the rule lives: propriety, standards, a sense that someone is watching whose good opinion matters. In 丁亥 and 辛巳 the god in the palace is also the Stem the Day Master combines with.

**When it is welcome:** For a strong Day Master, the house is where it becomes useful to the world. The partner's judgment has consequences and deserves to.

**When it is not:** For a weak one, the standard is always a little higher than can be met. In a chart built on the Hurting Officer, the enemy is in the bedroom.

**For a woman:** The husband star proper is at home in its own palace, and the bond has the closeness that the man's seat on his Earned Fortune has: the relation of marriage and the ground of daily life are one thing.

---

### **Owl Spirit (Indirect Resource) — *what generates you, your polarity***

**Day Pillars:** 丙寅 丁卯 庚辰 庚戌 辛丑 辛未 壬申 癸酉.

You sit on nourishment that comes on its own terms. Home feeds you and rearranges you; you expect to be looked after and are, at a price. It is a private, self-sufficient ground, and partners can find it hard to get in.

**When it is welcome:** A weak Day Master is fed from below every day of its life, and a chart pressed by the Killings has its converter at home.

**When it is not:** A chart that lives through its Output has here the god that seizes it. The intimate ground withholds, and the best of you is dammed where it should flow. For a woman, heaped Resource steals the breath of the husband star.

---

### **The True Patron (Direct Resource) — *what generates you, the other polarity***

**Day Pillars:** 甲子 乙亥 戊午 己巳.

Only these four. Home is unconditional support: the place where you are held simply because holding you is what it does. Two of these seats are the Day Master's Bathing or Death, two its Peak: the support is tender in some and total in others.

**When it is welcome:** For a weak Day Master there is no better ground. The partner is rest.

**When it is not:** For a strong one, comfort without a reason to move: a marriage that is a nursery. A man over-supplied here will be slow to become a husband, and a strong woman here needs her partner for little.

---

## Marked Day Pillars

The *marks* — is there anything particular about the pillar I was born on?

Some of the sixty Day Pillars carry names. They are of two kinds and must be held differently. The first kind follows from the grammar: the name is shorthand for a fact about what the Day Master sits on, and it is read the way any seat is read. The second kind is lore: a list of days handed down with a verdict and no reasoning. The classical critics kept the first kind and dissolved the second into it, and so does this table. Under every name, the question is still the one the last table asked: what does this seat hold, and does this chart want it?

---

### **Seated on the Blade (日刃)**

**Day Pillars:** 丙午 戊午 壬子.

**The rule:** A yang Day Master's Blade is the Branch of its Emperor's Peak, and only these three pillars put a yang Stem directly on it. In 丙午 and 壬子 the self stands on its own element at the most forceful point of its cycle. 戊午 is counted with them because Earth keeps the calendar of Fire, though what its Horse chiefly holds is the Resource of Earth and not Earth itself.

**The lore:** The old verse is as bleak as any in the literature: the red and the yellow Horse lie down alone, and the black Rat keeps an empty room. The compendium glosses it without mercy — the man obstructs his wife, the woman her husband — and lists the same three days again among the Lone Phoenix.

**The reading:** Shen Xiaozhan's ruling is the one to keep. A strong Day Master on its Blade has too much of itself at home, and the old verdicts apply to it. A weak Day Master, in a chart built on Wealth, Officer, Killings or Hurting Officer, depends on this seat for everything, and its spouse is the one who sustains. The same pillar is the ruin of one chart and the saving of another.

**Between two charts:** A strong Blade seat is steadied by a partner whose chart is, to it, Authority or Output: something to answer to, or somewhere to go. A weak one needs only that the seat be left in peace. Either way the hardest contact a Blade seat can meet is the Branch that clashes it in the partner's palace: Rat against the Horse days, Horse against the Rat day.

**On combination the books are divided:** The old manual's chapter on these three pillars says that a Day Blade welcomes neither clash nor combination, and that a combination arriving among the pillars means strange calamity; the compendium names the three-harmony and the six. Against that stand a maxim, that a Blade dreads the clash and is glad of the combination, which Zhang Nan reads of a Blade combined with the Seven Killings; and the compendium's own remark that what is said in dread of a Blade is said of a strong self, and what is said in its favor of a weak one. Where the self is strong, keep to the chapter. Where the self is weak and the Blade is its root, weigh a combination by what the pair leans toward: a frame that turns the Blade toward its own element feeds the root, and a six-harmony that turns it elsewhere quiets the seat and tethers it.

---
