### **Seated on the Office (专禄)**

**Day Pillars:** 甲寅 乙卯 庚申 辛酉.

**The rule:** The Day Master sits on the Branch of its own Office, the place the old books call its emolument. Stem and Branch are one element, and the principal hidden Stem is the Day Master itself.

**The lore:** These are the purest cases of the oldest warning about a seat: Stem and Branch alike, the wealth is lessened and the wife is hurt. 甲寅 also stands in the Lone Phoenix list.

**The reading:** It is the Equal in the spouse palace at working strength: a self that is entirely itself at home. For a weak chart it is root and rest. For a strong one it is a house with no room in it. And the lore should be set against the compendium's own practice, because several of its model charts of contented wives are of exactly these days — the quiet woman who keeps to her part sits on 乙卯, and the woman of blessing and long life on 辛酉 — and in each the commentary praises the seat: the self vigorous in its own right, beside a husband star that is vigorous too.

---

### **The Bound Seat**

**Day Pillars:** 戊子 壬午 丁亥 辛巳, where the Stem that combines with the Day Master is the principal Stem of its own Branch; 甲午 丙戌 庚辰 乙巳 己亥, where it is the secondary; 戊辰 壬戌 癸巳, where it is the residual.

**The rule:** The attraction of the Five Combinations is present beneath the Day Master, between the self and something hidden in its own ground. The companion reference marks six of these pillars; the same logic finds the bond, more faintly, in the other six.

**The lore:** The compendium knows the same six — 壬午 丁亥 戊子 甲午 辛巳 癸巳 — by a name of its own, self-transformation beneath the seat (座下自化), and thought well of most of them: 壬午 and 丁亥 the most deeply blessed, 戊子 clever, 辛巳 resourceful, 甲午 a lesser good fortune, and 癸巳 honored, with a weakness for wine and for love.

**The reading:** For the yang Day Masters — 戊子 壬午 甲午 丙戌 庚辰 戊辰 壬戌 — the hidden partner is Earned Fortune. A man of these days has his wife star in his palace and is tied to it by the combination itself; a woman of these days is bound to her own Wealth, her competence and her stewardship. For the yin Day Masters — 丁亥 辛巳 乙巳 己亥 癸巳 — the hidden partner is the Magistrate. A woman of these days has her husband star in her palace on the same terms; a man of these days is bound to his own duty. Where the partner is the principal Stem the bond governs the palace. Where it is a lesser Stem it is an undertone.

**The caution:** What is bound is also held. No seat keeps its marriage closer, and none finds it harder to let the partner be a separate person. A Day Master already tied to what it sits on has less attention left for what its chart needs.

---

### **The Star in Storage**

**Day Pillars:** For a man, 丁丑 戊辰 辛未 壬戌: the Day Master sits on the vault of the element it controls. For a woman, 乙丑 丙辰 己未 庚戌: the Day Master sits on the vault of the element that controls it.

**The rule:** The four storage Branches seal up one element each: the Ox Metal, the Dragon Water, the Goat Wood, the Dog Fire. In these eight pillars the element sealed beneath the Day Master is the spouse star's. Earth, which the life stages lodge with Fire, has no vault of its own, so Day Masters whose spouse star is Earth do not appear here.

**The lore:** Two lines, and they contradict each other usefully. A Wealth star entered into the tomb is a wife surely harmed. A Wealth star in the storehouse is a thrifty wife who guards the purse.

**The reading:** What is stored is preserved and withheld at once. The marriage is late, or guarded, or held in reserve; and by the common doctrine it is opened by the Branch that clashes the vault — Goat for the Ox, Dog for the Dragon, Ox for the Goat, Dragon for the Dog — arriving in a year, a decade, or a partner's palace. The compendium says outright that a clash to a vault holding what the chart needs is especially welcome, and the old note to Di Tian Sui (滴天髓) says the four vaults want to be clashed open and must not be shut.

**On combination:** The old note to Di Tian Sui holds that the four vaults must never be shut, and Ren Tieqiao that a combination is what shuts them: the Branch in six-harmony with the vault — Rat for the Ox, Rooster for the Dragon, Horse for the Goat, Rabbit for the Dog — joins it to something and holds its contents where they are. This canon follows that ruling. The companion reference had counted a combination, with a clash, among the things that open a vault, and is to be corrected to agree; a reader using the two texts together should know which way the correction runs. Ask what the vault stores before deciding whether you want it opened or kept.

**The critics:** The doctrine of the opening clash had its doubters, and they were not minor ones. Shen Xiaozhan called the rule that nothing in a vault prospers until it is clashed a saying of the vulgar books, unknown to the founder of the method. A clash between two vaults, he says, stirs the Earth they are chiefly made of: it is of use where Earth is the thing wanted, and where the thing wanted is the element in storage it does that element harm. Ren Tieqiao called the rule mistaken.

---

### **The Lone Phoenix Days (孤鸾)**

**Day Pillars:** 乙巳 丁巳 辛亥 戊申 甲寅, and in the longer list also 丙午 戊午 壬子.

**The lore:** An old song gives the days in riddles — the Wood and Fire snakes, the Metal pig, the Earth monkey, the Wood tiger — and the compendium supplies the verdict: the man overcomes his wife, the woman her husband. The older manual adds that those born on these days are unlucky in marrying: they marry without ceremony, or through relatives, or as a second spouse.

**The critics:** Zhang Nan took the days apart one at a time, and what he did is the model for handling every mark of this kind. A woman of 乙巳 sits on her own husband star. A woman of 丁巳 sits on Wealth, which generates the husband: women born on these two days, he says, mostly prosper husband and children. A woman of 辛亥 sits on her Earned Fortune, and Wealth too can stand for the husband — how can she be said to have no man? A woman of 戊申 sits on the Eating God that cuts her husband star, and that one he grants. A woman of 甲寅 sits where her husband star is extinct, and that one he grants. The star dissolves. What is left is five ordinary seats, three of them good.

**The use:** Read the seat by the last table and forget the name. The compendium's own rhyme allows as much: in a chart where Officer and Resource are in accord, such small stars are no sickness.

---

### **The Days of Yin and Yang Mismatched (阴阳差错)**

**Day Pillars:** 丙子 丁丑 戊寅 辛卯 壬辰 癸巳 丙午 丁未 戊申 辛酉 壬戌 癸亥.

**The lore:** The compendium's account of these twelve is not about the spouse at all. A woman born on one of them has little accord with her husband's parents and his brothers' wives, and her husband's house grows cold and declines. A man born on one finds his wife's family receding from him and falls into disputes with it. The mark is counted heavier where it falls on two or three of the Month, Day and Hour, and on the Day it is counted heavy alone.

**The critics:** No reason is given for the list anywhere in the classical books, and it comes under Chen Su'an's general ruling: character and conduct are to be judged from the five phases, and one must not be bound by the stars the old books invented.

**The use:** At most a reminder to look where the two families actually meet: at the Year Pillars of the two charts, and at the gods that stand for the other family in each.

---

### **The Eight Single Days (八专)**

**Day Pillars:** 甲寅 乙卯 己未 丁未 庚申 辛酉 戊戌 癸丑.

**The lore:** The compendium quotes an old line that blames a life of pleasure on these days, calls them a star of desire, and says that with one on the Day there is an irregular wife and with one on the Hour an irregular child. Elsewhere the same book praises four of them, 甲寅 乙卯 庚申 辛酉, as the purest seats in the cycle: clever, long-lived, seldom ill, and fond of wine and love.

**The reading:** In six of the eight the Day Master sits on its own element, and in the other two, 丁未 and 癸丑, its own Stem lies hidden in the Branch as its second tenant, beneath a Yin Earth that is the Eating God to 丁 and the Seven Killings to 癸. Four of the eight are the seats of the Office above. The mark is the old warning about Stem and Branch alike given a moral color. For the six, read the seat as the Equal in the spouse palace; for 丁未 and 癸丑, read it by its head tenant, with the Equal as the lesser one; and leave the color out.

---

### **The Nine Ugly Days (九丑)**

**Day Pillars:** 壬子 壬午 戊子 戊午 己酉 己卯 乙卯 辛酉 辛卯.

**The lore:** The compendium calls them days of obstruction. A woman born on one, it says, is in danger in childbirth, and a man born on one comes to disgrace and no good end.

**The reading:** All nine stand on the Rat, the Horse, the Rabbit or the Rooster: the four cardinal Branches, which are also the four Peach Blossom Branches. It is the Peach Blossom lore fastened to particular seats, and no reason is given for the choice of nine. Several of them are among the best-regarded seats in this table. Read the seat and forget the name.

---

### **The Kui Gang Days (魁罡)**

**Day Pillars:** 壬辰 庚戌 戊戌 庚辰.

**The lore:** The old manual's account is of character and fortune: clever, decisive, severe, and fond of killing; great where the day is repeated and the self is strong, and ruined at a stroke where Wealth and Officer appear. Its chapter says nothing of marriage. A gloss in Zhang Nan's book adds that a woman seated on the Dragon or the Dog, with the Kui Gang complete and punished and clashed besides, harms husband and children; the handbooks have made of that a rule for every woman born on these days.

**The reading:** Four yang Day Masters on the Dragon or the Dog, the two vaults that clash each other. 壬辰 sits on its Seven Killings and on the Tomb of its own element; 庚辰 and 庚戌 sit on the Owl; 戊戌 sits on itself. Each is read by the table of the palace, and each, being a vault, by what it stores.

---

### **Seated on the Noble (日贵)**

**Day Pillars:** 丁酉 丁亥 癸巳 癸卯.

**The rule:** The Day Master sits on one of its own Heavenly Noble Branches, by the count given among the stars of the lore.

**The lore:** The old manual's portrait is of a person pure, humane, good to look at and without pride. The seat dreads punishment, clash and harm, and falling in the Void: a Noble provoked, it says, turns its blessing into trouble.

**The reading:** Two of the four, 丁亥 and 癸巳, are also bound seats that hold a spouse star in the palace. The lore agrees here with the method, which is that a spouse palace left unclashed is the first condition of a quiet marriage.

---

### **The Void (空亡)**

**The rule:** The sixty pillars run in six decades of ten, and each decade uses ten of the twelve Branches. The two it leaves out are its Void. The count is made from the decade of the Year Pillar and from that of the Day Pillar. A Day Branch can be Void only by the Year's count, since no pillar is Void in its own decade; the Day's count marks the other three Branches. The mark discussed here applies when the Day Branch is one of the two left out by the decade of the Year.

**甲子 decade:** 甲子 乙丑 丙寅 丁卯 戊辰 己巳 庚午 辛未 壬申 癸酉 — the Void is 戌 Xu and 亥 Hai.

**甲戌 decade:** 甲戌 乙亥 丙子 丁丑 戊寅 己卯 庚辰 辛巳 壬午 癸未 — the Void is 申 Shen and 酉 You.

**甲申 decade:** 甲申 乙酉 丙戌 丁亥 戊子 己丑 庚寅 辛卯 壬辰 癸巳 — the Void is 午 Wu and 未 Wei.

**甲午 decade:** 甲午 乙未 丙申 丁酉 戊戌 己亥 庚子 辛丑 壬寅 癸卯 — the Void is 辰 Chen and 巳 Si.

**甲辰 decade:** 甲辰 乙巳 丙午 丁未 戊申 己酉 庚戌 辛亥 壬子 癸丑 — the Void is 寅 Yin and 卯 Mao.

**甲寅 decade:** 甲寅 乙卯 丙辰 丁巳 戊午 己未 庚申 辛酉 壬戌 癸亥 — the Void is 子 Zi and 丑 Chou.

**The lore:** Seated on the Void, the old manual says, wife and concubine come hard.

**The classical ruling:** The compendium is more careful. The Void is empty and yet has substance, and is not to be taken as ill at once. Where the harmful gods of a chart gather in a Branch, the Void dissolves them and is a mercy — and then the Branch ought not to be combined, because what is combined can no longer be empty. Where the blessings of a chart gather there, the Void scatters them — and then a combination is to be welcomed, for the same reason. The limiting case has a name: where a Void Branch meets no clash, no combination and no punishment, it is the true Void.

**Between two charts:** A partner's Branch that stands in harmony with a Void palace fills it, and a partner's Branch that clashes or punishes it reaches it too. Whether that is welcome is decided by the ruling just given. Ask first what the palace holds. If it holds what the chart needs, the partner who fills it makes the marriage ground real. If it holds the chart's excess, the Void was doing a service, and the same partner brings the excess to life with everything else.

**The use:** A color and never a verdict. The first two texts of this canon do not use the Void at all.

---

## The Spouse by Function

The *role* — if the star is missing, or is itself the trouble, where is the spouse in my chart?

The tradition gives three answers to where the spouse is, and a careful reading holds all three. By relation the spouse is a star: Wealth for a man, Authority for a woman. By place the spouse is the palace: the Day Branch. By function the spouse is whatever god brings the chart to accord. Where the three agree, the marriage is simple. Where they differ, the difference is the reading: it says what you pursue as marriage, where you live it, and what would do you good.

The reading by function is the last the tradition reached, and it is one school's. Use it whenever the star is absent, and whenever the star is present but is the chart's excess.

---

### **For a woman**

Ren Tieqiao's rule is that the husband star of a woman's chart simply is the Useful God, and the child star simply is the Auspicious God. His table works the rule out.

**The Officer too strong, and no Companions:** The husband is the Resource. What converts the pressure into support is the bond.

**The Officer too strong, with Companions and no Resource:** The husband is the Output. What restrains an overbearing rule is the bond.

**The Officer too weak, with a Hurting Officer present:** The husband is the Wealth. What turns the wounding god into a feeder of the Officer is the bond.

**The Officer too weak, no Wealth, Companions strong:** The husband is the Output. A crowded self needs an outlet before it needs a rule.

**Companions filling the chart, no Resource and no Officer:** The husband is the Output, again and for the same reason.

**Resource filling the chart, no Officer and no Output:** The husband is the Wealth. What breaks the smothering is the bond.

**The Hurting Officer strong and the Day Master weak:** The husband is the Resource. What shelters a self that is pouring itself away is the bond.

**The Day Master strong and Output abundant:** The husband is the Wealth. Where the outlet overflows, the bond is in what it flows to.

**The Officer light and Resource heavy:** The husband is the Wealth, which feeds the one and checks the other.

**The test:** Where two lines seem to apply, ask which god the chart needs first. That god is the husband, and the Auspicious God, which supports it, is the child.

**The other reckoning:** The seasonal handbook counts differently. Its rule for a man, given below, makes the Useful God the child and the element that generates it the wife; and for a woman, it says, take that wife as the husband and the Useful God as the child. The two schools disagree about which god is the husband. They agree that husband and child alike are found by asking what the chart needs.

---

### **For a man**

**The rule of the seasons:** The seasonal handbook states it month after month in one form: using this element, that one is the wife and this one the child. The child is the Useful God. The wife is the element that generates the Useful God: whatever feeds the thing the chart most needs.

**The wife star's own needs:** The old note to Di Tian Sui (滴天髓) treats the Wealth star as a patient. Where it is thin, it needs support. Where it is vigorous and the self is weak, the chart welcomes Companions. Where it injures the Resource, the chart wants the Officer to stand between them. Where it is thin and the Officers that draw on it are many, the chart wants the Hurting Officer. Where it is shut in, it wants a clash to open it or an outlet to release it; where it runs loose, it wants a combination or a storehouse to hold it.

**The judgment:** If the Auspicious God is itself the Wealth, the wife is all one could wish. If the two do not begrudge each other, that is also well. If they are at odds, harmony is wanting, and the remedy is whatever reconciles them.

---

### **For either**

**The breath of harmony:** Chen Su'an's rule for a chart with no spouse star at all. Look only at the Day Master and the chart as a whole. Know whether it has the breath of rootedness and you know the parents; the breath of life, and you know the children; the breath of harmony, and you know husband and wife. A chart whose forces pass into one another has a marriage in it, star or no star.

**Between two charts:** Where your partner's Day Master is the god your chart takes as its spouse by function, they are in person what the star only stands for. Few things a comparison can find count for more, and no table of combinations shows it.

---

## Disease and Medicine

The *remedy* — what is wrong with marriage in this chart, and what answers it?

A chart is not judged by whether it has a fault. Every chart has one. It is judged by whether the fault has a remedy, and by where the remedy is to be found: in the chart, in the years, or in a second chart. Each entry names a disease of the star or of the palace, the god that is its medicine, what in a partner's chart supplies that god, and which years bring it. The old books wrote all of these down as the overcoming of a wife or a husband. Not one of them is a statement about another person. Each is a strain on a relation, and each has an answer.

---

### **The wife star robbed**

**The sign:** A man's Wealth is light, his self strong, his Companions or Blades many, with no Output between them and no Officer to guard.

**The medicine:** Output first, which turns the robbers into suppliers; the Officer second, which holds them off.

**In a partner:** A chart that is, to him, mostly Output or Authority; and above all a woman whose own heavy Output is his Wealth. This is the first case of Zhang Nan's rule.

**In time:** Years of Output and Wealth mend it. Years of Companions aggravate it.

---

### **The wife star too heavy**

**The sign:** A man's Wealth commands the season or stands in several places, and the self is weak: the poor man in the rich house.

**The medicine:** Companions and Resource. He needs not less of a wife but more of himself.

**In a partner:** A chart that is, to him, mostly Companions and Resource, and that brings no more of his Wealth element. A woman who is his Wealth in person adds to the weight however dear she is.

**In time:** Years of Companions and Resource are relief. Years of more Wealth are heavy, even when they are the years he marries.

---

### **The wife star against the Resource**

**The sign:** A man's chart leans on its Resource — a weak self under strong Authority — and Wealth stands out and breaks that Resource. In life it is the man between his wife and his mother, or between what he pursues and what sustains him.

**The medicine:** The Officer, which Wealth feeds and which feeds Resource, so that the quarrel becomes a relay; or Companions to restrain the Wealth.

**In a partner:** A chart that is, to him, Resource or Officer, not more Wealth.

**In time:** Wealth years injure. Officer and Resource years mend.

---

### **The wife star feeding the Killings**

**The sign:** A man's Killings are already heavy on a weak self, and Wealth supplies them: harm arrived at through what he takes responsibility for.

**The medicine:** Resource, which turns the Killings into support; or the Eating God, which restrains them.

**In a partner:** A chart that is, to him, Resource. One that is his Wealth or Authority is the last grade.

**In time:** Resource years turn the pressure. Wealth and Killings years add to it.

---

### **The husband star struck**

**The sign:** A woman's Hurting Officer is heavy, her Officer weak, and no Wealth stands between them.

**The medicine:** Wealth first, which makes the wounding god a feeder of the Officer; Resource second, which restrains it.

**In a partner:** A chart that is, to her, mostly Wealth; or mostly Resource; or one whose abundance is her Authority, a husband star too plentiful to be wounded. The last two are the exact cases of Zhang Nan's rule.

**In time:** Wealth years mend. Years of more Output are the old books' second marriage: read them as the years the bond is most strained.

---

### **The husband star drained**

**The sign:** A woman's Resource is heaped and her Officer light. The heaped Resource steals the breath of the Officer and suppresses the child star besides. In life it is a self so well supplied that the bond has nothing to do.

**The medicine:** Wealth, which holds the Resource in check and feeds the Officer at once.

**In a partner:** A chart that is, to her, Wealth: something to manage, a use for her competence.

**In time:** Wealth years open it. Resource years close it further.

---

### **The husband star too heavy**

**The sign:** A woman's Officer or Killings stand in layers over a weak self. Zhang Nan says flatly that this is no good husband.

**The medicine:** Resource, which converts the pressure; or Output to restrain it, in measure, for overdone restraint is a new deficiency.

**In a partner:** A chart that is, to her, Resource and Companions. A man who is her Authority in person adds to what already crushes her.

**In time:** Resource and Companion years are relief.

---

### **The self too strong for its star**

**The sign:** A strong Day Master and a thin spouse star. In a man it is Wealth too slight to engage him. In a woman it is the condition the old books call seizing the husband's authority. The person lives in the marriage alone.

**The medicine:** The star made stronger: by what feeds it, or by its arrival in force. For a strong self with no star at all, the outlet.

**In a partner:** One whose Day Master is the star in person, or whose chart is mostly the star's element. This is the case in which the classical marriage of controller and controlled is exactly right.

**In time:** Years that bring the star or what generates it.

---

### **The star in two forms**

**The sign:** Direct and indirect Wealth both declared in a man's chart; Officer and Killings both present in a woman's with neither removed. The bond comes in two voices that do not agree.

**The medicine:** One of the two taken out of play, by a combination that ties it up or a god that restrains it.

**In a partner:** A Day Master that combines with the unwanted form, or that is the god which restrains it.

**In time:** Where the chart holds one form only, the years that bring the other are its tests.

---

### **The star absent**

**The sign:** No Wealth in a man's chart, or no Officer or Killings in a woman's, in any Stem or any Branch.

**The medicine:** The reading by function, which finds the spouse in another god; the breath of harmony in the chart as a whole; and time.

**In a partner:** A chart that holds the missing star; and better, one whose Day Master is the spouse by function.

**In time:** The decades that carry the star, or the functional spouse, on their Stems.

---

### **The star in storage**

**The sign:** The spouse star sealed in one of the four vaults, most of all the vault beneath the Day Master or the Hour.

**The medicine:** The Branch that clashes the vault, by the classical doctrine this canon follows. The Branch in six-harmony with it does the opposite: by the old note to Di Tian Sui and by Ren Tieqiao a combination holds a vault shut, and the companion reference, which counted a combination among the things that open one, is to be corrected to agree.

**In a partner:** A Day Branch that clashes the vault keeps it stirred and open. A Day Branch that combines with it keeps the star close and keeps it in.

**In time:** The years of the clashing Branch. Until then the marriage is late, or guarded, or held in reserve.

**The critics:** Shen Xiaozhan and Ren Tieqiao both denied that a clash releases what a vault stores; what it stirs, they held, is the Earth. If they are right, a star in storage waits for the decades that bring it out on a Stem.

---

### **The palace struck**

**The sign:** The Day Branch clashed or punished by the Month or the Hour Branch, and most gravely by a Month Branch in season.

**The medicine:** Something that gathers in the motion. The old note names four things: a combining god with strength, a Branch of the struck Branch's frame, the vault of its element, or a Noble. Its example is a Rat struck by a Horse and stilled by an Ox.

**Which combination:** Ren Tieqiao's refinement decides it, and it turns on what the palace holds. If the struck palace holds what your chart needs, the fitting remedies are two: a combination that takes hold of the Branch that strikes it and turns it away from the element it strikes with; and a combination, of six-harmony or of frame, that joins the palace to partners turning toward its own element, as its frame does for a cardinal Branch and the Pig does for a Tiger. A combination that turns the palace toward another element quiets the seat and tethers what it holds, and a combination that feeds the attacker's weapon is worse than none. If the struck palace holds your chart's excess, the combination that turns it toward another element is the remedy, because what it ties is what you wanted tied.

**The limit:** Where the Stems above the two Branches clash as well, it is heaven and earth at war, and Ren Tieqiao holds that neither a combining god nor a gathering one stills that.

**In a partner:** Look across their whole chart, and not only at their Day Branch, for the Branches just named. Where their Day Branch is the harmony partner of yours, know that their palace will stand in harm with the Branch that strikes you, and will bear some of what used to fall on you alone.

**In time:** The years of those Branches quiet the seat. The years of the striking Branch double the blow.

---

### **The palace untouched**

**The sign:** The Day Branch in no relation with any other Branch of the chart, and most of all a vault, or a Branch in the Void that nothing reaches.

**The medicine:** Contact, of one of three kinds. The Branch in six-harmony with the palace reaches it and joins it to something, and if it lay in the Void, fills it. The Branches of its three-harmony frame draw it into a common direction, and where the frame is completed, into a current. The Branch that clashes the palace moves it, and if it is a vault, opens it. Before calling any of these a cure, ask what the palace holds and what the chart needs. A room full of what the chart needs is the better for being reached. A room full of the chart's excess was better left quiet, and a frame that runs toward the chart's Useful God does more for such a chart than any contact with the room itself.

**In a partner:** A Day Branch that is the harmony partner makes the room real and holds it, and if the room is a vault, holds it shut. A Day Branch that clashes it keeps it in motion. A Branch anywhere in their chart that completes a frame through your palace does the third thing.

**In time:** The years of those two Branches are when things happen. In the years that repeat the Day Branch the room is doubled upon itself.

---

### **The palace holding what the chart shuns**

**The sign:** The principal Stem of the Day Branch is the chart's Inauspicious God: the Owl in a chart that lives by its Output, more Companions under a strong self, Wealth under a self that depends on its Resource.

**The medicine:** The god that controls the tenant, standing close to the Day Pillar: Wealth over the Owl, the Officer over Companions, Companions over Wealth.

**In a partner:** A chart that is, to you, that controlling god. A Day Branch in harmony with yours is a doubtful remedy here: by one old ruling the harmony holds the tenant idle, and by another, if the palace lay in the Void, it brings the tenant to life.

**In time:** Years that strengthen the controlling god protect the marriage ground. Years that attack it or tie it up release what it was holding.

---

## Day Master Meets Day Master (10×10)

The *meeting* — what is this person to me, and what am I to them?

Find your own Day Master's entry, and in it the paragraph for your partner's. Each paragraph opens with the two gods: what they are to you, and what you are to them. Then read the pair again from their entry, because the two descriptions are of one relation seen from its two ends and neither is the whole of it.

Five kinds of couple are possible, according to how the two elements stand in the cycle. Where one controls the other, the pair has the shape the tradition called marriage: one is the other's Wealth and the other is the first one's Authority. Where one generates the other, one is Resource and the other Output: the giver of substance and the one in whom it is spent. Where the two share an element they are Companions. Opposite polarity gives each relation its gentler, direct form; like polarity gives it the blunter, indirect one.

The spouse stars follow from this. If you are a man and your partner is your Wealth, she is your wife star in person and you are her husband star. If you are a woman and your partner is your Authority, the same is true from your side. In the other configurations neither of you is the other's spouse star, and that is no defect: it means only that the marriage is to be looked for in the exchange and in the palaces. One case is noted wherever it occurs. When the woman's element generates the man's, his wife star and her husband star are one and the same element, and the years that bring it bring it to both.

Whether any of these meetings is fortunate is decided by the charts and not by this table. A partner who is your Resource is the making of a weak chart and the smothering of a strong one. A partner who is your Wealth is a joy to a self that can carry it and a weight on one that cannot. Read each paragraph with your own strength in mind.

---

### **甲 Jia — Yang Wood beside each Day Master**

The tree grows upward and does not bend, and in partnership it wants ground to root in and something to reach toward. It is steadied by Earth, shaped by Metal, fed by Water and spent in Fire.

**甲 Jia (Yang Wood):** Your Equal; to them you are the Equal. Two tall trees side by side: the same uprightness, the same need for light, and the same inability to give way. You understand each other without effort and compete for the canopy without meaning to. A fine alliance where both are short of support, and a contest of two trunks where both are strong.

**乙 Yi (Yin Wood):** Your Rival; to them you are the Rival. The tree and the vine. Yi climbs what you have built and reaches light it could not reach alone, and you are softened and adorned by what clings to you. The vine gets more from this than the tree. It is a warm pairing as long as you do not count what it costs you, and Yi does not forget what it stands on.

**丙 Bing (Yang Fire):** Your Provider; to them you are the Owl Spirit. The tree in full sun: what you are goes up as light and warmth, gladly, and they shine the brighter for it. Bing makes you visible and gives your growth a purpose. You are the fuel. Splendid when you have substance to spare, and in a chart already giving in every direction, the pairing in which you are consumed with applause. If you are the woman, you share one spouse star, and it is Metal.

**丁 Ding (Yin Fire):** Your Underminer; to them you are the True Patron. Firewood and the lamp. Ding takes what you are and turns it into a steady, pointed light, and no Stem feeds a flame so well as you do. They are your sharpest expression and will say what you only think. You give, they refine; the giving is constant. If you are the woman, you share one spouse star, and it is Metal.

**戊 Wu (Yang Earth):** Your Windfall; to them you are the Merciless. The tree on the mountain. Your roots break into rock that did not ask to be broken, and the mountain holds you up at a height no field could. You bring life to something inert and it bears you as a burden and a crown. If you are the man, this is your wife star in its secondary form, and you are her husband star in its fiercer one.

**己 Ji (Yin Earth):** Your Earned Fortune; to them you are the Magistrate. The tree in the field, and one of the Five Combinations: the old books call it the centered and upright. Ji receives your roots and is held together by them; you are given a place to stand and something to be answerable for. The pull needs no explaining. If you are the man, each of you is the other's spouse star in person.

**庚 Geng (Yang Metal):** Your Merciless; to them you are Windfall. The axe and the timber, and a clash between Stems. Geng cuts, and what is cut becomes beam and pillar: the old line is that Wood in its vigor must get Metal before it is timber. It is a pairing of open contest that produces things. A strong tree is finished by it. A weak one is felled. If you are the woman, this is your husband star in its fiercer form.

**辛 Xin (Yin Metal):** Your Magistrate; to them you are Earned Fortune. The great tree and the small, exact knife. Xin does not fell; it prunes, corrects and holds you to a standard finer than your own. You are a large thing for a precise nature to work on, and its work is never done. If you are the woman, each of you is the other's spouse star in person.

**壬 Ren (Yang Water):** Your Owl Spirit; to them you are the Provider. The tree beside the great river. There is more water than a root can drink, and it feeds you on its own terms: abundantly, with a current that can carry a tree away. You are where Ren's restless depth becomes something that stands. If you are the man, you share one spouse star, and it is Earth.

**癸 Gui (Yin Water):** Your True Patron; to them you are the Underminer. Rain on the tree. Gui gives quietly and without condition, and everything in you that grows, grows from it. You are their outlet, taller and louder than they would ever be themselves. The easiest nourishment a Jia can receive. If you are the man, you share one spouse star, and it is Earth.

---

### **乙 Yi — Yin Wood beside each Day Master**

The vine bends, climbs and persists, and in partnership it wants something to hold. It is supported by what is larger than itself and endangered by whatever cuts.

**甲 Jia (Yang Wood):** Your Rival; to them you are the Rival. The vine and the tree. You rise by what Jia has built, and you give it grace in return; the old phrase for it is the creeper tied to the tall tree, which can pass the spring and the autumn alike. Of all the meetings of like with like this one favors you most. The risk is that you never learn to stand, and that Jia comes to feel climbed.

**乙 Yi (Yin Wood):** Your Equal; to them you are the Equal. Two vines intertwined: endlessly accommodating, quick to understand each other, and with nothing rigid between you to climb. Tender and well matched in temper. What the pair lacks is a trellis, and it must find one outside itself.

**丙 Bing (Yang Fire):** Your Underminer; to them you are the True Patron. The flower and the sun. Everything in you opens toward Bing, and what you give it costs you nothing you notice. They draw out your color, your wit and your daring. It is the most radiant thing that can happen to a Yi, and in a weak chart the flower is spent by noon. If you are the woman, you share one spouse star, and it is Metal.

**丁 Ding (Yin Fire):** Your Provider; to them you are the Owl Spirit. Dry grass and the candle. You feed a small, intimate flame, and it gives your gentleness a point of light. A quiet, domestic exchange: you supply, they glow. It asks little at a time and never stops asking. If you are the woman, you share one spouse star, and it is Metal.

**戊 Wu (Yang Earth):** Your Earned Fortune; to them you are the Magistrate. The vine on the wall, the green on the mountainside. Wu is vast, still and bare, and you cover it, soften it and hold its surface together. You are given something immovable to attach to. If you are the man, each of you is the other's spouse star in person.

**己 Ji (Yin Earth):** Your Windfall; to them you are the Merciless. Grass in the garden bed. Ji is easy ground: you take root at once and draw on it freely, and it bears the taking with more strain than it shows. A close and unequal comfort. If you are the man, this is your wife star in its secondary form.

**庚 Geng (Yang Metal):** Your Magistrate; to them you are Earned Fortune. The vine and the sword, and one of the Five Combinations: the old books call it humaneness and duty, the soft and the hard each supplying the other. Geng is the one blade that does not cut you; you are the one soft thing it will not strike. If you are the woman, each of you is the other's spouse star in person.

**辛 Xin (Yin Metal):** Your Merciless; to them you are Windfall. The flower and the scissors, and a clash between Stems. Xin cuts close and cuts for beauty, and what you lose to it you feel. A pairing of fine taste and real hurt, where the one who trims believes it is tending. If you are the woman, this is your husband star in its fiercer form.

**壬 Ren (Yang Water):** Your True Patron; to them you are the Underminer. The lotus on the lake. Ren holds you up on a depth you could never root in, and you are the one visible, living thing on its surface. Generous support, and unsteady footing: a vine can float. If you are the man, you share one spouse star, and it is Earth.

**癸 Gui (Yin Water):** Your Owl Spirit; to them you are the Provider. Dew on the leaf. Gui feeds you in small, exact amounts and expects to see what it has fed. You are where their quiet becomes something that flowers. Close, private and a little possessive. If you are the man, you share one spouse star, and it is Earth.

---

### **丙 Bing — Yang Fire beside each Day Master**

The sun gives without choosing and wants to be seen giving. In partnership it needs something to shine on and something to feed it, and it is tempered only by Water.

**甲 Jia (Yang Wood):** Your Owl Spirit; to them you are the Provider. The sun and the great tree. Jia is fuel in bulk and on its own terms: it feeds your blaze and expects you to be what it grew for. You are its light and its purpose. A strong, proud pairing in which the one who supplies never lets the debt be forgotten. If you are the man, you share one spouse star, and it is Metal.

**乙 Yi (Yin Wood):** Your True Patron; to them you are the Underminer. The sun and the flower. Yi gives you everything it has, lightly, and turns its whole face toward you. You draw out what is brightest in it. Be careful what you take: the supply is willing and it is not large. If you are the man, you share one spouse star, and it is Metal.

**丙 Bing (Yang Fire):** Your Equal; to them you are the Equal. Two suns in one sky. Each of you is used to being the source of warmth in the room, and here there are two. Exhilarating among friends and wearing at home, unless the two of you have enough to shine on between you.

**丁 Ding (Yin Fire):** Your Rival; to them you are the Rival. The sun and the lamp. By day no one sees the lamp, and Ding will feel that; by night no one sees the sun, and you will feel that. You share an element and work different hours. It goes well where each is given a time of their own.

**戊 Wu (Yang Earth):** Your Provider; to them you are the Owl Spirit. The sun on the mountain. Your warmth goes into something that keeps it: stone holds heat long after dark. Wu is where your giving comes to rest and is stored. Steady and undemonstrative, and it can absorb more than you meant to give. If you are the woman, you share one spouse star, and it is Water.

**己 Ji (Yin Earth):** Your Underminer; to them you are the True Patron. The sun on the field. You ripen what Ji grows and, shining too long, bake it hard. They are your sharpest outlet, practical and close to the ground, and they see through your display. If you are the woman, you share one spouse star, and it is Water.

**庚 Geng (Yang Metal):** Your Windfall; to them you are the Merciless. The sun on raw ore. Heat without a forge: you can make Geng glow and cannot make it a blade, and it resents being worked on by something that does not know its craft. A bold, grinding pairing. If you are the man, this is your wife star in its secondary form.

**辛 Xin (Yin Metal):** Your Earned Fortune; to them you are the Magistrate. The sun and the jewel, and one of the Five Combinations: the old books call it awe and command. Xin has no light of its own and shows every facet in yours; you are given something exquisite to illuminate. The pair reaches toward Water: the meeting leans toward depth. If you are the man, each of you is the other's spouse star in person.

**壬 Ren (Yang Water):** Your Merciless; to them you are Windfall. The sun and the sea, and a clash between Stems. It is a clash the old masters admired: sunlight on great water, each making the other magnificent. Ren is the only thing large enough to contain you. For a strong sun this is the pairing that gives it grandeur; for a weak one, the horizon into which it sets. If you are the woman, this is your husband star in its fiercer form.

**癸 Gui (Yin Water):** Your Magistrate; to them you are Earned Fortune. The sun and the rain cloud. Gui veils you, cools you and turns your heat to use: without it nothing you warm would grow. It is governance by softening, and you will sometimes feel dimmed by what makes you fruitful. If you are the woman, each of you is the other's spouse star in person.

---

### **丁 Ding — Yin Fire beside each Day Master**

The lamp and the forge: a small flame with a purpose. In partnership it needs fuel and something to work on, and it lives in fear of being put out.

**甲 Jia (Yang Wood):** Your True Patron; to them you are the Underminer. The lamp and the firewood. Jia is the best fuel there is for a flame like yours: solid, dry, lasting. With it you burn evenly for a lifetime. You are the point to which its mass is brought. If you are the man, you share one spouse star, and it is Metal.

**乙 Yi (Yin Wood):** Your Owl Spirit; to them you are the Provider. The candle and the grass. Yi feeds you readily and burns off fast: a flare, then a need for more. They live through your light and watch how you use it. Affectionate and uneven. If you are the man, you share one spouse star, and it is Metal.

**丙 Bing (Yang Fire):** Your Rival; to them you are the Rival. The lamp and the sun. Beside Bing your light is real and no one remarks on it. They do not mean to eclipse you; they simply shine. You keep the one thing they lack, which is aim. Give yourself the night hours.

**丁 Ding (Yin Fire):** Your Equal; to them you are the Equal. Two lamps in one room. The room is brighter and the oil is shared. You know each other's flicker, each other's fear of the draft. Gentle, loyal and prone to burning low together.

**戊 Wu (Yang Earth):** Your Underminer; to them you are the True Patron. The fire in the kiln. Wu banks you, holds your heat and takes its shape from it, and it also covers your light. They are where your warmth becomes something solid, and where your brightness goes unseen. If you are the woman, you share one spouse star, and it is Water.

**己 Ji (Yin Earth):** Your Provider; to them you are the Owl Spirit. The hearth and the floor of the house. You warm Ji daily and without drama, and it becomes livable ground. The most domestic of your outlets: unglamorous, constant, and kind. If you are the woman, you share one spouse star, and it is Water.

**庚 Geng (Yang Metal):** Your Earned Fortune; to them you are the Magistrate. The forge and the ore. This is the work you were made for: Geng comes to you rough and leaves an instrument, and it knows it. You are needed, and you will be tired. If you are the man, each of you is the other's spouse star in person.

**辛 Xin (Yin Metal):** Your Windfall; to them you are the Merciless. The flame and the finished jewel. Xin does not want forging; it wants light, and yours is too close and too hot. What you mean as attention it feels as damage. A pairing of two fine sensibilities that injure each other by proximity. If you are the man, this is your wife star in its secondary form.

**壬 Ren (Yang Water):** Your Magistrate; to them you are Earned Fortune. The lamp on the ocean, and one of the Five Combinations: the old books give it their most suspicious name, and they are describing how strongly it is felt. Ren could put you out and does not; it takes its bearing from you. The pair reaches toward Wood, and what should have been an extinguishing becomes a growing. If you are the woman, each of you is the other's spouse star in person.

**癸 Gui (Yin Water):** Your Merciless; to them you are Windfall. The candle in the rain, and a clash between Stems. Gui does not overwhelm; it gets in, drop by drop. You hiss and gutter and are never quite put out. Unless something stands between you — Wood, which drinks the rain and feeds the flame — it is a pairing of slow attrition. If you are the woman, this is your husband star in its fiercer form.

---

### **戊 Wu — Yang Earth beside each Day Master**

The mountain does not move and does not need to. In partnership it wants to be of use: to hold, to shelter, to bear something living. It is opened by Wood and warmed by Fire.

**甲 Jia (Yang Wood):** Your Merciless; to them you are Windfall. The mountain and the tree whose roots split rock. Jia breaks you open, and a mountain that has never been broken open has never borne anything. It is the pressure that makes you alive. For a massive chart, the best thing that can happen. For a thin one, a landslide. If you are the woman, this is your husband star in its fiercer form.

**乙 Yi (Yin Wood):** Your Magistrate; to them you are Earned Fortune. The mountain and the green that covers it. Yi governs you by adorning you: it holds your surface, gives you seasons and makes you worth looking at. A gentle rule, gladly borne. If you are the woman, each of you is the other's spouse star in person.

**丙 Bing (Yang Fire):** Your Owl Spirit; to them you are the Provider. The mountain in full sun. Bing warms you to the core and makes no secret of it; you are where its giving is stored. Abundant support that comes with an audience. In a chart already dry it is too much heat on bare stone. If you are the man, you share one spouse star, and it is Water.

**丁 Ding (Yin Fire):** Your True Patron; to them you are the Underminer. The fire in the mountain: the kiln, the hearth in the cave. Ding's warmth is small, steady and entirely for you, and you keep it alive by sheltering it. The quietest and most faithful of your supports. If you are the man, you share one spouse star, and it is Water.

**戊 Wu (Yang Earth):** Your Equal; to them you are the Equal. Two mountains in one range. Immense stability, and no traffic between the peaks. You will not quarrel much, because neither of you will move. The pairing needs a river running through it.

**己 Ji (Yin Earth):** Your Rival; to them you are the Rival. The mountain and the field at its foot. Ji is your own substance made soft and workable: it takes what washes down from you and grows things in it. You lose soil to them and gain a use.

**庚 Geng (Yang Metal):** Your Provider; to them you are the Owl Spirit. The mountain and its ore. Geng is what you carry inside and give up to the world: hard, valuable, dug out of you. You are its source and its weight. A strong pairing where the mountain is rich, and a mine worked out where it is not. If you are the woman, you share one spouse star, and it is Wood.

**辛 Xin (Yin Metal):** Your Underminer; to them you are the True Patron. The mountain and the gem hidden in it. You produce Xin and you bury it: the old warning is that thick Earth hides the jewel. They are the finest thing that comes out of you and they need to be let out. If you are the woman, you share one spouse star, and it is Wood.

**壬 Ren (Yang Water):** Your Windfall; to them you are the Merciless. The mountain and the great river: the dam. You can hold Ren, and holding it is the measure of your strength. It is restless, magnificent and never yours for long. If you are the man, this is your wife star in its secondary form.

**癸 Gui (Yin Water):** Your Earned Fortune; to them you are the Magistrate. The mountain and the mist, and one of the Five Combinations. The old books call it the combination without feeling and picture an old husband with a young wife. Read without the gossip, it is stillness giving a course to what moves: Gui comes to you of its own accord and leaves as a stream with a name. The pair reaches toward Fire. If you are the man, each of you is the other's spouse star in person.

---

### **己 Ji — Yin Earth beside each Day Master**

The field receives, holds and brings to fruit. In partnership it wants to be planted: to be given something to grow and someone who needs what it yields. It is warmed by Fire, worked by Wood and carried off by too much Water.

**甲 Jia (Yang Wood):** Your Magistrate; to them you are Earned Fortune. The field and the tree, and one of the Five Combinations: the centered and upright. Jia roots in you and gives you a purpose; you give it a place to stand. You are governed by being used well. If you are the woman, each of you is the other's spouse star in person.

**乙 Yi (Yin Wood):** Your Merciless; to them you are Windfall. The field and the crop that takes without thanks. Yi draws on you closely and constantly, and you feel every root. It makes you fertile and leaves you tired. If you are the woman, this is your husband star in its fiercer form.

**丙 Bing (Yang Fire):** Your True Patron; to them you are the Underminer. The field in the sun. Bing asks nothing and ripens everything you carry. You are where its radiance turns into something that can be eaten. Too much of it bakes you hard, but in measure there is no better weather. If you are the man, you share one spouse star, and it is Water.

**丁 Ding (Yin Fire):** Your Owl Spirit; to them you are the Provider. The hearth on the floor of the house. Ding warms one spot of you, steadily, and expects the house to be built around it. A close, indoor nourishment with a will of its own. If you are the man, you share one spouse star, and it is Water.

**戊 Wu (Yang Earth):** Your Rival; to them you are the Rival. The field beneath the mountain. Wu is your own substance in bulk: it shelters you from the wind and sends you its soil, and it also stands in your light. You are the part of the same ground that actually feeds anyone.

**己 Ji (Yin Earth):** Your Equal; to them you are the Equal. Two fields with a shared boundary. Patient, practical and modest together, and inclined to wait for the other to plant first. Nothing in the pairing is unkind and nothing in it begins anything. It needs a season from outside.

**庚 Geng (Yang Metal):** Your Underminer; to them you are the True Patron. The ground and the ore dug out of it. Geng is the hard, bright thing your softness gives up to the world, and it cuts where you would have yielded. They say what you swallowed. If you are the woman, you share one spouse star, and it is Wood.

**辛 Xin (Yin Metal):** Your Provider; to them you are the Owl Spirit. The clay and the jewel bedded in it. You hold Xin gently and feed it by simply being its ground; the old caution is that wet earth also dulls a gem. Give it the support and let something else wash it clean. If you are the woman, you share one spouse star, and it is Wood.

**壬 Ren (Yang Water):** Your Earned Fortune; to them you are the Magistrate. The field and the river. Ren is far larger than you are and you are its bank: you give it an edge, and you cloud it a little in doing so. A wife star greater than the self that governs it, to be channeled and never dammed. If you are the man, each of you is the other's spouse star in person.

**癸 Gui (Yin Water):** Your Windfall; to them you are the Merciless. The soil and the rain. You take Gui in at once and wholly, and it disappears into you: what was a free and wandering thing becomes moisture in your keeping. Fruitful for you and absorbing for them. If you are the man, this is your wife star in its secondary form.

---

### **庚 Geng — Yang Metal beside each Day Master**

The blade is hard, direct and loyal, and it does not know its own use until something gives it one. In partnership it wants to be forged and to be needed. It is made by Fire and softened only by what it will not cut.

**甲 Jia (Yang Wood):** Your Windfall; to them you are the Merciless. The axe and the timber, and a clash between Stems. You fell what stands tall, and what you fell becomes a house. It is open contest, and it produces. Jia will not thank you while it is happening. If you are the man, this is your wife star in its secondary form.

**乙 Yi (Yin Wood):** Your Earned Fortune; to them you are the Magistrate. The sword and the vine, and one of the Five Combinations: humaneness and duty. Yi is the one soft thing you do not cut, and around it your edge is laid down. The pair reaches toward Metal, the firmer of your two natures. If you are the man, each of you is the other's spouse star in person.

**丙 Bing (Yang Fire):** Your Merciless; to them you are Windfall. Ore under the sun. Bing heats you through and has no anvil: you are softened, exposed and not shaped. It is authority as glare. A strong Geng is brightened by it. A weak one is merely made to sweat. If you are the woman, this is your husband star in its fiercer form.

**丁 Ding (Yin Fire):** Your Magistrate; to them you are Earned Fortune. The forge. Ding is patient, exact heat applied to the right place, and under it raw metal becomes an instrument. No Day Master is more improved by the one who governs it. You will complain of the fire and be grateful for the edge. If you are the woman, each of you is the other's spouse star in person.

**戊 Wu (Yang Earth):** Your Owl Spirit; to them you are the Provider. The ore in the mountain. Wu made you and would as soon keep you where you are: the old warning is that thick Earth buries Metal. Massive support, and a weight on the lid. If you are the man, you share one spouse star, and it is Wood.

**己 Ji (Yin Earth):** Your True Patron; to them you are the Underminer. The blade laid on soft ground. Ji supplies you gently and without condition, and leaves a little mud on the steel. It is rest. It is not polish. If you are the man, you share one spouse star, and it is Wood.

**庚 Geng (Yang Metal):** Your Equal; to them you are the Equal. Two blades. Mutual respect, plain speech, and sparks whenever you touch. You will stand back to back against anything, and face to face you will test each other's temper.

**辛 Xin (Yin Metal):** Your Rival; to them you are the Rival. The sword and the jewel: one metal, two purposes. You are force and Xin is finish; each privately thinks the other has mistaken what metal is for. Useful to each other and seldom at ease.

**壬 Ren (Yang Water):** Your Provider; to them you are the Owl Spirit. The spring in the rock. What is hard in you gives rise to something that moves: Ren carries your strength away and makes it travel. It is the natural outlet of a strong Geng, and the old phrase for it is bright metal and clear water. If you are the woman, you share one spouse star, and it is Fire.

**癸 Gui (Yin Water):** Your Underminer; to them you are the True Patron. The blade in the rain. Gui draws you out drop by drop, and what it draws out is sharp: your criticism, your refusals, your wit. It also rusts what it wets. They are your edge turned into speech. If you are the woman, you share one spouse star, and it is Fire.

---

### **辛 Xin — Yin Metal beside each Day Master**

The jewel is finished, exact and easily marred. In partnership it wants to be seen truly and handled well. It is shown by light, cleaned by moving water and buried by too much Earth.

**甲 Jia (Yang Wood):** Your Earned Fortune; to them you are the Magistrate. The fine blade and the great tree. Jia is a large, straight thing for a precise nature to work on; you correct it and it bears the correction. Your standards will be felt, and the task is bigger than the tool. If you are the man, each of you is the other's spouse star in person.

**乙 Yi (Yin Wood):** Your Windfall; to them you are the Merciless. The scissors and the flower, and a clash between Stems. You trim what is tender and call it care. Yi is the most delicate thing you could have been given to manage and the least able to bear your exactness. If you are the man, this is your wife star in its secondary form.

**丙 Bing (Yang Fire):** Your Magistrate; to them you are Earned Fortune. The jewel and the sun, and one of the Five Combinations: awe and command. In Bing's light you are seen for what you are; you give that light something worth falling on. The companion reference's picture of the pull is of the jewel's rigid precision melting toward something more fluid. The pair reaches toward Water. If you are the woman, each of you is the other's spouse star in person.

**丁 Ding (Yin Fire):** Your Merciless; to them you are Windfall. The jewel and the flame held too close. Ding means to show you and scorches you instead: a finished thing does not want forging. It is attention that injures. Some distance, and something between you, makes it bearable. If you are the woman, this is your husband star in its fiercer form.

**戊 Wu (Yang Earth):** Your True Patron; to them you are the Underminer. The gem in the mountain. Wu supports you completely and hides you completely: nothing is safer and nothing is less seen. You are the one bright, sharp thing that comes out of all that mass. If you are the man, you share one spouse star, and it is Wood.

**己 Ji (Yin Earth):** Your Owl Spirit; to them you are the Provider. The jewel in wet clay. Ji keeps you close and feeds you on its own terms, and a little of it clings. You are held, and dimmed. If you are the man, you share one spouse star, and it is Wood.

**庚 Geng (Yang Metal):** Your Rival; to them you are the Rival. The jewel and the sword. Geng is your own element without the refinement, and it takes up room. It will defend you, and it will scratch you.

**辛 Xin (Yin Metal):** Your Equal; to them you are the Equal. Two jewels in one case. Perfect understanding of each other's sensitivity and no one to do the rough work. You will notice everything about each other, including what would be better unnoticed.

**壬 Ren (Yang Water):** Your Underminer; to them you are the True Patron. The jewel washed in the river. The old masters held that nothing shows this Metal so well as Yang Water: in moving water the gem comes clean and its brilliance is seen. Ren is where everything exact and guarded in you can at last be said. If you are the woman, you share one spouse star, and it is Fire.

**癸 Gui (Yin Water):** Your Provider; to them you are the Owl Spirit. The jewel in the mist. Gui is a soft outlet: you give to it without effort and it beads on you, close and quiet. It does not wash you as the river does. A gentle exchange that leaves you a little clouded. If you are the woman, you share one spouse star, and it is Fire.

---

### **壬 Ren — Yang Water beside each Day Master**

The ocean is vast, moving and without a shore of its own. In partnership it wants a source, a direction and a bank. It is fed by Metal, pointed by Fire and held by Earth.

**甲 Jia (Yang Wood):** Your Provider; to them you are the Owl Spirit. The river and the great tree on its bank. What you are goes up into Jia and becomes something that stands and can be seen. It is your easiest giving, and it has no end. If you are the woman, you share one spouse star, and it is Earth.

**乙 Yi (Yin Wood):** Your Underminer; to them you are the True Patron. The lake and the lotus. Yi lives on your surface, beautiful and unrooted, and draws from you the sharper, more playful part of yourself. You hold it up. It does not hold you. If you are the woman, you share one spouse star, and it is Earth.

**丙 Bing (Yang Fire):** Your Windfall; to them you are the Merciless. The sea and the sun, and a clash between Stems. It is the clash the old masters admired: light on great water. Bing is the bright, far thing you reach for and can never hold; you are the depth in which it sees itself. If you are the man, this is your wife star in its secondary form.

**丁 Ding (Yin Fire):** Your Earned Fortune; to them you are the Magistrate. The ocean and the lamp, and one of the Five Combinations. You could put Ding out and do not, and the small fixed light gives the boundless water its bearing. The pair reaches toward Wood. If you are the man, each of you is the other's spouse star in person.

**戊 Wu (Yang Earth):** Your Merciless; to them you are Windfall. The sea and the dam. Wu stops you, and a water that has never been stopped has never been deep. It is hard, impersonal containment. A flooding chart is saved by it. A shallow one is silted up. If you are the woman, this is your husband star in its fiercer form.

**己 Ji (Yin Earth):** Your Magistrate; to them you are Earned Fortune. The river and its soft bank. Ji gives you an edge without a wall, and clouds you a little in the giving. You are governed by something smaller than yourself, and it works by patience. If you are the woman, each of you is the other's spouse star in person.

**庚 Geng (Yang Metal):** Your Owl Spirit; to them you are the Provider. The river and the rock it rises from. Geng is source in its raw form: hard, plentiful, and with views of its own about where you should run. It makes you inexhaustible. If you are the man, you share one spouse star, and it is Fire.

**辛 Xin (Yin Metal):** Your True Patron; to them you are the Underminer. The spring in the jewelled rock: bright metal and clear water. Xin feeds you without condition and with great exactness, and you carry what it gives out into the world as speech and movement. A Ren chart without Metal is an ocean with no spring; this is the spring. If you are the man, you share one spouse star, and it is Fire.

**壬 Ren (Yang Water):** Your Equal; to them you are the Equal. Two oceans. Limitless understanding and no shore between you. Together you are deeper and no more contained than either was alone. The pairing needs a bank from outside.

**癸 Gui (Yin Water):** Your Rival; to them you are the Rival. The sea and the rain that falls into it. Gui is your element in its quiet, persistent form: it adds to you without display and takes its share without asking. Close enough to know your depths, different enough to want what you hold.

---

### **癸 Gui — Yin Water beside each Day Master**

The rain is quiet, pervasive and patient. In partnership it wants to be gathered: to be given a course and a use. It is collected by Earth, fed by Metal and burned off by too much sun.

**甲 Jia (Yang Wood):** Your Underminer; to them you are the True Patron. The rain and the tall tree. All you give is taken up into something far larger and louder than yourself: Jia is your voice. It grows by you and seldom looks down. If you are the woman, you share one spouse star, and it is Earth.

**乙 Yi (Yin Wood):** Your Provider; to them you are the Owl Spirit. Dew on the flower. The gentlest outlet you have: you give a little, every morning, to something that opens for it. Tender, private and quietly sustaining to you both. If you are the woman, you share one spouse star, and it is Earth.

**丙 Bing (Yang Fire):** Your Earned Fortune; to them you are the Magistrate. The rain and the sun: the rainbow. Bing is far brighter than you are, and you govern it by tempering it, never by outshining it. A wife star greater than the self, borne lightly if the self has substance. If you are the man, each of you is the other's spouse star in person.

**丁 Ding (Yin Fire):** Your Windfall; to them you are the Merciless. The rain and the candle, and a clash between Stems. You get in drop by drop, and the flame hisses and holds. Neither of you intends harm and both of you do some. With Wood between you it becomes a household. If you are the man, this is your wife star in its secondary form.

**戊 Wu (Yang Earth):** Your Magistrate; to them you are Earned Fortune. The rain and the mountain, and one of the Five Combinations. Wu gathers what was scattered and sends it down as a stream with a name. The old books thought the pair cold; what it has in place of heat is shelter. The pair reaches toward Fire. If you are the woman, each of you is the other's spouse star in person.

**己 Ji (Yin Earth):** Your Merciless; to them you are Windfall. The rain and the soil that drinks it. Ji takes you in entirely and you become its moisture: useful, absorbed, and no longer free to wander. It is a close governance. If you are the woman, this is your husband star in its fiercer form.

**庚 Geng (Yang Metal):** Your True Patron; to them you are the Underminer. The dew that forms on cold iron. Geng gives you being simply by standing there, hard and cool, and you are the soft thing that comes of its hardness. Unconditional and unspoken. If you are the man, you share one spouse star, and it is Fire.

**辛 Xin (Yin Metal):** Your Owl Spirit; to them you are the Provider. The mist on the jewel. Xin feeds you finely and watches what becomes of it. You are its quiet outlet and its veil. Intimate, exacting, and a little cool. If you are the man, you share one spouse star, and it is Fire.

**壬 Ren (Yang Water):** Your Rival; to them you are the Rival. The rain and the sea. You fall into Ren and are lost in it: it is your own nature at a scale that leaves you no outline. It will carry you anywhere. Keep something of your own.

**癸 Gui (Yin Water):** Your Equal; to them you are the Equal. Rain on rain. The same quiet, the same patience, the same habit of finding the low ground. You will never need to explain yourselves and will sometimes wish one of you would say something first.

---

## Palace Meets Palace (12×12)

The *rooms* — what does the ground I live on do to the ground my partner lives on?

Compare the two Day Branches. Every relation the companion reference describes between two Branches of one chart holds between the spouse palaces of two charts, with two differences. Each Branch stays in its own chart, under its own Month Command, so whether a harmony between two palaces does more than bind must be asked of each chart separately, by the companion reference's conditions for transformation. In most pairs it binds without merging. And nothing is adjacent: what gives the contact its weight is that these two Branches are the two rooms.

A harmony holds a palace that was struck and reaches one that stood alone. A clash moves what it meets and, by the common doctrine, breaks open what was stored. A punishment repeats; a harm erodes. In a clash each Branch brings its own season with it, and the one whose element commands its own chart has the upper hand. And in every case the question of the last tables remains: what is in each room, and does the other chart want it?

---

### **Finding a pair**

Look up either Branch. The relations are named in the order of the entries that follow.

**子 Zi (Rat):** Harmony with Chou. Clash with Wu. Frame with Shen and Chen. Punishment with Mao. Harm with Wei. Season with Hai and Chou. No named relation with Yin, Si, You, Xu.

**丑 Chou (Ox):** Harmony with Zi. Clash with Wei. Frame with You and Si. Punishment with Xu and Wei. Harm with Wu. Season with Hai and Zi. No named relation with Yin, Mao, Chen, Shen.

**寅 Yin (Tiger):** Harmony with Hai. Clash with Shen. Frame with Wu and Xu. Punishment with Si and Shen. Harm with Si. Season with Mao and Chen. No named relation with Zi, Chou, Wei, You.

**卯 Mao (Rabbit):** Harmony with Xu. Clash with You. Frame with Hai and Wei. Punishment with Zi. Harm with Chen. Season with Yin and Chen. No named relation with Chou, Si, Wu, Shen.

**辰 Chen (Dragon):** Harmony with You. Clash with Xu. Frame with Zi and Shen. Punishment with itself. Harm with Mao. Season with Yin and Mao. No named relation with Chou, Si, Wu, Wei, Hai.

**巳 Si (Snake):** Harmony with Shen. Clash with Hai. Frame with You and Chou. Punishment with Yin and Shen. Harm with Yin. Season with Wu and Wei. No named relation with Zi, Mao, Chen, Xu.

**午 Wu (Horse):** Harmony with Wei. Clash with Zi. Frame with Yin and Xu. Punishment with itself. Harm with Chou. Season with Si and Wei. No named relation with Mao, Chen, Shen, You, Hai.

**未 Wei (Goat):** Harmony with Wu. Clash with Chou. Frame with Mao and Hai. Punishment with Chou and Xu. Harm with Zi. Season with Si and Wu. No named relation with Yin, Chen, Shen, You.

**申 Shen (Monkey):** Harmony with Si. Clash with Yin. Frame with Zi and Chen. Punishment with Yin and Si. Harm with Hai. Season with You and Xu. No named relation with Chou, Mao, Wu, Wei.

**酉 You (Rooster):** Harmony with Chen. Clash with Mao. Frame with Si and Chou. Punishment with itself. Harm with Xu. Season with Shen and Xu. No named relation with Zi, Yin, Wu, Wei, Hai.

**戌 Xu (Dog):** Harmony with Mao. Clash with Chen. Frame with Wu and Yin. Punishment with Chou and Wei. Harm with You. Season with Shen and You. No named relation with Zi, Si, Hai.

**亥 Hai (Pig):** Harmony with Yin. Clash with Si. Frame with Mao and Wei. Punishment with itself. Harm with Shen. Season with Zi and Chou. No named relation with Chen, Wu, You, Xu.

---

### **Two palaces in harmony**

The rooms join. Each steadies the other, and what happens to one is felt in the other. Each entry says what the pair leans toward; whether either chart is changed by it is asked of that chart, by the conditions for transformation. A room that is joined is also held, and what is held may have been wanted free: ask of each palace what it keeps. The six pairs come in three opposed couples — the Branches that clash one harmonized pair are themselves a harmonized pair — so that the years which test one of you are always paired with the years that test the other.

**子丑 Zi-Chou (Rat and Ox):** The harmony of the night, and the loyal one. The Rat's restless depth agrees to be held; the Ox's sealed vault takes in a guest. The guest keeps the vault shut, by the classical ruling this canon follows. Not a passionate household. A devoted one, slow to warm and hard to break. The classical example of a clash stilled is this pair: a Rat struck by a Horse is quieted by an Ox. Its testing years are the Horse and the Goat. They come one after the other.

**寅亥 Yin-Hai (Tiger and Pig):** The generative harmony. The Pig's hidden depth feeds the Tiger's first roar: one of you is the source and the other the surge, and together you are always beginning something. A household with forward momentum and not much rest. Its testing years are the Monkey and the Snake.

**卯戌 Mao-Xu (Rabbit and Dog):** Passion ignited by tenderness. Dawn and dusk reach across the whole cycle to find each other: the Rabbit softens the Dog's guard, the Dog keeps watch over what the Rabbit grows. Beauty and loyalty, warmer together than either was alone. Its testing years are the Rooster and the Dragon.

**辰酉 Chen-You (Dragon and Rooster):** Refinement extracted from chaos. The Dragon is vast, layered and half hidden; the Rooster is exact. One of you supplies depth and the other makes it precise: the hoard becomes the gem. A household that clarifies each of you. Its testing years are the Dog and the Rabbit.

**巳申 Si-Shen (Snake and Monkey):** Depth born from intelligence. Two quick, sharp natures discover that together they are cooler and deeper than apart. But this pair is also one leg of the punishment of ingratitude, and it keeps a quarrel inside the harmony: you sharpen each other and you cut each other, often in the same sentence. Its testing years are the Pig and the Tiger.

**午未 Wu-Wei (Horse and Goat):** Warmth settling into ground. The Horse's blaze is banked by the Goat and becomes the heat of a house: the most domestic of the harmonies, intimate and undramatic. In two charts already dry it is one more summer. Its testing years are the Rat and the Ox.

---

### **Two palaces the same**

The rooms are one room. Whatever the palace was for one of you it is for both, with nothing in the other to vary it. You know each other's habits without being told and share each other's blind side without relief. Four of the twelve Branches punish themselves when doubled, and for those the doubling is the difficulty.

**子子 Zi-Zi:** Two wells. Complete understanding in the dark, and no lamp between you. Depth meets depth and neither surfaces first.

**丑丑 Chou-Chou:** Two vaults. Enduring, frugal, slow. Each of you waits for the other to thaw. What you build lasts; what you feel is seldom said.

**寅寅 Yin-Yin:** Two springs of the year. Energy, ambition and the wish to lead, twice over. A household that starts many things and argues about direction.

**卯卯 Mao-Mao:** Two gardens. Tender, quick to bruise, and without armor on either side. Kind to each other and unprotected together.

**辰辰 Chen-Chen:** Two reservoirs, and a self-punishment. Layered, private, brooding. Each of you holds a great deal back and resents what the other withholds.

**巳巳 Si-Si:** Two fires under the skin. Watchful, strategic and intense. You read each other perfectly and seldom rest in each other's company.

**午午 Wu-Wu:** Two noons, and a self-punishment. Brilliant, generous and too hot. The household burns its fuel fast, and the quarrel is over who shines.

**未未 Wei-Wei:** Two late-summer gardens. Nurturing, stubborn and inclined to worry. Much is given in this house, and much is remembered.

**申申 Shen-Shen:** Two workshops. Clever, restless and quick to move on. You are never bored together and never quite at home.

**酉酉 You-You:** Two jewelers, and a self-punishment. Exacting taste turned on a room that holds only the two of you. Each perfects the other, and the perfecting hurts.

**戌戌 Xu-Xu:** Two watchmen. Loyal, dry and guarded. The house is safe and the gates are shut; the warmth is real and kept under ash.

**亥亥 Hai-Hai:** Two seas, and a self-punishment. Dreaming, generous and without a shore. You drift together and neither of you drops an anchor.

---

### **Two palaces in clash**

The rooms oppose. The home cannot keep still: it moves, or parts and rejoins, or has to be made peaceful by effort in each year. The classical ruling is that a clash is in the main ill and sometimes a very great blessing. The compendium adds that between two vaults it is especially to be welcomed; Shen Xiaozhan and Ren Tieqiao doubted it, and called such a clash Earth stirring Earth; this canon keeps the compendium's ruling and records the dissent. A clashed pair of palaces is steadied by a combination of the kind the entry The palace struck calls fitting, in either chart: one that turns the attacker away from its weapon, or one that joins the struck Branch to partners of its own element.

**子午 Zi-Wu (Rat against Horse):** The fundamental opposition: midnight against noon. Each of you is what the other cannot be, and the meeting makes steam. The attraction is real and so is the collision. The Rat is pure and the Horse carries only a little Earth, so almost nothing inside them softens it. Look elsewhere in the two charts for an Ox, which holds the Rat, or a Goat, which holds the Horse.

**丑未 Chou-Wei (Ox against Goat):** Two vaults of Earth collide, and it is also a leg of the punishment of bullying by strength. The quarrel is stubborn and is about what is owed. But on the common doctrine the Ox's stored Metal and the Goat's stored Wood are both released: each of you brings out what the other had sealed away. A difficult household that makes both of you more available.

**寅申 Yin-Shen (Tiger against Monkey):** The axe on the tree: spring against autumn, growth against harvest. It is decisive and fast, and it is also a leg of the punishment of ingratitude. Both are Branches of travel and beginnings. A household on the move, with sudden turns.

**卯酉 Mao-You (Rabbit against Rooster):** The flower and the scalpel. Both Branches are pure, and the cut is precise: this is the clash of taste, of small exact wounds, of beauty corrected. The old books watch this pair for constancy. What you owe each other is gentleness about detail.

**辰戌 Chen-Xu (Dragon against Dog):** Two vaults again: on the common doctrine the Dragon's hidden Water and the Dog's banked Fire both break open. For someone who needed what was stored, the companion reference says, it is an earthquake that reveals the treasure. A household of upheavals, each of which uncovers something that was needed.

**巳亥 Si-Hai (Snake against Pig):** The forge against the womb. One of you transforms and the other gestates, and each interrupts the other's work. Restless and inventive, with long journeys in it. The peace of this house depends on leaving each other's processes alone.

---

### **Two palaces in one frame**

The rooms share a direction. Two Branches of a three-harmony frame lean toward one element; with the Peak Branch among them they are a half-frame, a standing readiness, and without it they only cradle what is missing. The couple lives toward the frame's element and completes it in the years that bring the third Branch, or holds it complete already where another pillar of either chart supplies that Branch. Whether that is fortunate depends on what the element is to each chart.

**申子辰 — toward Water:** Shen with Zi is the current with no reservoir: quick intelligence between you that is not stored. Zi with Chen is the deep reservoir with no source. Shen with Chen has neither river nor readiness until a Zi arrives. A Water household thinks, plans and feels beneath the surface.

**亥卯未 — toward Wood:** Hai with Mao is creation without preservation: things bloom between you and are not kept. Mao with Wei is growth with a storehouse and no deep root. Hai with Wei waits for the Mao that makes it flower. A Wood household grows things: children, work, plans.

**寅午戌 — toward Fire:** Yin with Wu is a blaze with no storehouse. Wu with Xu is a furnace running on its reserves. Yin with Xu is fuel and embers with no flame until a Wu arrives. A Fire household is warm, visible and quick to anger.

**巳酉丑 — toward Metal:** Si with You is fine work with no vault to keep it. You with Chou is the jewel box with no jeweler. Si with Chou is forge and vault with nothing yet made. A Metal household is orderly, exact and reserved.

---

### **Two palaces in punishment**

The rooms wear on each other in a pattern that repeats. A punishment does not part a couple as a clash can; it describes the quarrel they will have more than once.

**子卯 Zi-Mao (Rat and Rabbit):** The punishment of uncivil behavior, and the only one that is a pair. Water feeds Wood past the point of welcome: the nourishment that becomes control. One of you looks after the other until it is no longer care. The remedy is a limit, said aloud.

**寅巳 Yin-Si, 巳申 Si-Shen, 寅申 Yin-Shen:** The three legs of the punishment of ingratitude.
