Each is also something else — Tiger and Snake a harm, Snake and Monkey a harmony, Tiger and Monkey a clash — and the punishment adds the same note to all three: effort that is not acknowledged, favors that turn into grievances. Keep accounts short.

**丑戌 Chou-Xu, 戌未 Xu-Wei, 丑未 Chou-Wei:** The three legs of the punishment of bullying by strength: three vaults of Earth contending over what they hold. Ox and Goat also clash. Between two palaces it is a contest of stubbornness, each sure of standing on firmer ground. Neither yields by being pushed.

**辰辰, 午午, 酉酉, 亥亥:** The self-punishments. See the same Branch, above.

---

### **Two palaces in harm**

One room quietly spoils the other's best alliance. Every harm exists because each Branch clashes with the Branch the other would harmonize with, so the damage comes sideways, through a third thing: a friend, a family, a job, a habit.

**子未 Zi-Wei (Rat and Goat):** Depth against warmth. One of you needs a disciplined bond and the other a tender one, and each finds the other's kind insufficient. The Goat disturbs what the Rat builds with the Ox; the Rat disturbs what the Goat shares with the Horse.

**丑午 Chou-Wu (Ox and Horse):** Patience against intensity. The Horse strikes the Rat that the Ox was bonding with: plans laid slowly by one of you are scorched by the other's need for visible results. Where a third Branch in either chart is a Rat, this harm is the price of the Ox holding it.

**寅巳 Yin-Si (Tiger and Snake):** Ambition against calculation, and a leg of the punishment of ingratitude as well. One of you moves boldly and the other complicates; each undoes the other's sources. The sharpest of the harms.

**卯辰 Mao-Chen (Rabbit and Dragon):** Gentleness against complexity. The Dragon's upheavals drive off the protection the Rabbit relied on, and the Rabbit's sharpness with the Rooster spoils what the Dragon was refining. This is the pair the matchmakers' jingle says weep together.

**申亥 Shen-Hai (Monkey and Pig):** Ingenuity against depth. One of you solves and the other floods the workshop: sharp answers are swamped by the weight of what is still unformed.

**酉戌 You-Xu (Rooster and Dog):** Refinement against guardianship. One of you pursues the exquisite and the other protects what is already there, and each blocks the other's access to what they need.

---

### **Two palaces of one season**

Branches that stand side by side in the calendar belong to one of the four directions, and two of them lean toward the season's element without forming anything. The rooms are neighbours: alike in climate, easy in each other's weather.

**亥 子 丑 — the north, Water:** Hai with Zi is two waters, deep and quiet. Hai with Chou is the sea and the frozen bank. Zi with Chou is also a harmony. A winter household: inward, enduring, short of warmth.

**寅 卯 辰 — the east, Wood:** Yin with Mao is two woods, the tree and the flower, growing in one direction. Yin with Chen is the forest and its wet ground. Mao with Chen is also a harm. A spring household: busy, hopeful, crowded.

**巳 午 未 — the south, Fire:** Si with Wu is two fires, the forge and the blaze. Si with Wei is heat and dry ground. Wu with Wei is also a harmony. A summer household: bright, sociable, easily overheated.

**申 酉 戌 — the west, Metal:** Shen with You is two metals, the blade and the jewel. Shen with Xu is ore and its dry mountain. You with Xu is also a harm. An autumn household: clear, exacting, a little austere.

---

### **Two palaces with no named relation**

Twenty-five pairs stand in none of the relations above. The companion reference names nothing between them, and this table does not invent a name. The rooms simply stand in the relation of their elements, as any two things do, and it is read from the principal Stem of each Branch. These are quiet contacts: they neither hold nor strike, and such a couple must look for its bond in the Day Masters, in the exchange, and in the other pillars.

**One feeds the other:** Zi with Yin, Zi with You, Chou with Shen, Mao with Si, Mao with Wu, Chen with Si, Chen with Wu, Si with Xu, Wei with Shen, Wei with You, You with Hai. One room supplies the other without ceremony. The palace whose element is generated is the more comfortable of the two.

**One governs the other:** Zi with Si, Zi with Xu, Chou with Yin, Chou with Mao, Yin with Wei, Yin with You, Mao with Shen, Chen with Hai, Wu with Shen, Wu with You, Wu with Hai, Xu with Hai. One room sets the terms for the other. It is control without collision: a steady pressure, welcome where the governed chart is strong and tiring where it is weak.

**The same element:** Chou with Chen, Chen with Wei. Two vaults of Earth that neither clash nor punish: alike in substance, different in what they keep, and unable to open each other.

---

## Pillar Meets Pillar

The *domains* — which part of my life is meeting which part of theirs?

Between two charts every pillar of the one meets every pillar of the other. The relation is read from the companion reference, by its kind: combination or clash between Stems, harmony, clash, punishment or harm between Branches. The domain is read from the rank of the two pillars. Like meets like in the four shared domains of a couple. The crossed meetings say how one person's domain falls on another's.

By weight: the Day Pillars first, because they are the two persons; then whatever touches a Month Branch, because it is the commander of its chart; the Years and Hours last for the couple, though the Years may be first for the two families.

---

### **Like with like**

**Year meets Year:** The two lineages and generations: families, backgrounds, the world each of you was born into. A harmony here is two houses that take to each other. A clash or a harm is two backgrounds that do not sit easily, whatever the two of you feel. The matchmakers of the marketplace compared this pairing and no other, and read a marriage from it. It is the outermost ring.

**Month meets Month:** The two seasons and the two working lives, and the two parental homes. A harmony here is careers and climates that feed each other. A clash is two working worlds in collision: schedules, ambitions, cities. Because each Month Branch commands its chart, this contact is felt with the season's full force.

**Day meets Day:** The couple proper. Stem with Stem is what the two of you are to each other, by the table of the Day Masters. Branch with Branch is what your two rooms do to each other, by the table of the palaces. Nothing else in a comparison outweighs this pairing.

**Hour meets Hour:** The two private worlds: children, the inner life, the late years, whatever the two of you make together and keep out of public view. A harmony here is a privacy easily shared. A clash or a harm is two people who want different things from the same room at midnight.

---

### **Crossed**

**Day meets Month:** Your self and home ground meet their work and season; and theirs meet yours. A harmony is a working life that feeds the other's peace. A clash or a harm is a working life that costs it. The two directions are separate contacts and often disagree: one career may be easy to live beside and the other hard.

**Day meets Year:** Your intimate ground meets their family. A combination between your Day Master and their Year Stem, or a harmony between your Day Branch and their Year Branch, is a bond with their people. A clash, punishment or harm is strain there.

**Day meets Hour:** Your self meets their children and their inner life. A Day Master that appears again on the other's Hour Stem stands, in person, in that private world.

**Month meets Year, Month meets Hour, Year meets Hour:** One person's work against the other's family; one person's work against the other's private world; one family against the other's children and legacy. These are the far contacts. They color the edges of a shared life and should not be read as its center.

---

### **Stems that cross**

**A Day Master combined:** Where your Day Master combines with a Stem in another pillar of your partner's chart, you are bound to the domain of that pillar: their family at the Year, their working life at the Month, their private world at the Hour. If the Stem is also one of their spouse stars, you have tied some of it up.

**A Stem in common:** The same Stem in both charts is a thing held in common that each of you reads as a different god. The same Month Stem is one of the most useful: what it is to each Day Master says how each of you regards the same work.

**A contested combination:** Where two Stems in one chart both combine with a single Stem in the other, the one is wanted by two. If it is your Day Master that is contested, you are pulled two ways. If your Day Master is one of the two, you are in a competition, with something or someone, for a partner who cannot be had whole.

**A combination crossed by a clash:** The companion reference's third condition for a combination is that nothing clash with either party. Where a Stem of your own chart clashes with the Stem your Day Master reaches for in theirs, the two reach for each other and are pulled apart: the attraction stays and the bond does not complete.

**A Day Master bound to what it does not need:** The classical name is the tethering god. A combination between Day Masters is felt as attraction whatever the charts need, and where the partner's element is your excess, the attachment is real and is to the wrong thing. Count no combination as a merit until you have asked what it binds.

---

### **Heaven and Earth Joined (天地德合)**

Two Day Pillars whose Stems combine and whose Branches stand in harmony: the selves reach for each other and so do the rooms. Each of the sixty pillars has exactly one such partner. The Song books speak of the relation between two pillars of a single chart, and prize it most where one of them is the Hour; its use between the Day Pillars of two people is the handbooks'. They give this name to the twenty-four pairs whose two pillars lie in different decades of the sixty-fold cycle, and keep a grander one, ruler and minister in glad assembly (君臣庆会), for the six pairs that fall within a single decade. This table uses the first name for all thirty.

**The pairs:** 甲子 with 己丑. 乙丑 with 庚子. 丙寅 with 辛亥. 丁卯 with 壬戌. 戊辰 with 癸酉. 己巳 with 甲申. 庚午 with 乙未. 辛未 with 丙午. 壬申 with 丁巳. 甲戌 with 己卯. 乙亥 with 庚寅. 丙子 with 辛丑. 丁丑 with 壬子. 戊寅 with 癸亥. 庚辰 with 乙酉. 辛巳 with 丙申. 壬午 with 丁未. 癸未 with 戊午. 丙戌 with 辛卯. 丁亥 with 壬寅. 戊子 with 癸丑. 壬辰 with 丁酉. 癸巳 with 戊申. 甲午 with 己未. 戊戌 with 癸卯. 己亥 with 甲寅. 甲辰 with 己酉. 乙巳 with 庚申. 庚戌 with 乙卯. 丙辰 with 辛酉.

**Within one decade:** 戊辰 with 癸酉. 甲戌 with 己卯. 丙戌 with 辛卯. 戊戌 with 癸卯. 甲辰 with 己酉. 丙辰 with 辛酉.

**The reading:** The highest mark that contact alone can give, and it is still only contact. It says the pull is complete, above and below. It does not say that what each of you is made of is what the other needs. A pair joined in this way and wrong in the exchange is the tethering god at its most persuasive.

**In time:** The same pillar arriving as a year or a decade is, for the handbooks, the fullest signal of bonding the calendar can give a person: the Day Master combined and the palace combined at once. The old books are divided about it. One line in a Song collection calls such a year the mandarin-duck combination (鸳鸯合): good things come, and where it is of use the household gains a member. The old manual and the compendium call any year that combines with the Day Stem gloom (晦气), and the old manual says so expressly of the year that combines with Stem and Branch together.

---

### **Heaven and Earth at War (天地交战)**

Two Day Pillars whose Stems clash and whose Branches clash: the turning moan, standing between two people. Day Masters of Earth have no clashing Stem, so twelve of the sixty pillars have no such partner, and the other forty-eight have one each.

**The pairs:** 甲子 with 庚午. 乙丑 with 辛未. 丙寅 with 壬申. 丁卯 with 癸酉. 甲戌 with 庚辰. 乙亥 with 辛巳. 丙子 with 壬午. 丁丑 with 癸未. 甲申 with 庚寅. 乙酉 with 辛卯. 丙戌 with 壬辰. 丁亥 with 癸巳. 甲午 with 庚子. 乙未 with 辛丑. 丙申 with 壬寅. 丁酉 with 癸卯. 甲辰 with 庚戌. 乙巳 with 辛亥. 丙午 with 壬子. 丁未 with 癸丑. 甲寅 with 庚申. 乙卯 with 辛酉. 丙辰 with 壬戌. 丁巳 with 癸亥.

**The reading:** The old note says of this meeting inside one chart that it is ill beyond doubt, and allows only that where a decade or a year combines with one side the outcome turns on which prevails. Ren Tieqiao is stricter: where heaven and earth are at war together, no combining god puts the motion to rest. Between two people it is declared will against declared will over two rooms that cannot share a wall. A Branch in either chart that harmonizes with one of the palaces helps less here than it does against a clash of Branches alone. Where the two Branches are vaults, the common doctrine has something each of you needed broken open by it.

**In time:** The same pillar arriving as a year or a decade overturns the self and its ground together. It is a year to dress for.

---

### **The Same Pillar (伏吟)**

Two people born on the same Day Pillar: the same Stem on the same Branch. Each is the other's Equal and each lives in the other's room. Everything is understood, and the translation runs the same way in both directions: every element is the same god to both of you, so neither can bring the other a god the other would read differently. That is not to say the two charts are alike. Only four of the sixteen characters are fixed, two in each chart; the other six of each and the season can differ, and a strong self and a weak one on the same Day Pillar need opposite things, the surplus of the one arriving as Companions to the other. Where the two charts are alike as well, whatever one lacks the other lacks, and whatever one has in excess is doubled. It is the easiest of all pairings to enter, and the one that most needs the other twelve characters to differ.

**In time:** Your own Day Pillar arriving as a year or a decade is the crouching moan: the palace laid upon itself, heavy and slow. When it arrives for your partner, the calendar is doing to their chart for a year what you do to it always.

---

## What One Chart Brings Another

The *exchange* — is what they are made of what I lack?

This is the comparison the classical critics pointed to and Yuan Shushan first wrote down: take the five phases of the two charts, weigh what is to be reduced and what supplied, and decide by that. It is done in three steps. Know your own chart's need. Translate your partner's chart into your own gods. Weigh what it brings on the scale. Then do all three again from their side, and expect a different answer.

What a partner brings is a climate, not a deposit. They are a standing instance of their own chart, and to live beside them is to live in its weather, as a year of their element would be if it never ended.

---

### **Translating their chart**

The relation between your two Day Masters fixes what every part of their chart is to you. Find the line for their element, and read off their five relations as yours.

**Their element is the same as yours:** Their Companions are your Companions. Their Output is your Output. Their Wealth is your Wealth. Their Authority is your Authority. Their Resource is your Resource. You read each other's charts in the same terms, and neither can bring the other a god the other would read differently. Excess and lack are still decided chart by chart: a strong self and a weak one of the same Stem need opposite things, and the surplus of the one is Companions to the other.

**Their element is the one you generate:** Their Companions are your Output. Their Output is your Wealth. Their Wealth is your Authority. Their Authority is your Resource. Their Resource is your Companions. They are where you are spent, and what they make is what you manage. If you are the woman, the two of you share a spouse star: their Wealth is your Authority.

**Their element is the one you control:** Their Companions are your Wealth. Their Output is your Authority. Their Wealth is your Resource. Their Authority is your Companions. Their Resource is your Output. If you are the man, she is your wife star in person and you are her husband star. What feeds them is what you make, and what they make is what holds you to a line.

**Their element is the one that controls you:** Their Companions are your Authority. Their Output is your Resource. Their Wealth is your Companions. Their Authority is your Output. Their Resource is your Wealth. If you are the woman, he is your husband star in person and you are his wife star. What they make shelters you, and what you make is what governs them.

**Their element is the one that generates you:** Their Companions are your Resource. Their Output is your Companions. Their Wealth is your Output. Their Authority is your Wealth. Their Resource is your Authority. They are your source, and what you make is what they manage. If you are the man, the two of you share a spouse star: their Authority is your Wealth.

---

### **The scale**

Weigh the translated chart by what it mostly is, giving most weight to their Day Master, which is the person; then to the principal Stem of their Month Branch, which commands their chart; then to whatever element they have most of.

**First:** It brings your Useful God. The element your chart needs is what they are made of. This is the best a pairing can be for you, and it is better still where the element is their Day Master itself.

**Second:** It brings your Auspicious God: the element that generates what you need, or that stands with it. Also good.

**Third:** It brings what drains your excess without supplying your need. A relief and not a remedy, and of lesser worth where the draining element is also the enemy of what you were waiting for.

**Last:** It brings your Inauspicious God: the element you already suffer from, or the one that feeds it. Whatever else joins the two of you, this is being fed every day.

**Both ways:** A pairing is first grade for both, or for one, or for neither. The second case is the common trap, and it is felt as generosity on one side and gratitude on the other long before it is felt as depletion.

**In time:** A chart supplied by its decades is to be counted as balanced for as long as they last, and so is a chart supplied by a person. Set the two sequences of decades side by side before judging what either of you needs from the other now.

---

### **What their chart mostly is to you**

**Mostly your Resource:** You are sheltered. Their world restores you: there is knowledge, patience and provision in it, and it asks little back. Welcome to a weak chart, for which it is rest. To a strong one it is a nursery, and the office of shelter becomes the management of another adult.

**Mostly your Companions:** You are accompanied. Their world is made of your own kind: friends, equals, people who do what you do. Welcome to a weak chart, for which it is an army. To a strong one it is a crowd at the table, and for a man with thin Wealth or a woman with a contested Officer it brings rivals.

**Mostly your Output:** You are drawn out. Their world gives you things to make, say and do, and an audience for them. Welcome to a strong chart that needed somewhere to go. To a weak one it is a daily expense. For a woman who keeps an Officer in her chart, it is more of what strikes it.

**Mostly your Wealth:** You are given charge. Their world is something to manage, provide for and take responsibility for. Welcome to a strong chart, for which it is scope. To a weak one it is the rich house and the poor man in it. For a chart that leans on its Resource, it is the breaker of Resource.

**Mostly your Authority:** You are held to a line. Their world has rules in it, and expectations, and a public face you must keep up. Welcome to a strong and scattered chart, for which it is form. To a weak one it is pressure without pause.

---

### **What is absent**

**An element you lack and they have:** The plainest case of supply. Where it is also your Useful God, nothing in a comparison counts for more. Where it is your Inauspicious God, they have brought into the house the one thing you were spared.

**An element both lack:** The pair has a common want that neither can answer. Look for the decades that bring it, and expect the two of you to seek it outside the marriage, in work, in friends or in a place.

**An element both have to excess:** The pair doubles it. Two charts that share an excess understand each other at once and make each other worse.

**The five phases between you:** Many charts lack one phase. Where each of two people has the phase the other lacks, the sixteen characters are complete though neither eight is, and the old preference for a chart with all five is satisfied by the couple.

---

### **Climate**

**The rule:** The seasonal handbook weighs a chart by its climate before it weighs anything else. A chart born in deep winter is cold and wet whatever its balance of strength, and wants Fire first. A chart born at midsummer is hot and dry, and wants Water first.

**Cold with cold:** Two winter charts, or two charts of Metal and Water with no Fire between them. Steady, inward and slow to warm. The pair must find its warmth in the years, in a place, or in its work.

**Hot with hot:** Two summer charts, or two charts of Fire and dry Earth with no Water between them. Bright, quick and easily parched. The pair must find its coolness outside itself in the same way.

**Cold with hot:** Each tempers the other. This is the plainest good a comparison of climates can find, and it holds even where neither is the other's Useful God.

**The old list:** Ren Tieqiao's list of the charts he calls childless is largely a list of climates: Fire blazing over parched Earth, Earth and Metal sodden, Water in flood, Metal cold in cold Water. Read it as a description of ground and never as a verdict on a body.

---

### **The rule of the heavy Companions and the heavy Output**

Zhang Nan's one explicit rule: a man heavy with Companions is to be matched with a woman heavy with Output, and she with him. It is a rule for charts that are not balanced. It holds or fails according to how the two elements stand, because his Companions and her Output are different things in each case.

**His element generates hers:** His Companions are her Resource, and her Output is his Wealth. Each excess is the other's remedy: her overflow is the wife star he was robbed of, and his crowd is the Resource that governs her Hurting Officer. Here the rule is exact.

**His element controls hers:** His Companions are her Authority, and her Output is his Authority. Each excess governs the other: she meets a husband star too abundant to wound, and he meets the restraint his Companions lacked. Here too the rule holds.

**Her element generates his:** His Companions and her Output are one element. The rule's two conditions are a single shared abundance, good if both charts wanted it and doubled trouble if neither did.

**The two share an element:** Her Output is his Output and bridges his Companions to his Wealth, which helps him. His Companions are hers, and feed an Output already heavy, which helps her only if she is strong and needed the outlet.

**Her element controls his:** Her Output is his Resource and feeds the Companions he has too many of. The rule fails him, though his Companions, being her Wealth, give her Output somewhere to go.

---

## The Five Combinations as Marriages

The *fable* — how the Stems themselves marry.

The compendium tells the five combinations as five weddings. Each element fears the one that controls it; so the elder brother of each, the yang Stem, gives his younger sister, the yin Stem, in marriage to the yang Stem of the clan he fears. The husband's element always controls the wife's; the two are always of opposite polarity; and the wife is the husband's Earned Fortune as he is her Magistrate. The tradition's two definitions of the spouse star are this one relation seen from its two ends.

Between two people a combination attracts. Whether it does more is asked of each chart separately, by the companion reference's conditions for transformation, and in most pairs it does not. What the pair reaches toward is still worth knowing, because it is the element their meeting favors.

---

### **甲己 — Jia and Ji: the centered and upright**

**The wedding:** Earth fears Wood, so Yang Earth gives his sister Yin Earth to Yang Wood. Jia is the husband and Ji the wife.

**The portrait:** Yang Wood, humane and first among the Stems, with Yin Earth, settled and honest, which has the power to bring things to life. The compendium names the pair the combination of the centered and upright, and says that one who carries it is dignified, generous and straight. It adds that a woman need not fear this combination.

**When the yang Stem is the man's:** The tree and the field. He is her husband star and she his wife star; the pull and the stars say one thing. He gives her a purpose and she gives him ground.

**When the yang Stem is the woman's:** She is Jia and he is Ji. The pull is the same and the stars are reversed: she is his Magistrate and he is her Earned Fortune. She is the upright one and he the ground she stands in.

**What it reaches toward:** Earth. The meeting favors steadiness, and it is kindest to charts that wanted Earth.

---

### **乙庚 — Yi and Geng: humaneness and duty**

**The wedding:** Wood fears Metal, so Yang Wood gives his sister Yin Wood to Yang Metal. Geng is the husband and Yi the wife.

**The portrait:** Yin Wood is humane and too yielding; Yang Metal is hard and does not bend; together the hard and the soft supply each other. The compendium names the pair the combination of humaneness and duty, and says that one who carries it is resolute, principled and not to be flattered. This too a woman need not fear.

**When the yang Stem is the man's:** The sword and the vine. He is her husband star and she his wife star. Her softness is the one thing he does not cut.

**When the yang Stem is the woman's:** She is Geng and he is Yi. She is his Magistrate, and he is the one soft thing in a hard life: her Earned Fortune.

**What it reaches toward:** Metal. The meeting favors firmness: the yielding partner takes on the other's edge.

---

### **丙辛 — Bing and Xin: awe and command**

**The wedding:** Metal fears Fire, so Yang Metal gives his sister Yin Metal to Yang Fire. Bing is the husband and Xin the wife.

**The portrait:** Yang Fire blazing in its own splendor, and a Yin Metal that the compendium calls sharp and fond of severity. It names the pair the combination of awe and command, and says that one who carries it has a stern bearing that others fear. Its remarks on women who carry it are among its least kind, and belong with the lore.

**When the yang Stem is the man's:** The sun and the jewel. He is her husband star and she his wife star. In his light she is seen for what she is.

**When the yang Stem is the woman's:** She is Bing and he is Xin. She is his Magistrate and the source of his visibility; he is the precious thing she shines on.

**What it reaches toward:** Water. Neither leans toward what the other is. The meeting leans toward depth and coolness and away from display: the companion reference calls it the most surprising alchemy of the five.

---

### **丁壬 — Ding and Ren: the hidden**

**The wedding:** Fire fears Water, so Yang Fire gives his sister Yin Fire to Yang Water. Ren is the husband and Ding the wife.

**The portrait:** A Water that no light reaches and a Fire that hides its own flame. The compendium's name for the pair can only be translated as it stands, the combination of concealed wantonness, and it says that one who carries it is bright-eyed, quick to feeling and easily moved. Take the name as a measure of how strongly the pull is felt.

**When the yang Stem is the man's:** The ocean and the lamp. He is her husband star and she his wife star. He could put her out, and takes his bearing from her.

**When the yang Stem is the woman's:** She is Ren and he is Ding. She is his Magistrate, vast where he is small and aimed; he is her Earned Fortune, the one fixed light on her water.

**What it reaches toward:** Wood. What should have been an extinguishing becomes a growing: the pair is at its best when it is making something.

---

### **戊癸 — Wu and Gui: the pair without feeling**

**The wedding:** Water fears Earth, so Yang Water gives his sister Yin Water to Yang Earth. Wu is the husband and Gui the wife.

**The portrait:** The compendium calls Yang Earth an old and ugly husband and Yin Water a graceful young wife: an old yang and a young yin, joined and indifferent. It names the pair the combination without feeling. Read without the gossip, it is the stillest of the five: a great mass and a thing that moves, each completing what the other lacks.

**When the yang Stem is the man's:** The mountain and the mist. He is her husband star and she his wife star. She comes to him of her own accord and he gives her a course.

**When the yang Stem is the woman's:** She is Wu and he is Gui. She is his Magistrate and his shelter; he is the living water on her slopes.

**What it reaches toward:** Fire. The coolest of the pairs reaches for warmth, and is happiest in charts and years that supply some.

---

## The Stars of the Lore

The *omens* — what did the matchmakers look for, and what is it worth?

A crowd of named stars surrounds the subject of marriage. Most are fixed by the Branch of the birth year and one other Branch. The classical critics dismissed them as a class: Zhang Nan called the worst of them doctrines with the head lopped off and the feet severed; Chen Su'an asked how every girl born in one month of one year could be a widow; the verse of Di Tian Sui (滴天髓) says of the Peach Blossom, believe the half, and its old note says that even where such stars seem to prove true there is in the end not much reason in them. They are set out here because a reader will meet them everywhere, and because each of them, looked at closely, is pointing at something the method reads properly by other means.

---

### **Peach Blossom (桃花, 咸池)**

**The rule:** Counted from the Branch of the Year or of the Day. For Shen, Zi or Chen, the Peach Blossom is You, the Rooster. For Hai, Mao or Wei, it is Zi, the Rat. For Yin, Wu or Xu, it is Mao, the Rabbit. For Si, You or Chou, it is Wu, the Horse. In each case it is the Branch where the element of that three-harmony frame has its Bathing.

**The lore:** Charm, desire and scandal. The manuals fear it in a woman's chart and say of a man that with it at the Emperor's seat he perishes through passion. And they contradict themselves freely: the Peach Blossom joined to the Day palace is wealth through the wife, and an Officer star that carries it is the mark of a good woman of the inner courtyard, and it stands in the old list of noble patterns.

**The critics:** The miscellany at the end of Ming Li Yue Yan (命理约言) gives the reasoning and rejects it: the star is nothing but the Bathing stage, the newborn washed and naked, and the inference from nakedness to vice is absurd.

**The use:** It is the Bathing stage by another name, and the companion reference reads that stage properly: exposure, attraction, the beauty of being seen without armor. Where one person's Peach Blossom Branch is the other's Day Branch, the lore says the pull is strong. Believe the half.

---

### **Red Phoenix and Heavenly Joy (红鸾, 天喜)**

**The rule:** Counted from the Year Branch. For a Zi year the Red Phoenix is Mao, and it runs backward from there: Chou year, Yin. Yin year, Chou. Mao year, Zi. Chen year, Hai. Si year, Xu. Wu year, You. Wei year, Shen. Shen year, Wei. You year, Wu. Xu year, Si. Hai year, Chen. Heavenly Joy is always the Branch that clashes the Red Phoenix: for a Zi year You, for Chou Shen, for Yin Wei, for Mao Wu, for Chen Si, for Si Chen, for Wu Mao, for Wei Yin, for Shen Chou, for You Zi, for Xu Hai, for Hai Xu.

**The lore:** The stars of weddings and of births. A year whose Branch is one's Red Phoenix or Heavenly Joy is a year of glad occasions; a partner whose Day Branch is one of them is fated company.

**The critics:** No classical critic troubles to refute them, and no classical method uses them. When the old rhapsody speaks of the glad omen of the Red Phoenix it means something else: the year and the decade together completing the three-harmony frame of the spouse star.

**The use:** A year the lore marks is worth a second look by the method. If the same year also brings the star, completes its frame, or moves the palace, the lore and the method agree. If it does not, the method is to be believed.

---

### **Lonely Star and Widow's Lodge (孤辰, 寡宿)**

**The rule:** Counted from the Year Branch by its season. For Hai, Zi or Chou years, the Lonely Star is Yin and the Widow's Lodge is Xu. For Yin, Mao or Chen years, they are Si and Chou. For Si, Wu or Wei years, Shen and Chen. For Shen, You or Xu years, Hai and Wei. The Lonely Star is the Branch just after the season; the Widow's Lodge is the Branch just before it. The marketplace applied the rule to the month of birth; the looser reckoning applies it to any pillar.

**The lore:** A man fears the Lonely Star and a woman the Widow's Lodge: solitude, a spouse lost, a house without company.

**The critics:** The imperial compilers traced the pair to a method of the diviners of war that concerns directions and has nothing to do with persons, and called its use on marriages baseless. Chen Su'an had examined the charts of women who grew old with their husbands in honor and found the Widow's Lodge in a great many of them.

**The use:** None as a verdict. The two Branches stand at the edges of one's birth season, and a chart that has them is no more solitary than its palace and its star make it.

---

### **The lesser stars**

**The Heavenly Noble (天乙贵人):** Counted from the Day Stem, and by some from the Year Stem. For 甲, 戊 and 庚 the Noble Branches are Chou and Wei. For 乙 and 己, Zi and Shen. For 丙 and 丁, Hai and You. For 壬 and 癸, Mao and Si. For 辛, Wu and Yin. The handbooks say that a partner whose Day Branch is your Noble is your benefactor.

**The Emolument (禄):** The Branch of the Day Master's Office. Yin for 甲, Mao for 乙, Si for 丙 and 戊, Wu for 丁 and 己, Shen for 庚, You for 辛, Hai for 壬, Zi for 癸. The handbooks prize a couple each of whose Day Branches is the other's Emolument. By the method it is a partner's palace that is root for your Day Master, and it is read as Companions are read.

**The Traveling Horse (驿马):** Counted from the Year or the Day Branch. Shen for Yin, Wu or Xu. Yin for Shen, Zi or Chen. Hai for Si, You or Chou. Si for Hai, Mao or Wei. Movement and distance. A Song collection has a line on the Horse beneath the Day, that the wife is often ailing or idle; the handbooks say that the spouse comes from far away.

**The Canopy (华盖):** Counted from the Year or the Day Branch, and always the vault of its frame. Chen for Shen, Zi or Chen. Xu for Yin, Wu or Xu. Chou for Si, You or Chou. Wei for Hai, Mao or Wei. Solitude, art and religion. The same collection has an unkind line about the wife of a man seated on his Canopy, and the compendium has another.

**Red Allure (红艳):** Counted from the Day Stem. Wu for 甲, Yin for 丙, Wei for 丁, Chen for 己, Xu for 庚, You for 辛, Shen for 癸. For 乙, 戊 and 壬 the versions differ. The compendium lists it beside the Peach Blossom and says a woman's chart fears it most. No reasoning is offered for it anywhere.

**The two Virtues (天德, 月德):** Counted from the Month Branch, by the rhyme the Song collection preserves. The Heavenly Virtue is 丁 for a Yin month, Shen for Mao, 壬 for Chen, 辛 for Si, Hai for Wu, 甲 for Wei, 癸 for Shen, Yin for You, 丙 for Xu, 乙 for Hai, Si for Zi, and 庚 for Chou. The Moon Virtue is 丙 for the months Yin, Wu and Xu, 壬 for Shen, Zi and Chen, 庚 for Si, You and Chou, and 甲 for Hai, Mao and Wei. The lore has them relieve what is ill in a chart, and the compendium names them, with a pardon, among the things that spare a chart the Blade's calamity. They are the one pair of lesser stars this book leans on, in the entry on the Blade year.

**The use:** None of these is a method. Each names a Stem or a Branch, and it is read by the tables above: what it holds, what that is to the Day Master, and what it does to the palace.

---

### **The wedding day**

**The art:** Choosing the day of a wedding is the business of the almanac and not of the Four Pillars. It is a different art, the selection of days (择日), with its own books.

**The critics:** The imperial compilers who struck the matchmakers' tests from the calendar kept the almanac's diagram of wedding days in an appendix, because it stood in the official calendar. They said of it that no fortune or misfortune could arise from such a thing, and that no one need be bound by it.

**The use:** This canon does not treat the choice of days. Nothing in it says that a marriage is made better or worse by the date of the ceremony.

---

### **The tests of the marketplace**

**The Nine Palaces:** The two birth years turned into two trigrams and paired as upper, middle and lower matches. The imperial compilers showed that the rule was lifted from the siting of graves, where it prizes what marriage should avoid, and that its attribution to Lu Cai (吕才) is a forgery fathered on a man who spent his life refuting such things.

**The six harms of the zodiac:** Horse with Ox, Goat with Rat, Snake with Tiger, Dragon with Rabbit, Rooster with Dog, Pig with Monkey. These are the Six Harms, applied to the two Year Branches and nothing else. The harm is real and it is read where it falls: between the two families, at the outermost ring.

**Draining and increasing:** The sound-element of the birth year treated as the person, and the element it controls or is controlled by run through the life stages against the birth month. The imperial compilers noted that the true method does take the wife from what a man controls, but reckons from the Day Stem, and that the diviners had stretched the rule onto the year. Zhang Nan's answer stands: whether wealth comes or goes hangs on one's own fate and luck, and no one's son or daughter is the cause of another's fortune.

**The Eight Defeats and their kin:** Broken Marrow, Iron Broom, the Great Defeat, the Scattering: each fixed by the year Branch and one month. Zhang Nan had met old women, rich and honored, whose parents had falsified their birth years to hide such a mark; when he cast the true charts he found husband star and child star in good order. The star had caused nothing but the lie.

**The Month of Great Profit:** The month in which alone a wedding might be held, counted from the bride's birth year. Struck from the calendar by imperial order together with the rest.

---

## The Years

The *timing* — what does this year do to my marriage, and to ours?

A decade is the country one is passing through; a year is the person one meets there. Time adds nothing of its own: it brings Stems and Branches, and what they do depends on what they find in the chart. For a signal to mean an event, three things should agree: the decade makes it possible, the year sounds it, and the life is at an age and in a state where it can be a wedding or a parting. For a couple a fourth is added: the year should speak in both charts.

---

### **The star arrives**

**For a man:** A year or decade whose Stem is his Wealth brings the relation into the open, and most of all where the natal star was hidden, thin or absent. The old rule for early marriage is a luck that meets the country of Wealth. Where the decade and the year together complete the three-harmony frame of his Wealth element, the rhapsody calls it the glad omen itself.

**For a woman:** A year or decade whose Stem is her Officer or Killings does the same. Marriage comes hard while her luck runs through the place that turns its back on the husband: where the star is at its Death or Extinction, or where the Hurting Officer arrives with nothing to convert it.

**By function:** Where the spouse is taken from another god, it is the arrival of that god which counts.

**The caution:** A star that arrives where there is already too much of it is not a wedding. It is weight. And a second form of the star arriving where one form already stands divides the bond for as long as it stays.

---

### **The Day Master is combined**

The year that brings a Day Master its combining partner draws the self into a bond. For a yang-Stem man and a yin-Stem woman the partner is the spouse star proper, and this signal and the last are one. For the others it is a year of attachment all the same. If the partner's element is the chart's excess, the year is the tethering god: an attachment that is real and that is to the wrong thing.

**The old books:** This signal is later practice, and the old books do not support it. All three that speak to the point call a year whose Stem combines with the Stem of the birth day or the birth hour gloom (晦气): a subject presuming on the sovereign of the year, with a clouded year to follow. The compendium counts it heavier where the birth Stem is the yang one of the pair. Only one obscure line, in a Song collection, makes an exception where the Branches harmonize, and calls that the mandarin-duck combination (鸳鸯合).

---

### **The palace is combined**

A Branch in six-harmony with the Day Branch, or one that completes a three-harmony frame through it, engages the room. It settles a palace that was clashed, reaches one that stood alone, and fills one that lay in the Void. For a vault, remember that a combination holds it shut, by the classical ruling this canon follows, and that only a clash opens it.

**The old books:** The handbooks count this the gentlest signal of marriage. The one Song rule on the point says the opposite: that it is rare to take a wife in a year standing in three-harmony or six-harmony with the day. Keep the signal as a color.

---

### **The palace is clashed**

A Branch that clashes the Day Branch moves the room. The one old rule on the year of a wedding belongs here: the Branch that clashes the wife's seat, a Song manual says, marks the year of taking a wife. For one living alone on a quiet seat it is the year the household changes; for a seat that is a vault it is, by the common doctrine, the year the door comes open; for a marriage already made it is a test. Weigh it by the season: a clash from a Branch the decade favors strikes hard, and a clash from a Branch out of season only jostles. And look for a fitting combination, of the two kinds the entry The palace struck names, in the chart, in the decade, or in the partner's palace.

---

### **The crouching moan (伏吟)**

The year or decade repeats the Day Branch, or the whole Day Pillar. The palace is laid upon itself: whatever it was, it is twice. The tradition reads heaviness, stalling and grief, and its proverb pairs this moan with the next. For a vault it is the vault doubled. Read it as a time when the marriage ground is least able to move, and do not force it.

---

### **The turning moan (反吟)**

The year or decade clashes the Day Pillar in Stem and Branch together. The self and its ground are overturned at once. It is the gravest of the annual signals for the body and for the marriage, and it is still weather: it asks for caution in exactly the domains the natal chart already marks. A Branch in harmony with the struck palace, which quiets a clash of Branches alone, is not to be relied on here: Ren Tieqiao holds that where heaven and earth are at war together no combining god puts the motion to rest.

---

### **The star is attacked**

**For a man:** Years of Companions and Rob Wealth fall on his Wealth. Where the natal Wealth is thin and nothing bridges or guards it, these are years of loss in money and in marriage together. Where the natal Wealth is too heavy for a weak self, the same years are help.

**For a woman:** Years of the Hurting Officer fall on her Officer, where she has one and has no Wealth to convert the blow. Years of heavy Resource drain it. Where her husband is taken by function, the attack comes from whatever controls that god.

---

### **The star enters its Tomb**

The decade or year carries the spouse star's element to the Branch of its Tomb or its Extinction. The old lines call it the husband star gone to the Yellow Springs and the paired birds flying apart. Read it as a season in which the star is out of reach. The two stages differ in what ends it. At the Tomb the star is stored in a vault, and the Branch that clashes the vault opens it, by the classical doctrine. The Extinction falls on no vault, and nothing is stored there: the season ends when the calendar moves on, and what shortens it is whatever feeds the star.

---

### **Year and decade together (岁运并临)**

**The old sense:** A decade and a year that bring the same thing at once, and above all the Blade. Rob Wealth and the Blade are most to be feared at the Hour, the old line runs, and when year and luck arrive upon them together, calamity is at the door. The same books add that for a Day Master without strength a Blade is no evil.

**The narrower sense:** The year's pillar is the very pillar of the decade. The compendium knows this sense too, and judges it in a line: with the Blade and the Seven Killings it is ill, and with Wealth, Officer and Resource it is good as well.

**The judgment:** In either sense whatever arrives arrives twice over. The oldest judgment is conditional: if it injures the Useful God there is harm.

---

### **The Blade and the lord of the year**

**The saying:** When the Blade clashes or combines with the lord of the year, calamity comes of a sudden.

**The years:** For a chart that holds a Blade, the year whose Branch clashes it, and the years whose Branches stand in three-harmony or six-harmony with it.

**The qualification:** The compendium gives it in the same breath. A chart of shallow resources will find the saying true. A chart that is robust, or that has the stars of virtue to relieve it, comes to no great harm. Where the decade and the year both touch the Blade the trouble is whole, and where only one of them does it is halved. The same chapter records an exception for two Day Masters: Yang Fire and Yang Water that meet the Blade in the clash of Rat and Horse mostly come to no harm, because the Rat carries Yang Fire's Direct Officer and the Horse carries Yang Water's, and the clash is reckoned a noble influence instead.

**The reading:** The saying is written of the Blade as a menace. For a weak Day Master whose Blade is its root, weigh each such year by what it does to the root: a frame that turns the Blade toward its own element, a harmony that holds it, a clash that strikes it.

---

### **More than once**

**The signs:** The old books read these as more than one marriage. For a man: Blades and Companions in number, the wife star declared in both its forms, the palace clashed. For a woman: Officer and Killings mingled, the Hurting Officer in layers, the husband star combined away or absent, the palace clashed. None of them is a count. Each describes a marriage ground that does not keep its first arrangement easily, and in a world with divorce each is far more often a parting and a second beginning than a death.

**The room:** The palace is the same for every marriage. Whoever you marry, first or third, comes to live in the same Day Branch.

**The guest and the time:** A second partner is a second chart, and the whole comparison is made again from the beginning. A second marriage is made in another decade, with the star in another condition.

**Two devices not used here:** Taking the direct star for a first spouse and the indirect star for a later one, and moving the palace of a second marriage to another Branch. Neither is in the texts this canon follows.

**The limit:** No chart forecasts the death of a spouse, and no reading entitles anyone to tell a person that they are a danger to whoever they marry.

---

### **Children**

**Where they are read:** At the Hour Pillar, which every school takes for their palace, and from the Output, which is what a person generates. Where the chart is read by function, Ren Tieqiao's rule of thumb is that for a strong self Wealth serves as the child, and for a weak self Resource.

**When they come:** In decades and years that bring the child star or what feeds it, that complete its frame, or that join or move the Hour Pillar.

**When it is hard:** Where the child star reaches the Hour at its Death, Tomb or Extinction; where heaped Resource, and the Owl above all, sits on the Output; and in the four climates of the old list: scorched, sodden, flooded and frozen.

**Between two charts:** Compare the two Hour Pillars, and ask of each chart whether the other brings the season it lacks.

**The limit:** No chart counts children, and none decides whether there will be any.

---

### **Reading a year for two**

**The six questions:** What is the year's Stem to each Day Master? What does its Branch do to each palace? Does it bring, attack or bury each one's star? What decade is each of you in, and does the year agree with it? Does the year speak in both charts, and does it say the same thing in both? And what do the contacts between your two charts do with it?

**Three kinds of year:** Years that speak in both charts, years that speak in one, and years that speak in neither. A wedding is an event in two charts. A year in which one palace is struck and the other is quiet is a year one of you has and the other watches.

**Paired years:** Where your two palaces stand in harmony, the Branches that clash them stand in harmony too. The year that strikes your room and the year that strikes your partner's come as a pair, and so do the year that combines with yours while it repeats theirs and the year that does the reverse. Write them down in advance.

**Two clocks:** The years are shared and the decades are not. One of you may be supplied while the other is drained; one Stem standing over both decades is a different god to each. The commonest hard thing in a long marriage is not a bad match but ten years spent in different weather.

---

### **The years of each Day Master**

By the Stem of the year or decade. The wife star is for a man and the husband star for a woman; the combining and clashing Stems are for either.

**甲 Jia:** Wife star 己 Ji, secondary 戊 Wu. Husband star 辛 Xin, secondary 庚 Geng. Combined by 己 Ji. Clashed by 庚 Geng.

**乙 Yi:** Wife star 戊 Wu, secondary 己 Ji. Husband star 庚 Geng, secondary 辛 Xin. Combined by 庚 Geng. Clashed by 辛 Xin.

**丙 Bing:** Wife star 辛 Xin, secondary 庚 Geng. Husband star 癸 Gui, secondary 壬 Ren. Combined by 辛 Xin. Clashed by 壬 Ren.

**丁 Ding:** Wife star 庚 Geng, secondary 辛 Xin. Husband star 壬 Ren, secondary 癸 Gui. Combined by 壬 Ren. Clashed by 癸 Gui.

**戊 Wu:** Wife star 癸 Gui, secondary 壬 Ren. Husband star 乙 Yi, secondary 甲 Jia. Combined by 癸 Gui. Clashed by no Stem.

**己 Ji:** Wife star 壬 Ren, secondary 癸 Gui. Husband star 甲 Jia, secondary 乙 Yi. Combined by 甲 Jia. Clashed by no Stem.

**庚 Geng:** Wife star 乙 Yi, secondary 甲 Jia. Husband star 丁 Ding, secondary 丙 Bing. Combined by 乙 Yi. Clashed by 甲 Jia.

**辛 Xin:** Wife star 甲 Jia, secondary 乙 Yi. Husband star 丙 Bing, secondary 丁 Ding. Combined by 丙 Bing. Clashed by 乙 Yi.

**壬 Ren:** Wife star 丁 Ding, secondary 丙 Bing. Husband star 己 Ji, secondary 戊 Wu. Combined by 丁 Ding. Clashed by 丙 Bing.

**癸 Gui:** Wife star 丙 Bing, secondary 丁 Ding. Husband star 戊 Wu, secondary 己 Ji. Combined by 戊 Wu. Clashed by 丁 Ding.

---

### **The years of each palace**

By the Branch of the year or decade, against the Day Branch.

**子 Zi (Rat):** Combined by Chou. Clashed by Wu. Repeated by Zi. Framed by Shen and Chen. Punished by Mao. Harmed by Wei.

**丑 Chou (Ox):** Combined by Zi. Clashed by Wei. Repeated by Chou. Framed by Si and You. Punished by Xu and Wei. Harmed by Wu.

**寅 Yin (Tiger):** Combined by Hai. Clashed by Shen. Repeated by Yin. Framed by Wu and Xu. Punished by Si and Shen. Harmed by Si.

**卯 Mao (Rabbit):** Combined by Xu. Clashed by You. Repeated by Mao. Framed by Hai and Wei. Punished by Zi. Harmed by Chen.

**辰 Chen (Dragon):** Combined by You. Clashed by Xu. Repeated by Chen, which is also its self-punishment. Framed by Shen and Zi. Harmed by Mao.

**巳 Si (Snake):** Combined by Shen. Clashed by Hai. Repeated by Si. Framed by You and Chou. Punished by Yin and Shen. Harmed by Yin.

**午 Wu (Horse):** Combined by Wei. Clashed by Zi. Repeated by Wu, which is also its self-punishment. Framed by Yin and Xu. Harmed by Chou.

**未 Wei (Goat):** Combined by Wu. Clashed by Chou. Repeated by Wei. Framed by Hai and Mao. Punished by Chou and Xu. Harmed by Zi.

**申 Shen (Monkey):** Combined by Si. Clashed by Yin. Repeated by Shen. Framed by Zi and Chen. Punished by Yin and Si. Harmed by Hai.

**酉 You (Rooster):** Combined by Chen. Clashed by Mao. Repeated by You, which is also its self-punishment. Framed by Si and Chou. Harmed by Xu.

**戌 Xu (Dog):** Combined by Mao. Clashed by Chen. Repeated by Xu. Framed by Yin and Wu. Punished by Chou and Wei. Harmed by You.

**亥 Hai (Pig):** Combined by Yin. Clashed by Si. Repeated by Hai, which is also its self-punishment. Framed by Mao and Wei. Harmed by Shen.
